- **Decision:** use one causal detector, `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim`, everywhere a direct-causal-claim check runs, regardless of market state. `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim` (the raw, non-stripping detector) remains as the shared primitive used internally by `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim` and by `hasPriorCompletedSessionCausalClaim`, but no call site chooses between the two by state any more.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: the completed-session `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` check (around line 842) now calls `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim` instead of `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim`. The now-unused `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim` import was removed from this file (it is still exported from and used within `claude-analysis-contract.js`).
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`: the `sections[1]: market causality requires a principal catalyst` check (around line 1216) dropped its `activeUs ? hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim(...) : hasDirectMarketCausalClaim(...)` ternary in favor of calling `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim` unconditionally.
  - No other causal-claim call site changed. `hasPriorCompletedSessionCausalClaim` (contract.js ~line 954) still calls the raw `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim` on its own matched sentence; it was out of scope for this step and is unaffected either way, since the day/date phrase it looks for is the identifying target, not the thing being stripped.
- **Must not change (kept):** protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; post-close evidence is never cited as the cause of the completed-session move; Section 3/4/6 grounding; Section 7 empty-when-unsupported; the frozen 8-section structure; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC completed-session-only path (untouched).
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 818/818 passing (2 new tests added across Step 8U.1 and 8U.4, see below), 46/46 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid Anthropic calls; both new tests replay fixtures with a mocked writer response.
  - New: `tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js` — "Step 8U.1: a completed driver claim naming only a day or date survives, while a genuine uncatalyzed cause still localizes". A completed Section 2 reading "Stocks rose on Friday." with no catalyst reference now survives (`validateClaudeAnalysisOutput` no longer flags it, and the full invocation pipeline returns the content unchanged with no `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` diagnostic). The same section reading "Stocks rose on the jobs report." with no catalyst reference still localizes to null with the existing `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` diagnostic and `DEGRADED` status, unchanged from before this step.
  - Existing: the pre-existing completed-session `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` fixture ("localizes uncited driver causality and broad-market evidence leaking into Section 4 independently", `tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js`) still blanks, because its causal sentence ("...drove stocks higher in the completed session.") does not match the day/date-stripping pattern and remains a genuine causal claim.
- **Rejected options:** none considered beyond the plan's proposed approach; this is the lowest-risk step in the Step 8U review (narrows one detector, adds no request bytes).
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; no live validation run needed on its own (validator-only change exercised with fixtures), but covered by the already-pending D-001 PRE/POST/CLOSED live runs since those exercise the same causal-claim path.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8U.4 — Duplicate telemetry-ref and uncertainty dedupe in every market state (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U section-survival review found that `normalizeActiveTransportMetadata` (`lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js:728`), which trims a section's duplicate/blank-after-trim `telemetryRefs` and `uncertainties` strings (and the top-level `evidenceGaps` array) to a canonical deduplicated form, only ran when the US market was in an active session state with current-session evidence. A completed-session (CLOSED/WEEKEND/HOLIDAY) writer reply with the same kind of duplicate or untrimmed strings skipped this repair entirely and reached the stricter downstream checks (`canonicalStringArray` in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`, and the equivalent final-validator check) unrepaired, which would fail the whole report as `CONTRACT_FAILURE` instead of being silently cleaned up.
- **Decision:** run the same dedupe unconditionally for any US-scope report, not only active ones.
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, `normalizeActiveTransportMetadata`: removed the active-session-state and current-session-evidence conditions from the early-return guard, leaving only the existing `selectedScope !== 'US'` and malformed-`output.sections` guards. The function's internal behavior (deduplicating `telemetryRefs` and `uncertainties` per section plus top-level `evidenceGaps`, and defaulting `status` from surviving content when the writer's own `status` value is invalid) is unchanged; it simply now executes for every US market state. The function name and its two still-active-only callers elsewhere in the file (`US_ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES`, `currentSessionEvidenceContext`) were left as-is — this step only changes this one guard.
- **Must not change (kept):** same list as Step 8U.1 above; this step adds no new normalization behavior, only widens when the existing one runs.
- **Evidence/tests:** covered by the same full-suite run as Step 8U.1 above (818/818, 46/46 syntax, `git diff --check` clean).
  - New: `tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js` — "Step 8U.4: completed-session duplicate telemetry refs, uncertainties and gaps are canonicalized, not only in active sessions". A completed-session fixture with a duplicated `telemetryRefs` entry, duplicate/blank/untrimmed `uncertainties` strings, duplicate/blank/untrimmed top-level `evidenceGaps` strings, and an invalid `status` value now returns `SUCCESS` with everything canonicalized (status correctly defaulted to `DEGRADED` from the surviving gap), mirroring the existing active-session test of the same shape ("active transport metadata is canonicalized without changing grounded analysis", `tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`).
- **Rejected options:** none; the plan rated this the lowest-risk item in the Step 8U review.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; no live validation run needed on its own.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8U.2 — Per-section survival in CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that the section-by-section normaliser (`normalizeUsIndependentSections`) only ran in PRE, REGULAR and POST. A rule break that empties one section in an active report (an internal-identifier leak, malformed prose, a section with no evidence, a Section 3 that names no focus subject, the report going over 2,500 words, the wrong empty-list text for Section 4) failed the whole report as `CONTRACT_FAILURE` in CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY, then used up the Step 8L retry. Step 8M recorded this gap and deferred it as "Step 8Q"; this step is that work.
- **Decision:** run the same normaliser in completed states. A section that still breaks a rule after the earlier repairs is emptied with the existing Step 8J wording ("Not enough data to write the [SECTION] section."), the gap is added, the report becomes DEGRADED and continues. An empty Section 4 list gets the exact "No securities…" text, as in active reports. Only a broken writer reply (transport) fails the whole report.
- **Completed-day differences kept on purpose:**
  - Section 1 must survive. The normaliser never empties Section 1 on a completed day, so a broken or empty Section 1 still fails the report and gets the one Step 8L retry.
  - FAILED rescue rule unchanged: a FAILED reply is rescued to DEGRADED only when Section 1 is supported (`normalizeEvidenceLimitedStatus`, untouched). The normaliser skips a completed reply whose status is still FAILED, so FAILED handling is exactly as before.
  - No "nothing survived" fallback (the active-only `ACTIVE_NO_SUPPORTED_ANALYSIS_GAP` path): with Section 1 required, a completed report always keeps at least one section.
  - Section 8 unchanged: `resolveActiveFurtherReadings` returns completed output as is, so completed Further Readings stay the package's own list.
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, `normalizeUsIndependentSections` only. The entry guard now accepts any US-scope report (completed ones only when status is NORMAL or DEGRADED); a `firstEligibleIndex` of 1 on completed days keeps Section 1 out of the blanking set; the final "nothing survived" fallback is gated to active states. Active-state behaviour is unchanged.
- **Must not change (kept):** protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC completed-session-only path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 3/4/6 grounding rules; Section 7 empty when unsupported; post-close evidence never cited as the cause of the completed-session move; completed Section 1 must survive; completed Section 8 behaviour.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 820/820, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; all fixtures use a mocked writer.
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`): "Step 8U.2: in every state group each rule break empties one section, not the report" runs PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY against five breaks (internal-identifier leak, section with no evidence, Section 3 naming no focus subject, word overflow, wrong Section 4 empty-list text). Each returns SUCCESS on one writer call, empties only the target section, keeps every other section's text, and keeps Further Readings unchanged. "Step 8U.2: completed Section 1 must still survive…" shows a broken completed Section 1 still fails with exactly one retry.
  - Changed, because they asserted the old whole-report failure on completed days: "CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY retain completed sections and empty only an unsupported Section 3" (was "…reject unsupported Section 3"; now expects Section 3 emptied and the rest kept); "T5 completed-session malformed splice is still blocked from reaching the user" (now expects Section 5 emptied instead of a failed report; the splice still never reaches the user); "Step 8M: an internal-identifier leak … with one silent retry" (leak moved from Section 3 to Section 1, which must survive, so the test still proves the leak fails the report and gets one retry).
  - Step 8L retry tests (`tests/contract-retry.test.js`) unchanged and passing.
- **Effect on retries:** fewer completed-day `CONTRACT_FAILURE`s means fewer Step 8L retries and lower cost per affected run.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview WEEKEND/CLOSED run to confirm `OPTIONAL_SECTION_VALIDATION` events replace whole-report failures.
- **Supersedes:** the "Scope boundary — explicitly deferred" bullet of Step 8M (the deferred "Step 8Q").

## Step 8U.3 — Section 2 sentence trim instead of blanking, in every market state (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that one causal-sounding sentence in Section 2 (Key Market Drivers) without the needed catalyst emptied the whole section, even when the other sentences were valid descriptions of the drivers. On completed days a failed CNBC recap usually leaves no principal catalyst (post-close news is a later development and can never be one), so any causal sentence lost the whole section.
- **Decision:** for the three Section 2 causal rules — `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` (completed days), `MISSING_COMPLETED_SESSION_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` and `MISSING_CURRENT_SESSION_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` (active days) — remove only the offending sentences and add the fixed uncertainty "The news does not show for certain what moved the market." The section is kept when text remains and it still cites a driver ref (material event or principal catalyst). Otherwise it is emptied exactly as before, with the Step 8J wording "Not enough data to say what moved the market." and its evidence gap.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`: new exported `splitReportSentences`. It splits on `.`, `!` or `?` followed by a space or the end, but not after "U.S."-style initials, "Inc.", "Corp.", month abbreviations and similar, not before a lowercase word, and never inside a decimal. An unclear boundary keeps text together, so a trim can only remove more. The detectors' own `/[.!?;]+/` split is unchanged.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`: the three rules keep their exact trigger conditions; only the response changes, through `trimOrLocalizeDriverCausality`. Offending sentences are those the same detector flags (`hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim`, or `hasPriorCompletedSessionCausalClaim` for the earlier-session rule). Refs are not removed, so on active days a ref that only backed a removed sentence can still reach Further Readings. The event carries `action: 'TRIMMED'` and `removedSentenceCount` (counts only, no prose). A trim adds no evidence gap, so like Step 8O it does not set DEGRADED on its own.
  - `MISSING_CURRENT_SESSION_DRIVER` (no current driver ref cited) still empties the section; it is a ref rule, not a sentence rule.
- **Must not change (kept):** protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; post-close evidence is never named as the cause of the completed-session move (trims only remove sentences; catalyst rules and the classifier throw are unchanged); the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC completed-session-only path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 3/4/6 grounding; Section 7 empty when unsupported; completed Section 1 must survive. No request bytes added.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 825/825, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; all fixtures use a mocked writer.
  - New: splitter unit test (`tests/claude-analysis-contract.test.js`); "completed CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY Section 2 drops only the uncatalyzed causal sentence" and "weekend with a failed CNBC recap and only post-close news keeps descriptive drivers and drops the causal sentence" (`tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js`, the latter also shows an all-causal section is still emptied); "PRE, REGULAR and POST Section 2 drops only a current-session causal sentence without a current catalyst" and "PRE, REGULAR and POST never keep current evidence as the cause of the earlier completed-session move" (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`).
  - Existing single-sentence causal fixtures still empty Section 2 unchanged, because nothing remains after the trim.
- **Known gap, not changed here:** the latest WEEKEND Preview run kept "the jobs report directly drove the session's gains" with no principal catalyst. The detector did not flag it: "session" is not one of its market nouns and "gains" (plural) is not one of its move words, so none of its patterns match. No rule fired, so neither the old blank nor the new trim applied. Because the cited news was post-close, this sentence named post-close evidence as the cause of the completed-session move, which the rule forbids; the detector's word lists are the gap. Left for a later step with Kevin's approval.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview PRE, POST and WEEKEND runs should show `TRIMMED` events for these categories where `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` blanks appeared before.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8U.5 — Unknown references are handled per section instead of failing the report (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that one well-formed but made-up reference (for example `e999`, not in the package) anywhere in the writer reply threw in `assertUsReferenceIntegrity` and failed the whole report as `CONTRACT_FAILURE` in every market state, then used up the Step 8L retry. An active-only localiser for the same case (`UNKNOWN_SECTION_REFERENCE` inside `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`) could never run, because the throw came first. It was dead code with no test.
- **Decision:**
  - A well-formed reference that is not in the package is dropped from its section. If the section still cites at least one valid evidence ref, it is kept (refs trimmed, `action: 'TRIMMED'`, no gap added, so the trim alone does not set DEGRADED).
  - If none of the section's evidence refs were valid, the section is emptied, not trimmed, because a made-up ref may signal made-up text. It uses the Step 8J wording "Not enough data to write the [SECTION] section.", adds that gap, and the report becomes DEGRADED.
  - An empty initiating list in Section 4 gets the exact "No securities…" text with no refs.
  - **Section 1 keeps today's behaviour in every state:** any unknown ref there still fails the report and gets the one Step 8L retry. Completed reports require Section 1. For active reports this is the conservative reading of "keep today's behaviour" and can be relaxed later if Kevin wants.
  - **Malformed transport still fails the report:** a ref token that is not of the form `e<n>` / `t<n>` (including an empty entry from `e1||e2`) is a broken writer reply and still throws.
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`. `assertUsReferenceIntegrity` is replaced by `localizeUnknownUsReferences` at the same point in `attemptClaudeAnalysis`. Its events are emitted as `claudeAnalysisSectionNormalization` with `violationCategory: 'UNKNOWN_SECTION_REFERENCE'` (counts only). The dead active-only localiser in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations` and the now-unused `GENERATED_CITATION_GAPS.referenceIntegrity` message were removed. Later rules (driver, focus, list, opportunity, the Step 8U.2 normaliser and the final validator) still re-check a trimmed section.
- **Must not change (kept):** protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC completed-session-only path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 3/4/6 grounding; Section 7 empty when unsupported; post-close evidence never cited as the cause of the completed-session move; completed Section 1 must survive; completed Section 8 behaviour. No request bytes added.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 832/832, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; all fixtures use a mocked writer.
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, each run in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY): a made-up ref in Section 5 empties only Section 5 on one writer call; made-up refs beside a valid evidence ref are dropped and the output equals the clean reply; a made-up ref in Section 1, a malformed ref token or an empty ref token still fails with exactly one retry; a made-up ref beside the empty-list Section 4 text keeps the exact text.
  - Changed, because they asserted the old whole-report failure: "unknown references in active optional sections and Section 1 remain hard failures" (renamed; Section 5 now empties, Section 1 still fails) and "does not launder unknown global references through section-local normalization" in `tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js` (Sections 2–4 that cited only `e999` are now emptied, and the ref never reaches the report).
  - Unchanged and passing: "rejects malformed or unknown delimiter-encoded compact references without repair" and "rejects unknown references that survive the direct transport" (both put the bad ref in Section 1).
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview runs should show `UNKNOWN_SECTION_REFERENCE` events where whole-report failures with "unknown or invalid reference" appeared before.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8U.9 — Section 3 trims sentences naming an unfocused portfolio company (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that one Section 3 sentence naming a My Stocks or Watchlist company that is not in broad-market focus (`UNFOCUSED_PORTFOLIO_MENTION`) emptied the whole of Section 3 in every state, even when the rest was valid focus analysis. It also found that the Section 3 prompt still told the writer never to use stock telemetry, which contradicts Step 8R.A, and that the active pre-normalization diagnostic counted every stock telemetry ref as a violation, including the linked ones Step 8R.A keeps.
- **Decision:**
  - Remove only the sentences that name an unfocused portfolio company, using the Step 8U.3 sentence splitter. Keep the section only when text remains, it still cites a focus ref, it names a subject of a cited focus entry, and no unfocused name is left. Otherwise empty it as before ("Not enough data to point out specific stocks or sectors."). The trim runs only when no other Section 3 scope violation (non-focus evidence, unlinked stock telemetry) is left; those still empty the section. No qualifier and no gap are added, so like Step 8O the trim alone does not set DEGRADED.
  - Prompt: the Section 3 telemetry wording now matches Step 8R.A, replacing the old wording rather than adding to it. In the system prompt, "never portfolio or watchlist stock telemetry" became "except as the Section 3 telemetry allowlist below permits". In the allowlist, "Do not cite any other telemetry ref in Section 3. This prohibition applies even when a company is both in evidenceContext.broadMarketFocus and My Stocks or Watchlist." became "Stock telemetry is allowed only for a company that is a COMPANY subject of a cited broadMarketFocus entry. Cite no other telemetry ref in Section 3." The benchmark list itself is unchanged.
  - Diagnostics: the active pre-normalization `NON_BENCHMARK_TELEMETRY` category now counts only unlinked stock telemetry (`unlinkedStockTelemetryReferences`), the refs Step 8R.A actually removes.
- **Request size:** the writer request is **5 bytes smaller** (system prompt 19,705 → 19,700 bytes; full request on the rich completed fixture 31,898 → 31,893 bytes).
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js` only: a new block in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations` between the Step 8R.A trim and the existing Section 3 localization (event `UNFOCUSED_PORTFOLIO_MENTION` with `action: 'TRIMMED'` and `removedSentenceCount`, counts only), the two prompt strings, and the one diagnostics line. The matcher (`sectionThreeScopeViolations`) is unchanged.
- **Must not change (kept):** Section 3 still needs a focus ref and a named focus subject; membership in My Stocks or Watchlist still never qualifies a company for Section 3; Section 3/4/6 grounding; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 7 empty when unsupported.
- **Evidence/tests:** same run as Step 8U.5 (832/832, 47/47, `git diff --check` clean).
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, each in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY): one sentence naming Apple (AAPL, Watchlist, not in focus) is removed and the focus text kept; a Section 3 that names only AAPL, or names it in the only sentence that names a focus subject, is still emptied. Plus an active test that the pre-normalization diagnostic no longer flags a linked stock telemetry ref.
  - Changed, because they pinned the old prompt wording: "adds the exact package benchmark refs to the request-specific Section 3 allowlist" and "gives Section 3 non-causal broad-market session-association instructions".
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview runs should show `UNFOCUSED_PORTFOLIO_MENTION` `TRIMMED` events where Section 3 used to be emptied.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8U.6 — Section 6 removes ungrounded opportunity sentences and keeps the risks (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that when the Section 6 opportunity matcher rejected an opportunity (`GENERIC_OPPORTUNITY_CLAIM`, `UNSUPPORTED_OPPORTUNITY_CLAIM` or `UNGROUNDED_OPPORTUNITY_SUBJECT`), the whole of Section 6 was emptied in every state, including valid, cited risk sentences.
- **Decision (approved by Kevin, 2026-10-03, reversing an earlier must-not-change note):**
  - The matcher (`sectionSixOpportunityViolations`, `hasOpportunityClaim`, `hasGenericOpportunityFiller`) stays exactly as strict as before. It still decides whether the opportunity is grounded.
  - When it rejects the opportunity, only the sentences that make an opportunity claim are removed, using the Step 8U.3 sentence splitter. The risk sentences and the section's refs are kept.
  - If no sentence would remain, or the section cites no evidence ref, the section is emptied as before with the Step 8J wording "Not enough data to point out a clear opportunity." and that gap.
  - Same behaviour in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY. No qualifier and no evidence gap are added, so like Step 8O the trim alone does not set DEGRADED. The kept text is re-checked by the Step 8U.2 normaliser and the final validator.
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, the Section 6 block in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`. The event keeps the same `violationCategory` (and `violationSubtype`) and adds `action: 'TRIMMED'`, `offendingReferenceCount: 0` and `removedSentenceCount` when trimmed (counts only, no prose).
- **Request size:** no change (system prompt 19,700 bytes; full request on the rich completed fixture 31,893 bytes, same as HEAD).
- **Must not change (kept):** the literal Section 6 opportunity matcher; Section 7 empty when unsupported; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 3 grounding.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 836/836, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; all fixtures use a mocked writer.
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, each in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY, for all three categories): a risk sentence plus an ungrounded opportunity sentence keeps only the risk, stays NORMAL, uses one writer call and equals the risk-only reply; a Section 6 made only of ungrounded opportunity sentences is still emptied; a grounded opportunity is unchanged.
  - Changed: "T6 ungrounded Section 6 opportunity still localizes exactly as before when style words are present" (Step 8F). It asserted the old blanking. It now asserts the opportunity is still detected as `UNGROUNDED_OPPORTUNITY_SUBJECT`, and that only that sentence is removed while the style-only sentence stays.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview runs should show Section 6 `TRIMMED` events where Section 6 used to be emptied.
- **Supersedes:** the Section 6 part of the must-not-change notes in Step 8F ("Section 3/4/6 grounding (including the literal UNGROUNDED_OPPORTUNITY_SUBJECT rule)") and Step 8O ("Section 6 literal opportunity grounding"), but only for the result of a rejection (blank → sentence trim). The literal matcher itself is not superseded.

## Step 8U.7 — Section 4 drops other-list references instead of blanking, when it is safe (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that any reference outside the initiating list in Section 4 (`NON_INITIATING_EVIDENCE` / `NON_INITIATING_TELEMETRY`) emptied the whole section in every state, even when the text was only about the initiating list and a valid initiating-list ref remained.
- **Decision (approved by Kevin, 2026-10-03, reversing an earlier must-not-change note):**
  - The other-list refs are dropped and Section 4 is kept only when all of these hold: every offending ref belongs to a security in the other list (My Stocks vs Watchlist; a security in both counts as initiating); at least one initiating-list evidence ref remains; and the text names no security from the other list (its symbol, case-sensitive whole word, or its instrument name from the package's stock telemetry).
  - Otherwise the section is emptied as before with "Not enough data to comment on the stocks in this list." and that gap. That includes a ref from outside both lists (for example a benchmark telemetry ref or broad-market news), which is the conservative reading.
  - Same behaviour in every state. No gap is added on a trim, so the trim alone does not set DEGRADED.
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, the Section 4 block in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`; the existing `NON_INITIATING_*` events gain `action: 'TRIMMED'` when trimmed (counts only). `containsWholeTerm` is now exported from `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js` (unchanged) so the same name matching as Section 3 is reused.
- **Known limit:** a company named only by a plain name (for example "Apple") with no stock telemetry in the package can't be recognised as an other-list security. That is the same limit the Section 3 portfolio-mention check has.
- **Request size:** no change (same figures as Step 8U.6).
- **Must not change (kept):** Section 4 covers only the initiating list, and its refs must belong to it in the final report; the empty-list "No securities…" text; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 7 empty when unsupported.
- **Evidence/tests:** same run as Step 8U.6 (836/836, 47/47, `git diff --check` clean).
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, each in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY): an appended Watchlist evidence ref is dropped, Section 4 stays NORMAL, uses one writer call and equals the clean reply. Section 4 is still emptied when the text names a Watchlist security (AAPL), when only other-list refs are cited, or when a ref from outside both lists is cited.
  - Changed: "Section 4 accepts selected refs and still localizes two appended refs from outside either initiating list" in `tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js`. Its myStocks case still empties (the appended `t1` is a benchmark ref, outside both lists). Its watchlist case appended only My Stocks refs to text that names no My Stocks security, so it now trims. The test was renamed and asserts both outcomes.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview runs should show `NON_INITIATING_*` `TRIMMED` events where Section 4 used to be emptied.
- **Supersedes:** the Section 4 part of the Step 8O must-not-change note ("Section 4 initiating-list isolation and its null-all behavior"), for null-all behaviour only. Initiating-list isolation is kept. It also supersedes the Section 4 part of "Section 3/4/6 grounding" in Step 8F to the same extent.

## Step 9B — Section 4: one unrelated stock never blanks the whole section (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** under Step 8U.7, Section 4 (My Stocks / Watchlist) was still emptied when the text named a security from the other list, or when it cited a ref that belonged to neither list (for example an index). One unrelated stock or index ref could wipe out valid text about every other stock in the initiating list.
- **Decision (Kevin's rule, 2026-10-03):**
  - Every Section 4 ref outside the initiating list is dropped, whether it belongs to the other list or to neither list (an index, broad-market news, another stock's telemetry). A security in both lists still counts as initiating.
  - Sentences naming a stock outside the initiating list are removed with the Step 8U.3 sentence splitter. A stock is recognised by its symbol (case-sensitive whole word) or its instrument name from the package's stock telemetry; it counts when it is in the other list or has stock telemetry in the package. Index names are not stocks, so a sentence naming the S&P 500 is kept.
  - Every other stock's text and refs are kept. The section is emptied with the Step 8J wording "Not enough data to comment on the stocks in this list." and that gap only when no text or no initiating-list evidence ref is left.
  - Same behaviour in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY. A trim adds no gap, so on its own it does not set DEGRADED.
- **Implementation:** `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, the Section 4 block in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`. It still runs only when Section 4 cites a non-initiating ref. The `NON_INITIATING_EVIDENCE` / `NON_INITIATING_TELEMETRY` events keep `action: 'TRIMMED'` on a trim; a new `NON_INITIATING_MENTION` event (`action: 'TRIMMED'`, `removedSentenceCount`, counts only, no prose) is added when sentences are removed.
- **Known limits (unchanged from Step 8U.7):** a company named only by a plain name (for example "Broadcom") with no stock telemetry and not in the other list can't be recognised, so its sentence stays while its ref is dropped. Text naming an other-list stock with no non-initiating ref cited is not checked, as before.
- **Request size:** no change (system prompt 19,700 bytes; full request on the rich completed fixture 31,893 bytes, same as HEAD).
- **Must not change (kept):** Section 4 covers only the initiating list, and its refs must belong to it in the final report; the exact empty-list "No securities…" text; the Step 8J empty wording; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 7 empty when unsupported.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 839/839, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; all fixtures use a mocked writer.
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, each in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY): ten My Stocks securities, with a Section 4 that discusses ten stocks of which one is a Watchlist stock (AAPL) named in one sentence with its ref cited — only that sentence and ref are removed, the other nine stocks' text is kept, NORMAL on one writer call, equal to the clean reply; an index (`t1`, S&P 500) ref in Section 4 is dropped and the section kept unchanged; a Section 4 with only unrelated content and refs is emptied with the Step 8J wording and the rest of the report survives.
  - Changed, because they expected the stricter Step 8U.7 result:
    - "Step 8U.7: in every state Section 4 is still emptied when it names the other list or keeps no initiating ref" (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`), renamed to "…when it keeps no initiating ref or only unrelated text". Its "text names a Watchlist security" and "a ref from outside both lists" cases now trim and moved to the Step 9B tests; it now checks only-other-list refs and only-unrelated content.
    - "Section 4 accepts selected refs, localizes refs from outside both lists, and (Step 8U.7) trims refs only from the other list" (`tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js`): the myStocks case (appended benchmark `t1`) now trims like the watchlist case; renamed.
    - "localizes uncited driver causality and broad-market evidence leaking into Section 4 independently": the broad-market `e4` ref is now dropped from Section 4 and the section kept (Section 2 is still emptied); renamed.
    - "localizes non-initiating Section 4 telemetry without filtering references into unsupported prose": the benchmark `t1` ref is now dropped and the section kept; renamed "Step 9B: drops non-initiating Section 4 benchmark telemetry and keeps the section text".
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview runs should show `NON_INITIATING_*` and `NON_INITIATING_MENTION` `TRIMMED` events where Section 4 used to be emptied.
- **Supersedes:** the stricter parts of Step 8U.7 — emptying Section 4 when the text names an other-list security, and emptying it when a ref belongs to neither list. The rest of Step 8U.7 (other-list refs dropped, empty when no initiating evidence ref remains) is kept.

## Step 8U.8 — One causal-claim rule for Sections 1 and 3–7 in every market state (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that a causal claim without the needed catalyst in Sections 1 and 3–7 emptied the whole section on active days (PRE, REGULAR, POST), even when the other sentences were valid, and was not checked at all on completed days (CLOSED, WEEKEND, HOLIDAY). So the same sentence was treated two different ways, and on completed days a section could name post-close news as the cause of the completed-session move.
- **Decision:**
  - One rule in all six states. It fires when a section makes a causal claim (`hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim`, the Step 8U.1 detector) and cites no principal catalyst of the needed kind. Completed days: any principal catalyst (post-close news can never be one). Active days: a completed-session catalyst for a claim about the earlier session, otherwise a current-session catalyst (unchanged from before).
  - Only the offending sentences are removed, using the Step 8U.3 splitter, and the fixed uncertainty "The news does not show for certain what moved the market." is added. The section is kept when text remains, it still cites an evidence ref, the rule no longer fires and, for Section 3, the text still names a subject of a cited focus ref.
  - Otherwise Sections 3–7 are emptied as before with "Not enough data to say what moved the market." and that gap.
  - Section 1 is never emptied by this rule. If the trim can't keep it, active days keep today's Step 8U.2 blank, and completed days keep Section 1 exactly as written (today's behaviour; completed Section 1 must survive and is not checked by the validator for this rule).
  - A trim adds no evidence gap, so like Step 8U.3 it does not set DEGRADED on its own. Refs are left as cited, so on active days Further Readings is unchanged.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations`: the active-only loop that emptied Sections 3–7 is replaced by one loop over Sections 1 and 3–7 for every US state. Events keep the existing categories (`MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` on completed days; `MISSING_COMPLETED_SESSION_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` / `MISSING_CURRENT_SESSION_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` on active days) and add `action: 'TRIMMED'`, `offendingReferenceCount: 0` and `removedSentenceCount` on a trim (counts only, no prose).
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`, `validateClaudeAnalysisOutput`: the same rule now also checks Sections 3–7 on completed US days (message "completed-session market causality lacks a principal catalyst"). The active message is unchanged.
- **Replay before building (no paid calls):** 12 saved completed (WEEKEND/CLOSED) reports and 2 saved PRE replays were run through the full pipeline with a mocked writer, before and after. 3 of the 12 completed reports lose one sentence each; none loses a section, all stay SUCCESS with the same status, refs and Further Readings; the 2 PRE replays are unchanged. The three sentences: a WEEKEND Section 1 sentence about the 10-year yield "driven by inflation concerns … yet stocks still advanced" (the detector reads it as a cause of the stock move; arguably a false positive); a WEEKEND Section 5 sentence "bank stocks declined on rate-increase concerns …"; and a WEEKEND Section 3 sentence saying a compute deal "has driven Akamai shares up nearly 20%" (cited news, but not a principal catalyst). The CLOSED 29 Sep and WEEKEND 27 Sep report PDFs (text matched back to package refs) lose nothing.
- **Request size:** no change (system prompt 19,700 bytes; full request on the rich completed fixture 31,893 bytes, same as Step 9B). The qualifier is output text, not request text.
- **Must not change (kept):** protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; post-close evidence is never named as the cause of the completed-session move; completed Section 1 must survive; the catalyst rules themselves and the classifier throw; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the CNBC path (untouched); the frozen 8-section structure; Section 3/4/6 grounding; Section 7 empty when unsupported (this step only removes text, never adds any).
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 843/843, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; all fixtures use a mocked writer.
  - New (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, each in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY): an uncatalyzed causal sentence added to Section 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 or 7 is removed, the qualifier added, the rest of the report unchanged, NORMAL on one writer call (on completed days the cited ref is the post-close recap; on active days current news cited as the cause of the previous session); on active days a current-session causal sentence citing only the completed-session catalyst is removed from Sections 1, 5, 6 and 7; a section with only uncatalyzed causal text is emptied (Sections 3–7), active Section 1 keeps the Step 8U.2 blank and completed Section 1 is kept as written; a causal sentence that cites the needed catalyst is unchanged.
  - Existing tests: none changed. The completed-depth test ("CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY retain completed broad-market depth across Sections 2, 3, 6 and 7") passes unchanged.
- **Known gap (unchanged):** the detector's word lists, recorded under Step 8U.3 ("the jobs report directly drove the session's gains" is not flagged). This step uses the same detector, so it inherits that gap.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision; Preview PRE, POST and WEEKEND runs should show `TRIMMED` events for these categories on Sections 1 and 3–7 where sections used to be emptied (active) or kept unchecked (completed).
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8U.10 — A failed completed-day classifier degrades instead of returning 502 (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-03 · **Branch:** step-8t-integration · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed; live validation pending.
- **Context:** the Step 8U review found that when the evidence-role classifier failed or could not run, active days (PRE, REGULAR, POST) continued with no roles and one plain gap, but completed days (CLOSED, WEEKEND, HOLIDAY) threw, so the package endpoint returned 502 and the user got no report at all.
- **Decision:** completed days now take the same fallback as active days. With no roles, no principal catalysts and no broad-market focus, the package carries the existing gap "Could not sort the news by what it explains, so no news is presented as the cause of a move." and emits the existing `evidenceRoleClassificationFallback` / `CONSERVATIVE_UNCLASSIFIED` diagnostic. The writer then runs as normal: the existing no-roles rule empties Sections 2 and 3 with the Step 8J wording, and the report is DEGRADED. This covers a failed or thrown classifier call, a classifier result the package rejects (invented ref, partial coverage, a post-close item labelled as a principal catalyst), and the case where the classifier cannot run (no fresh benchmark or no evidence to classify).
- **Implementation:** `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` only. The completed-day rethrow in the classifier `catch`, and the completed-day "Completed-session evidence-role classification is unavailable" throw, are removed. Nothing else changes.
- **Post-close rule kept:** the classifier still rejects any output that names post-close (subsequent-development) evidence as a principal catalyst (`lib/claude-evidence-role-classification.js`, unchanged). The rejected output is thrown away whole, so in the fallback no item gets a role, and CNBC news items (kept only when the classifier rates them material) are not added to the package.
- **Request size:** no change. System prompt 19,700 bytes; full request on the rich completed fixture 31,893 bytes, the same as Step 8U.8. A completed run that used to stop at 502 now sends one normal-size writer request.
- **Must not change (kept):** protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; CNBC and Yahoo acquisition (fetching, searching, article extraction, source routing) untouched; post-close evidence is never a principal catalyst; every other completed-day check, including that completed Section 1 must survive; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the frozen 8-section structure; one writer request per run.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 846/846, 47/47 syntax checks, `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; the writer is mocked.
  - New (`tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js`): in CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY, a classifier that returns CONTRACT_FAILURE, returns UPSTREAM_FAILURE, or throws gives a valid package with no roles and the gap, and the writer then returns a DEGRADED report with that gap, Section 1 kept and Section 2 emptied with "Not enough data to point to a main market driver."; a working classifier in the same three states keeps its roles with no gap and no fallback; PRE, REGULAR and POST classifier failure fallback is unchanged.
  - Changed existing tests (each asserted the old 502 and now asserts the fallback): "completed-session classifier failure or invented ref…", "completed-session partial classifier coverage…", "completed-session package … when only stock telemetry survives", "completed-session classifier rejects a subsequent-development principal catalyst" (now also checks that no CNBC item reaches the package), "a failed benchmark does not stop portfolio acquisition…", "completed classifier failure … recoverable source failures".
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision. Live: a completed-day run where the classifier fails should show `evidenceRoleClassificationFallback` and a DEGRADED report instead of `PACKAGE_ASSEMBLY_FAILURE`.
- **Supersedes:** the earlier rule that a completed-day classifier failure fails the package (the "fails instead of returning shallow material" behaviour).

## Step 9D.2 — Section-rule settings table and one "what kind of day is it" helper (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04 · **Branch:** step-9e-repo-notes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** the Step 9D plan found the same "is this an active US day" boolean computed four separate times (`lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`'s `activeUsIndependentSurvival` and `isActiveUsRequest`, `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`'s `isActiveUsAnalysis`, and an inline copy inside `emitActiveSessionDiagnostics`), plus a fifth, private literal for the three completed states in `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` that nothing else could read. This step gives both one shared home, as a pure refactor: nothing a report reader would see changes.
- **Decision:** new file `lib/section-rules.js` holds a settings row per US market state (the six: PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND, HOLIDAY) and `reportSettings(market, state, hasCurrentEvidence)`, the one helper that reads it. The four duplicate booleans are replaced with calls into this file; the orchestration literal is replaced with this file's new `US_COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES` export.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/section-rules.js` (new): `US_COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES = ['CLOSED', 'WEEKEND', 'HOLIDAY']` (previously only a private literal at `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js:92`, per the Step 9D plan's inventory, §1). `US_STATE_SETTINGS`, one row per state, built from `US_ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES` (imported from `lib/us-active-session-evidence.js`, the canonical active-state list) and the new `US_COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES`. Each row records, as data only (nothing reads these fields yet): `sessionMode`, `sectionOneRequired` (rule 25), `catalystScope` (rules 13–16, informational at this stage — the causal rules themselves are untouched), `nothingSurvivedFallback` (rule 28), `failedRescue` (rule 10) and `furtherReadingsSource` (rule 29). Values reproduce the Step 9D.1 matrix's recorded behaviour exactly; see that file's comments for the state-by-state detail.
  - `reportSettings(market, state, hasCurrentEvidence = false)`: a non-US market returns the frozen `{sectionRulesEnabled: false}` sentinel (today's no-op, unchanged). A US market with an unrecognized state throws `TypeError: Unsupported US market state: ${state}` — the exact message `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`'s existing `analysisModeForMarketState` already throws for the same condition, so this is a new, stricter contract for a new function, not a new kind of error in the system. Otherwise returns the frozen settings row plus `sectionRulesEnabled: true` and `hasCurrentEvidence`.
  - `reportSettingsForInput(input, hasCurrentEvidence)` and `isActiveUsRequest(input)`: derive the market/state pair from a canonical Claude analysis input (as the four replaced copies did, by finding the US entry in `marketPackages`) and call `reportSettings`. A non-US scope, or a US scope with no US `marketPackage` present, is disabled rather than throwing — neither was an error case in any of the four replaced copies.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: `activeUsIndependentSurvival` and `isActiveUsRequest` are now one-line delegations to the shared `isActiveUsRequest`; the inline check inside `emitActiveSessionDiagnostics` is replaced by a call to the file's own (now-shared) `isActiveUsRequest`. The now-unused `US_ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES` import was removed.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`: `isActiveUsAnalysis` is now a one-line delegation. `US_ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES` stays imported here — it is still used directly at two other, unrelated call sites (the active-session anchor checks) that this step does not touch.
  - `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: only the `COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES` literal (line 92) was changed, to `new Set(US_COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES)` from the new import; `ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES` (line 91) and every other line in the file are untouched, per the task's instruction to change only those two lines.
- **One disclosed, verified-unreachable strictness change:** because `isActiveUsRequest`/`isActiveUsAnalysis` are now built on the throwing `reportSettings`, a US `marketPackage` carrying a market state outside the six recognized ones now throws where the four original copies would have silently answered "not active". No current test exercises this, and in production it cannot occur: `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`'s own `analysisModeForMarketState` already throws on exactly this condition during package assembly, strictly before a US `marketPackage` can reach the writer-invocation code these four functions live in. Recorded here rather than hidden; not a behaviour change on any reachable path.
- **Must not change (kept):** every section rule's own logic is untouched — this step only centralizes the "is this active" check and adds settings data nothing yet reads. The CNBC acquisition path, `?rev=` tokens, and Neon persistence were not touched.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 866/866 passing (859 before this step + 7 new test() calls in the new `tests/section-rules-settings.test.js`), syntax checks pass on every file under `api/` and `lib/` (including the new `lib/section-rules.js`), `git diff --check` clean. The Step 9D.1 matrix (`tests/section-rules-matrix.test.js`, 13 tests) passes unedited, including its writer-request-size guard (system prompt 19,700 bytes; full request 31,893 bytes, unchanged). No paid Anthropic calls. `git status`/`git diff --stat` show only `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`, `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`, `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` (all three minimal, shown above), plus the two new files — no existing test file was changed.
- **Not shared (recorded, not built here):** the five remaining settings fields (`sectionOneRequired`, `catalystScope`, `nothingSurvivedFallback`, `failedRescue`, `furtherReadingsSource`) are populated but not yet consumed anywhere; wiring them into the actual rule logic is Steps 9D.3–9D.5, each to be checked against the Step 9D.1 matrix as it lands.
- **Pending:** Steps 9D.3–9D.6. Kevin's decision on the open "no more than 2 steps waiting unmerged" question recorded under Step 9D.1.

## Step 9D.3 — Shared trim ladder (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-04 · **Branch:** step-9e-repo-notes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** the Step 9D plan's inventory found nine separate "trim then blank" implementations across the section rules, each reimplementing the same four mechanical steps (drop a bad reference, drop a bad sentence, add a caveat, blank only if nothing valid remains) slightly differently, plus four copies of the shared "names a cited focus subject" check and three copies of the empty-initiating-list rewrite. This step gives the mechanical steps one shared home in `lib/section-rules.js` and moves four of those nine rules onto it, as a pure refactor.
- **Decision:** add the ladder's four steps as small, composable primitives (not one rigid pipeline, since which steps a rule uses and what "nothing valid remains" means for it differs rule by rule), and move rules 4, 18–21, 22 and 24 onto them. Rules not listed here (9, 10, 16, 25, 27, 28, 29) are untouched; wiring the settings table's remaining fields into them is later Step 9D work.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/section-rules.js`: added `withFilteredReferences` (step 1: drop refs a predicate rejects), `dropOffendingSentences` (step 2: drop sentences a predicate rejects, using a caller-supplied splitter to avoid a require cycle with `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`; returns the section completely unchanged, not rejoined, when nothing is removed, so a rule that removes nothing never alters the writer's exact wording or spacing), `withCaveat` (step 3, idempotent), `blankSection` (step 4), plus `emptyInitiatingListSection` (the shared empty-list rewrite), `sectionUnavailableMessage` (the shared "Not enough data to write the … section." wording) and `normalizeSectionText` / `namesCitedFocusSubject` (the shared text-normalizing and subject-match check).
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`:
    - `localizeUnknownUsReferences` (rule 4): the per-section ref-filtering and the three outcomes (empty-list rewrite, trim, blank) now call the shared helpers; the Section 1 exception (any unknown ref there still throws, never reaches the ladder) is untouched.
    - The shared `localize(index, message)` closure used by every rule in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations` (including ones not moved this step, such as rules 9, 11–16) now calls `blankSection` internally -- a drop-in equivalent, so this changes nothing for any rule, moved or not.
    - Section 3 (rules 18–20: `NON_FOCUS_EVIDENCE`, `NON_BENCHMARK_TELEMETRY`, `UNFOCUSED_PORTFOLIO_MENTION`) and Section 6 (rule 24: the opportunity trim) now call `withFilteredReferences` / `dropOffendingSentences` / `namesCitedFocusSubject` instead of their own inline copies. Rule 21 (the Section 3 final blank) needed no separate change: it already called the now-shared `localize`.
    - Section 4 (rule 22, Step 9B's `NON_INITIATING_*` trim) now composes `withFilteredReferences` then `dropOffendingSentences` for its combined ref-and-sentence trim; it adds no caveat, exactly as today.
    - All three copies of the empty-initiating-list rewrite (in rule 4 above, in the last-resort `OPTIONAL_SECTION_VALIDATION` pass, and in the active-only "nothing survived" fallback) now call `emptyInitiatingListSection`. Only that one rewritten branch changed in each of the latter two functions; their other branch (the plain blank, which uses `uncertainties: []` with no message, a different shape from `blankSection`) was left untouched rather than forced to fit, and the "nothing survived" fallback and `OPTIONAL_SECTION_VALIDATION` pass are otherwise unchanged.
  - Did not touch: the Step 8U.8 causal-claim loop (rule 16, Sections 1 and 3–7) and its own copy of the text-normalizing/subject-match check -- out of scope for this step, since rule 16 is not in the list of rules being moved; `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`'s own, separate copy of the same subject-match check (the validator's rule 17, which has no cleanup counterpart) -- also out of scope and left exactly as is, to avoid a require cycle (`lib/section-rules.js` depends on neither contract nor invocation, by design) and because it is not one of the rules this step moves.
  - `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`, `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`'s `isActiveUsAnalysis`, and the CNBC acquisition path were not touched in this step.
- **Must not change (kept):** every rule's trigger condition, event shape, wording, and the writer request bytes are unchanged -- this step only centralizes the mechanical steps already being performed, verified by running the full suite and the unedited Step 9D.1 matrix before and after.
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 884/884 passing (866 before this step + 18 new unit tests in the new `tests/section-rules-ladder.test.js`), syntax checks pass on every file under `api/` and `lib/`, `git diff --check` clean. The Step 9D.1 matrix (13 tests) and the Step 9D.2 settings tests (7 tests) pass completely unedited, including the writer-request-size guard (system prompt 19,700 bytes; full request 31,893 bytes, unchanged). No paid Anthropic calls. `git status`/`git diff --stat` show only `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js` (137 lines changed, all within the moved rules and the shared `localize` closure) and `lib/section-rules.js`, plus the one new test file -- no existing test file was changed.
- **Not shared in this step (recorded, not built here):** the Step 8U.8 causal-claim loop (rule 16) and its own `normalizeText`/subject-match copy; the validator's own subject-match copy in `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js` (rule 17, no cleanup counterpart); the generic `localize` closure's call sites for rules not listed above (9, 10, 11–16, 25, 27, 28, 29) are mechanically unchanged by this step (their blanking now goes through `blankSection` only because `localize` itself does, not because those rules were individually touched). Unifying rule 16 across Sections 1 and 3–7 is Step 9D.4.
- **Pending:** Steps 9D.4–9D.6, each to be checked against the Step 9D.1 matrix as it lands. Kevin's decision on the open "no more than 2 steps waiting unmerged" question recorded under Step 9D.1.

## Step 9D.4 — One "causal claim without a cause" rule for Sections 1 to 7 (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-05 · **Branch:** step-9e-repo-notes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** Section 2 (rules 13–15: no current evidence, a prior-session claim, a current-session claim) and Sections 1 and 3–7 (rule 16, Step 8U.8) each checked "does this causal claim cite the right kind of catalyst" with their own copy of the trim-then-decide mechanics and, in rule 16's case, its own copy of the text-normalizing/subject-match check already shared everywhere else since Step 9D.3.
- **Decision, and a safety finding that shaped it:** before merging anything, I checked whether rule 16's single generic predicate (`hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim`) could simply replace Section 2's three distinct predicates. It cannot, without changing behaviour: the sentence "Stocks fell on September 4." is flagged by `hasPriorCompletedSessionCausalClaim` (Section 2's prior-claim predicate) but **not** by `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim` (rule 16's predicate, and Section 2's own non-prior predicate), because the active detector strips the trailing "on <date>" clause before testing and the prior-session detector does not. Verified directly against the real detectors (see the new test file). A merged predicate would therefore keep a sentence one of today's rules removes, or remove one the other keeps -- a real behaviour change. So the unification shares only the **mechanical** steps (drop the offending sentences, decide whether to keep or blank, add the caveat), not a single merged "which sentence is offending" formula; each call site still supplies its own predicate, exactly as it does today.
  - **A second asymmetry found, also preserved, not fixed:** Section 2's rule for "no current-session evidence at all" accepts **any** cited principal catalyst. Rule 16 (Sections 1,3–7) has no equivalent fallback -- confirmed by running the real pipeline: on an active day with only older evidence and a non-prior causal claim in Section 7, today's code still trims the sentence demanding a current-session catalyst, even though a real, older principal catalyst is cited. This is distinct from the already-recorded Section 2-vs-validator inconsistency (Step 9D.1) and is not mentioned in the Step 9D plan, so it is flagged here rather than silently carried forward or silently fixed.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/section-rules.js`: added `causalClaimTrim(section, {offendingSentence, splitSentences, keepIf, caveat})`, the shared mechanical core (drop offending sentences via the Step 9D.3 `dropOffendingSentences`, decide via the caller's `keepIf`, add the caveat via `withCaveat` on success). Returns `{trimmed: false}` on failure; the caller still does its own blanking via `localize`, exactly as before.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`:
    - `trimOrLocalizeDriverCausality` (Section 2's shared helper for its three rules) now calls `causalClaimTrim` instead of inlining the sentence-split/decide/caveat steps. Its "keep if some driver ref is cited" check and each rule's own offending-sentence predicate are unchanged.
    - The Sections 1,3–7 loop (rule 16) now also calls `causalClaimTrim`, and its own copy of `normalizeText`/subject-match is replaced by the already-shared `namesCitedFocusSubject`. The one genuinely state-driven decision in this rule -- whether a day is active -- now reads `reportSettingsForInput(input).sessionMode === 'ACTIVE'` instead of calling `isActiveUsRequest(input)` directly, per the Step 9D plan's "differences... must come from the settings table" instruction. Section 2's own three gates (`activeWithCurrentEvidence`, unchanged as a local boolean) were deliberately **not** routed through the settings table: that boolean is "does current evidence exist," not a live-vs-closed state lookup, and forcing it through `settings.hasCurrentEvidence` would have relied on `undefined` behaving like `false` in a boolean context for non-US scope -- correct today, but needlessly fragile, so it was left as the plain existing variable.
    - The recorded Section 2 inconsistency (Step 9D.1) now has an explicit `PRESERVED ON PURPOSE, NOT A DECISION` comment at the exact `if` block where it lives (the "no current evidence" branch of Section 2's rule), pointing at Step 9D.1 and this entry. Nothing about it changed.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js` (rule 17, the validator's own Section 3 subject-match check): shared without a require cycle, since this file already requires `lib/section-rules.js` one-directionally. Replaced the inline `normalizedSubjectText`/`mentionsSubject` computation with a call to the shared `namesCitedFocusSubject` (confirmed byte-identical to `normalizedSubjectText`'s formula first). `normalizedSubjectText` itself is unchanged and still used by its other, unrelated call sites in this file.
- **Must not change (kept):** every rule's trigger condition, event shape, wording, category selection, and the writer request bytes are unchanged; verified by running the full suite before and after each edit (884/884 both times after the invocation.js rewiring, before adding this step's own new tests).

## Step 9D.5a — One rule registry read by both the cleanup stage and the final checker (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-08 · **Branch:** step-9d-shared-section-module · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.

- **Decision:** behaviour-neutral. Same report text, same events in the same order, same event wording, same checker messages byte for byte, same writer request bytes. The cleanup stage keeps its exact rule order; each call site now asks the registry for its core test only.

Contract-level helpers (causal-claim, prose, Section 6 matcher, word cap) are passed in, so this file still requires neither the contract nor the invocation. Added the settings readers `failedRescueScope`, `sectionOneRequired` and `nothingSurvivedFallbackEnabled`: an enabled row reads its own field, and the disabled sentinel (Singapore, Hong Kong, or a US scope without a US package) gets the completed-day answer, because that is what every non-active report got before. Also added `appendFirstUseReferences` (rule 30) and the `US_SECTION_RULE_STATES` export.

Rule 25 reads `sectionOneRequired(reportSettingsForInput(input))` instead of `!isActiveUsAnalysis(input)`, which gives the same answer, including for Singapore and Hong Kong.

Both preserved inconsistencies (Step 9D.1's Section 2-vs-checker case, Step 9D.4's rule-16 fallback gap) behave exactly as before.

Rule 21 is cleanup only. Rule 22's `NON_INITIATING_MENTION` sentence trim is cleanup only. Rule 23's exact-statement comparison stays in the checker. Rule 29 (Further Readings source) was not rewired. Also not shared: the active-only `validationViolationCategories` gate in rule 27, which is not one of the existing settings fields, and the pre-cleanup Section 6 diagnostic, which computes the matcher even with no opportunity claim, so it cannot use rule 24's detector without changing what it reports.

Those three were added because the guard showed them uncovered: the 9D.1 matrix has no rule-8 row, labels rule 27 only in a comment, and runs rule 28 in REGULAR only. Checker-side rows pin the exact messages. Every row also passes against the HEAD (`78d422f`) versions of the three lib files, so it records today's behaviour, not the new code's.

- **Date:** 2026-10-08 · **Branch:** step-9d-shared-section-module · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
