## Step 9D.6 — Market-neutral section rules (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-09 · **Branch:** step-9d-shared-section-module · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** every section rule still tested `selectedScope === 'US'` directly, or fell back to a US-only literal (the causal detector's benchmark names, the text-normalizers' 'en-US' locale), so none of it could be reused for Singapore or Hong Kong without another copy. This step removes those switches and literals, replacing them with a market settings table the section rules read instead. It does not enable SG or HK -- their rows stay `sectionRulesEnabled: false`, so their reports are unchanged.
- **Decision:** behaviour-neutral for US. Same report text, same events in the same order, same event wording, same checker messages, same writer request bytes. SG and HK reports are unchanged from before this step. Build once in the shared module; no per-market copies of any rule.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/section-rules.js`: new `MARKET_SETTINGS` table (US, SG, HK) and `marketSettings(market)`, the one reader. The US row holds `sectionRulesEnabled: true`, `activeStates`/`completedStates` (the same `US_ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES`/`US_COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES` as before), `benchmarkNames: ['s&p 500', 'nasdaq', 'dow']` (today's literal, moved here) and `locale: 'en-US'` (today's literal, moved here). SG and HK hold only `sectionRulesEnabled: false`. `reportSettings(market, state, hasCurrentEvidence)` is now fully generic: it reads `marketSettings(market)` for the enabled gate and the active/completed state sets, instead of special-casing `market === 'US'`; its returned shape and every exported helper built on it (`reportSettingsForInput`, `isActiveUsRequest`, `failedRescueScope`, `sectionOneRequired`, `nothingSurvivedFallbackEnabled`) are unchanged, so no existing test needed editing. The old `usMarketPackage` (hard-coded to `market === 'US'`) is now `marketPackageForInput`, which matches a canonical input's own `selectedScope` against its marketPackages generically; a scope with no matching package (including the legacy `'ALL'` scope) is disabled exactly as before.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: the three switches named in the task each now read the settings table instead of a literal: `localizeUnknownUsReferences` (rule 4) and `normalizeActiveTransportMetadata` (rule 5) check `reportSettingsForInput(input).sectionRulesEnabled`; `normalizeUsIndependentSections` (rules 27/28) reuses the `settings` it already computes for rule 25/27's split.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`: `completedUs` (rule 16's completed-branch gate) now reads `reportSettingsForInput(input).sectionRulesEnabled` instead of `selectedScope === 'US'`. `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim` takes an optional `benchmarkNames` parameter defaulting to `marketSettings('US').benchmarkNames` -- every existing call site (both direct and through `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim`/`hasPriorCompletedSessionCausalClaim`, which pass none) keeps the US list unchanged. `normalizedSubjectText` and `containsWholeTerm` take an optional `locale` parameter the same way, defaulting to `marketSettings('US').locale`.
  - `lib/section-rules.js`: `normalizeSectionText` and `namesCitedFocusSubject` take the same optional `locale` parameter, same default, same reasoning.
  - `US_SECTION_RULE_STATES`'s export is now built directly from `US_ACTIVE_SESSION_STATES`/`US_COMPLETED_SESSION_STATES` (same six names, same order) rather than from the now-removed `US_STATE_SETTINGS` lookup object.
- **A finding, not fixed here:** `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations` (which houses rules 11-24, the causal-claim, Section 3, Section 4 and Section 6 rules) has never had a market guard at all -- confirmed unchanged at `ee7ddb7` and today, by probing a Singapore package with an uncatalogued causal claim: Section 2 is blanked by `MISSING_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` exactly as it would be for the US. The final checker's equivalent rules (11, 12, 17, and others) are not scope-gated either. This is not one of the four switches the task named, not something this step changed, and not a regression -- it is a pre-existing gap the task's "exactly as they do today" requirement is measured against. Left as a blocker for Step 9M, below.
- **Why the benchmark-names/locale change is a default, not a full thread-through:** `hasActiveDirectMarketCausalClaim(content)` and most of the registry's rules call these detectors with no market context at all (no `input`, just `content`), and are called from many sites across `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js` and `lib/section-rules.js`. Threading a real per-market value through every one of those sites would touch far more of the codebase than this step's four named switches, for no behaviour change today (SG/HK stay disabled), and risks the exact kind of per-call-site copy this refactor exists to avoid. Instead, the default itself now reads from the market settings table (so the US value is genuinely data, not a hard-coded literal), and the new test file proves the underlying functions are parameter-driven, not hard-coded, by calling them directly with a different market's values.
- **Could not share / blocks SG and HK (Step 9M):**
  - `completedSessionDatePatterns` (`lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`) still filters `marketPackage.market === 'US'` directly. Generalizing it to read `selectedScope` would change `tests/section-rules-causal.test.js`'s own test fixture (a package with `market: 'US'` but no `analysisRequest` at all), which this step must not edit. Left as-is.
  - The `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', ...)` calls that build the English weekday/month names `completedSessionDatePatterns`/`activeSessionOpeningDatePatterns` match against the report text are bound up with the same function and the same test; left as-is for the same reason.
  - `activeSectionOneLeadsCurrentSession`/`hasActiveSessionOpening` (diagnostics only, not report-affecting) still read `marketPackages.find(item => item.market === 'US')` directly.
  - Everything the Step 9D plan's Part B §6 already listed as "market-specific, not state-specific": the active-session anchor checks (`con` ~471/666), Further Readings resolution and Yahoo-only eligibility (`con` ~814/834), the US-only future-dated evidence omission (`lib/analysis-package-service.js`), `America/New_York` in the orchestration layer, the SG/HK `LUNCH` market state (would throw at `analysisModeForMarketState`), and the CNBC evidence gate (`lib/evidence-items.js`, untouched per this task's rules).
  - The pre-existing gap above (rules 11-24 never scope-gated) -- fixing it is a product decision (would it change SG/HK's current silent pass-through to active trimming/blanking?), not a refactor, so it stays recorded, not changed.
- **Tests:** new `tests/section-rules-market.test.js` (16 tests).
  - (a) the US settings row and `reportSettings`'s six-state shape are pinned exactly; `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim`, `containsWholeTerm`, `normalizeSectionText` and `namesCitedFocusSubject` are shown to give identical answers with and without an explicit US-settings argument.
  - (b) `marketSettings('SG')`/`marketSettings('HK')` and `reportSettings` for both across nine state values (including garbage ones) are disabled with no throw; a full SG and a full HK report round-trip through `invokeClaudeAnalysis` with a clean writer reply are returned byte-identical with no normalization events; and, for both markets, a writer reply with a duplicate telemetry ref (which rule 5 would silently dedupe if enabled) instead reaches the final checker unchanged and is rejected with `CONTRACT_FAILURE`/`invalid telemetry references` -- the same two-step outcome this input produced before this step, confirmed against `ee7ddb7`.
  - (c) a `FAKE_MARKET_SETTINGS` object, defined only in the test file (never added to `MARKET_SETTINGS`), is passed directly into `hasDirectMarketCausalClaim`, `containsWholeTerm`, `normalizeSectionText` and `namesCitedFocusSubject`: a sentence naming a fake index ("Zeta 30") is recognized as a causal claim only with the fake benchmark names, not the US ones, and vice versa for "S&P 500"; a Turkish locale's dotless-ı case-folding makes "INDEX"/"index" match under `en-US` but not under `tr-TR`, proving the locale argument is genuinely used, not merely accepted. A last test confirms `marketSettings('FAKE')` is disabled, i.e. the fake row was never added to production code.
- **Evidence:** full suite 935/935 (919 before this step + 16 new). Syntax 48/48 on `api/` and `lib/`. `git diff --check` clean. The Step 9D.1 matrix, 9D.2 settings, 9D.3 ladder, 9D.4 causal, 9D.5a matrix-extra and registry, and 9D.5b order tests all pass unedited (73 tests across those seven files), including the writer-request-size guard (19,700 / 31,893 bytes). No existing test file changed. No paid calls.
- **Free real-reply comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `ee7ddb7` (built with read-only `git show`) against the new code, with a stand-in writer, exactly as the Step 9D.5b comparison was run.
  - All 22 saved replies in the Replay Packages folder, matched to their package by `generatedAt` (5 packages), plus 500 variants built from each package's own saved sentences and references: 0 differences.
  - 2,400 random broken replies across all six US states: 0 differences (2,224 SUCCESS, 176 CONTRACT_FAILURE in both trees, outcome-for-outcome).
  - Total 2,922 cases, 0 differences.
- **Pending:** Kevin's commit decision. Step 9M (enabling SG/HK) is not started; see "Could not share / blocks SG and HK" above for what it still needs.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 9F.1c — Admit active-session Yahoo articles by reading window and publish time only (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f1-reading-window · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule:** news inside the reading window is relevant news. The window runs from the trigger back to the last close, then 24 more hours (`READING_EXTENSION_HOURS`, clamped 0-72, Step 9F.1a). An article is in or out by its publish time only.
- **Decision:**
  1. In PRE, REGULAR and POST, `readYahooNews` (`lib/yahoo-news-reading.js`) admits a Yahoo article only when its `datePublished` is inside `readingWindow({market: 'US', triggerAt: acquisitionStartedAt})`, both ends inclusive. Audit decisions `REJECTED_BEFORE_READING_WINDOW` / `REJECTED_AFTER_READING_WINDOW` replace `REJECTED_BEFORE_WINDOW` / `REJECTED_AFTER_WINDOW`; the existing counters (`beforeSessionWindowCount`, `afterSessionWindowCount`, `activeWindowRejectionCount`) count them. The redundant second session-window check (`REJECTED_OTHER`) is removed. The updated time was never part of Yahoo admission and still is not.
  2. **Supersedes Step 8K.5 item 3.** The page label no longer skips any candidate. `activeYahooStaleByLabel`, now measured against the reading window start plus the same 4-hour tolerance, only moves a candidate to the back of the try order (rank order kept within each group). Its audit entry gets `labelOld: true`; `staleByLabelCount` now counts those. `SKIPPED_STALE_BY_LABEL` is no longer produced.
  3. Unchanged: no publish time is still `REJECTED_NO_PUBLICATION_TIME` after the download (one attempt used); an unavailable session window (including `BENCHMARK_OVERLAY_INVALID`) still rejects as `REJECTED_WINDOW_UNAVAILABLE`; caps 12 attempts / 6 admitted / 4 s / 8 KB; the session window's other jobs (current-session labels, Section 2, Further Readings, causality) and the CNBC path and its horizon functions.
  4. The candidate-audit event header adds `readingWindowStartsAt`; the 3400-byte / 10-candidate splitter is unchanged.
- **Known consequence, deferred to Step 9F.4:** an article admitted from the extra 24 hours still enters the classifier as `CURRENT_SESSION` evidence, but is outside the session window that `currentSessionEvidenceContext` uses, so it is not listed as current-session evidence for the writer. Evidence labelling is Step 9F.4.
- **Run time:** the 12-attempt cap still bounds downloads. Label-old candidates used no attempt before; now they can, so a run can make up to 12 more downloads than before in the extreme case where every candidate was label-skipped, at most 12 x 4 s = 48 s extra. The worst case for the whole stage is unchanged (12 x 4 s plus discovery).
- **Tests:** full suite 960/960 (956 before; 4 new: a PRE/REGULAR/POST table with articles at, just inside and just outside the reading window start, one before the old session start, a future one, one with no publish time, one the label used to skip and one with a wrong old label; and a clamped-extension test). Five existing tests were updated to the new rule: the stale/future Yahoo test and the decision-type test now place their "before" article before the reading window start and expect the new decision names; the three Step 8K.5 label tests now expect tried-last ordering instead of a skip.
- **Free comparison (scratch only):** `7b84462` (via read-only `git show`) against the new code, the 9F.1b stand-in pages, all eight saved packages. 107 article decisions. Differences: 8 label-skipped stand-ins now downloaded (7 admitted, 1 left at the 6-admitted cap), 3 articles published in the 24 hours before the old window start now admitted. One extra synthetic run with two more stand-ins showed one knock-on: an extra download of a label-skipped article used the last of the 12 attempts, so a later candidate that was admitted before was not tried. The `PRE-nomaterial` package, which failed assembly before for lack of current news, now assembles with 2 admitted articles.
- **Must not change:** as Step 8K.5 (CNBC completed-session path, anchors and V1 replay, Neon storage, the 6-admitted cap, tier 4a dropping); plus the caps above and every `?rev=` token.
- **Supersedes:** Step 8K.5 item 3 (stale-by-label skip).

## Step 9F.1d — Up to 3 download attempts per Yahoo article, and a 30 second reading budget (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f1-reading-window · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule:** a one-off download glitch should not lose a good article, so a failed download is retried, up to 3 attempts in total. A time budget stops a bad Yahoo day from pushing the run toward the 300 s function limit (typical run about 61 s).
- **Decision:**
  1. One shared helper, `downloadWithRetries` in `lib/reading-window.js`, runs a download up to `DOWNLOAD_ATTEMPTS` (3, fixed in code since Step 9F.1a) times, pausing 0.25 s then 0.75 s (`DOWNLOAD_RETRY_PAUSES_MS`, fixed). `isTemporaryDownloadFailure` retries only `TIMEOUT`, `RETRIEVAL_FAILURE`, `RESPONSE_READ_FAILURE`, and `HTTP_FAILURE` with status 429 or 5xx. Everything else is final after one attempt: 404 and any other status, an `HTTP_FAILURE` with no status, an unreadable page (`NO_USABLE_ARTICLE`), a wrong page identity, the wrong content type, an oversized page, bad input. A thrown error is returned after one attempt, not retried (as before, it is audited as `FETCH_FAILED`/`EXCEPTION`).
  2. `lib/yahoo-current-news-article-content-acquisition.js` now puts `httpStatus` on its `HTTP_FAILURE` result so the helper can tell 429/5xx from 404. No other fetcher change; the 4 s timeout and 8 KB article size are unchanged.
  3. Reading budget: `readingBudgetSeconds: 30` is in the settings table (`READING_SETTINGS`, `lib/section-rules.js`) next to the other reading settings, fixed in code (no environment override), and returned by `readingSettings`. `createReadingBudget` starts it when `readYahooNews` begins its article downloads. Once spent, no new article download starts and no retry starts; a retry is also not started when less budget is left than its pause. A download already running is never cut short; the fetcher's own 4 s timeout bounds it. The clock is the orchestration's injected `monotonicNow`; pauses use an injectable `sleep` (new optional orchestration option, default a real timer), so tests use fake time.
  4. Retries do not count against the 12-article cap (`articleFetchAttemptCount` still counts articles tried). The 12-attempt and 6-admitted caps, the admission rules (Step 9F.1c) and the try order are unchanged.
  5. Audit: each tried article's audit entry gets `attempts` (1-3). An article whose retry the budget stopped gets `retriesStoppedByBudget: true`; articles never tried because the budget ran out get the new decision `SKIPPED_READING_BUDGET` (the caps still take precedence: `SKIPPED_MAX_ADMITTED`, then `SKIPPED_MAX_ATTEMPTS`). The `activeYahooAcquisition` summary event adds `downloadAttemptCount` (downloads including retries) and `readingBudgetStopped`. The 3400-byte / 10-candidate audit splitter is unchanged and absorbs the extra field.
- **Run time:** without the budget, retries could have taken the article stage to 12 x (3 x 4 s + 1 s of pauses) = 156 s. With it, no download or retry starts after 30 s and the last one can run up to its 4 s timeout, so the article stage is bounded at about 34 s, below the old worst case of 12 x 4 s = 48 s. Trade-off: on a slow but working day (say 3 s per article) the budget now stops after about 10 articles where 9F.1c would have tried all 12.
- **Recap:** the helper is ready for the Yahoo recap download (`lib/yahoo-recap-article-content-acquisition.js`) but not wired. Wiring needs its `HTTP_FAILURE` result to carry the status it already records for diagnostics, and its own budget instance on the completed-session path. Left for a later step.
- **Could not share:** nothing in the retry or budget logic is copied per state; PRE, REGULAR and POST all run through `readYahooNews`. The mapping from a final download result to audit decisions and counters stays in `readYahooNews`, because it is specific to the Yahoo news audit. CNBC acquisition and its timing functions are untouched by rule.
- **Tests:** full suite 985/985 (960 before; 25 new). New `tests/download-retries.test.js` (17: a table of fails-once, fails-twice, fails-three-times, 429, 503, 404, no status, unreadable page, wrong identity, wrong content type, oversized page, bad input and first-time success; fixed constants; a thrown error; budget rows where the budget stops before a retry; budget spent at exactly 30 s). Orchestration (7): a table through the real reading loop (fails once, twice, three times, 404, unreadable page) checking audit attempts, pauses and summary counts; retries not using up the 12-article cap (13 candidates each failing once: 12 articles tried, 24 downloads, the 13th `SKIPPED_MAX_ATTEMPTS`); the budget running out partway with fake time (articles 1-2 three attempts each, article 3 one attempt with its retry stopped, articles 4-5 `SKIPPED_READING_BUDGET`). Fetcher (1): 404/429/503 carry their status. Updated: the Step 8K decision-type test (its timeout article is now downloaded 3 times, so it checks 12 distinct articles and 14 downloads), the reading-settings test and the two settings-table tests (new `readingBudgetSeconds`). Syntax 50/50. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; no real waiting in tests.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `644b3d3` (via read-only `git show`) against the new code, the 9F.1c stand-in pages with the failing stand-ins turned into successes, all eight saved packages plus the 9F.1c extended run (9 runs). Each old run was done twice to rule out run-to-run noise (none). Package, audit entries, counts and download order are identical; the only differences are the new `attempts: 1` on every tried audit entry and the two new summary fields (`downloadAttemptCount` equal to the articles tried, `readingBudgetStopped: false`). With the original failing stand-ins, the only change is the timeout stand-in being downloaded 3 times; the packages and decisions are unchanged.
- **Must not change:** as Step 9F.1c; plus the 3 attempts, the 0.25 s / 0.75 s pauses, the 30 s budget and the retry-only-temporary-failures rule, CNBC acquisition and its timing functions, Neon storage, and every `?rev=` token.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 9F.1e — Yahoo news reading in CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f1-reading-window · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule:** the Yahoo news list runs in every market state with the same reading window (trigger back to the last close, plus 24 hours; publish time is the only in-or-out test). Until now CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY did not read it; they used only the Yahoo recap and CNBC.
- **Decision:**
  1. The completed-state gate (`activeYahooAcquisition` `SKIPPED_COMPLETED_SESSION`) is removed. `acquireAnalysisMaterial` calls `readYahooNews` once for every state, after the Yahoo recap, CNBC recap and CNBC news research, so neither waits on it. Same function, window, 12-attempt / 6-admitted caps, 3 download attempts and 30 s budget as PRE/REGULAR/POST. In active states the call order, packages and diagnostics are unchanged (the `completedSessionResearch` `SKIPPED_ACTIVE_SESSION` event is still emitted right after the reading).
  2. After-close articles use the existing completed-state horizons from `deriveCnbcResearchHorizons`: published after the canonical completed close (`COMPLETED_SESSION.endsAtInclusive`) is `SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT`, at or before it `COMPLETED_SESSION`. The references go into the existing `subsequentDevelopments` and `supportingEvidence` lists, the same way general CNBC news is split (`completedSessionCnbcReferences` / `subsequentCnbcReferences`), and the classifier receives the same horizon. No new label. `horizonForArticle` itself is not used because it also rejects by update time and by the previous close; the reading window's publish-time rule decides in or out. With no canonical horizons the article is rejected as `REJECTED_WINDOW_UNAVAILABLE`.
  3. Order: completed-state Yahoo news is appended after all existing evidence, including retained CNBC news (`baseEvidenceItems`, retained CNBC, then Yahoo news), so no existing reference moves. In the classifier input it follows the admitted CNBC news; its provisional references are remapped after CNBC retention. It is broad-market news like CNBC news (`requiresBroadMarketSubjects`), and it is kept whatever its materiality, as in active states. A final classifier size check (`completedYahooEvidenceHandoff`) mirrors `activeYahooEvidenceHandoff`.
  4. Duplicates: `readYahooNews` now treats an article whose link is already in the evidence as `REJECTED_DUPLICATE` (in completed states the Yahoo recap can also be on the news list). In active states that evidence is only quote pages and Fed releases, so nothing changes there.
  5. Audit: the existing `activeYahooAcquisition` summary and `activeYahooCandidateAudit` events (same 3400-byte / 10-candidate splitter) now appear in completed runs. Completed runs add `subsequentDevelopmentCount` to the summary and `afterClose: true` on admitted after-close audit entries. The window fields stay null (no session window); `readingWindowStartsAt` is set.
  6. Unchanged by rule: CNBC acquisition and its timing functions, the Yahoo recap path (still completed-states only), Further Readings (still the package list of recap and CNBC references; completed-state Yahoo news is not added), My Stocks/Watchlist references, article size (9F.1f), report labels (9F.4), recap acceptance (9F.1g), Neon storage and every `?rev=` token.
- **Run time:** a completed run can now take up to about 44 s longer in the worst case: the Most Active page (4 s timeout), then the latest-news pages (Singapore 4 s and US 6 s, in parallel, so 6 s), then the article stage (no download starts after 30 s, and the last runs up to its 4 s timeout, so about 34 s). A normal day adds the two list pages plus a few seconds of downloads.
- **Could not share:** the final hand-off into the package. Active and completed states build different evidence lists (active Yahoo news sits before CNBC and is `CURRENT_SESSION`; completed Yahoo news must come after CNBC so existing references do not move), so the classifier size check after acquisition is a separate loop for each. The reading itself, its caps, retries, budget, window, duplicate check and audit are shared.
- **Tests:** full suite 987/987 (985 before; 2 new): a table across CLOSED (night), CLOSED (pre-dawn), WEEKEND and HOLIDAY with articles exactly at and one second before the reading window start, before, at and after the close and late in the evening, checking decisions, `afterClose`, appended order, horizons, `supportingEvidence`/`subsequentDevelopments`, every other evidence-context list, the classifier input prefix, and identical recap and CNBC calls and diagnostics against a run with no Yahoo news; and a recap-listed-as-news duplicate test. Three tests that pinned the old skip were updated: the CLOSED/WEEKEND/HOLIDAY test now expects the reading to run, the Step 8K completed-audit test now expects the audit event, and the recap sanitization test ignores the `articleBodyUnavailableCount` field name (not a leaked value). Syntax 50/50. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `4c085cb` (via read-only `git show`) against the new code. Completed path, end to end: the saved `CLOSED-2026-09-29`, `WEEKEND-2026-09-27` and `WEEKEND-2026-10-03-1600` packages with stand-ins that replay their own telemetry, market data, Fed releases, Yahoo recap, CNBC recap and CNBC news, and the package's own roles by link. The old code reproduces each saved package's evidence exactly (33, 34, 34 items). Pass on all three: old evidence an unchanged prefix, recap and CNBC evidence, calls and diagnostics identical, every other list unchanged, and the only new items are 5 stand-in Yahoo news articles per package (2 before the close in `supportingEvidence`, 3 after it in `subsequentDevelopments`). The stand-ins also covered one second before the window (rejected), no publish time (rejected), a timeout then success (admitted on attempt 2), a permanent timeout (3 attempts, failed), the recap on the news list (duplicate) and a dropped-tier headline. Active path: the Step 9F.1d script on all eight saved packages plus its extended run, with and without failing stand-ins: no differences.
- **Must not change:** as Step 9F.1d; plus the after-close split by the canonical completed close, Yahoo news appended after all existing completed-state evidence, and the recap and CNBC paths untouched.
- **Supersedes:** the completed-state skip of the Yahoo news reading (Step 8K `SKIPPED_COMPLETED_SESSION`).

## Step 9F.1f — Yahoo article read size 16 KB; the classifier reads the first 8 KB (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f1-reading-window · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule:** read each Yahoo article up to 16 KB so more of it is available, but send the classifier only the first 8 KB, so its 64 KB request still holds the six admitted articles. Cost per run must not double.
- **Finding before the build:** until now a Yahoo article (news list or recap) longer than 8 KB was not cut to 8 KB. It was rejected whole (`ARTICLE_TEXT_TOO_LARGE`, audited as `EXTRACTION_FAILED`). Kevin chose: keep the first 16 KB of an article of any length.
- **Decision:**
  1. Shared helpers in `lib/reading-window.js`: `yahooArticleBounds` (text = `articleKb` x 1024 from the settings table, `ARTICLE_KB` in Vercel, default 16, clamped 2-32; result cap = text + 4 KB, the same 4 KB headroom as the old 8 KB / 12 KB pair), `firstBytes` (the first N bytes, cut back to a whole character, trailing space removed, so it is always a prefix), `classifierArticleText` (`CLASSIFIER_ARTICLE_BYTES` = 8 KB, fixed in code).
  2. Yahoo news reader (`lib/yahoo-current-news-article-content-acquisition.js`): default text and result caps come from `yahooArticleBounds`; a longer article is kept with its first 16 KB instead of being rejected. Yahoo recap: `lib/analysis-package-runtime.js` builds its article, evidence and result caps from the same setting (`yahooRecapPackageBounds`); the recap reader cuts the body to make room and keeps the CPI comparison excerpts whole at the end. The runtime passes the same bounds to the news reader. Text that already fits is unchanged in both.
  3. The classifier cut happens in exactly one place: `projectClaudeEvidenceRoleClassificationInput` (`lib/claude-model-input-projection.js`) replaces each item's summary with its first 8 KB. Every classifier size check builds the request through it, so the reading loop's check, both hand-off checks and the final request all measure the cut text. A classifier subject is grounded only in that first 8 KB (`canonicalSubjects`).
  4. Subject repair: before this step it did not cut at 8 KB; it refused the whole repair batch when any article was over 8 KB (`MAX_SUBJECT_REPAIR_SUMMARY_BYTES`, now removed), so one long article would have cost every repair. Now `canonicalSubjectRepairInput` cuts each article to the same first 8 KB, and the repair is sent and grounded on that text, like the classifier.
  5. The writer still receives the full stored text (up to 16 KB per article), subject to the temporary writer budget below.
  6. Unchanged: the 12-article / 6-admitted caps, 3 attempts, 30 s budget, CNBC acquisition and its size caps, Neon storage, every `?rev=` token, the 64 KB classifier cap, the 128 KB writer cap.
- **Writer input (measured on the 8 saved packages, `buildClaudeAnalysisRequest`):** today 89,776-113,509 bytes against the 131,072-byte cap. With every Yahoo news article grown to 16 KB: 111,778-197,122 bytes; 5 of 8 packages go over the cap (the six-article PRE-8K5 package 197,122; three-article packages 133,361-145,729). At 8 KB the six-article package is already 148,028. With the writer budget below, every package fits (PRE-8K5: six articles trimmed to about 5.4 KB each; POST: three to about 11.5 KB). The writer request-size guard test (system 19,700 / request 31,893 bytes) is unaffected: its fixture is under the cap, so nothing is trimmed and the bytes are identical.
- **Classifier (measured):** with all-long articles the first 8 KB of five fit, but the sixth takes the request to about 65.7 KB, just over 64 KB; it is rejected as `REJECTED_CLASSIFIER_PREFLIGHT` and counted in `classifierPreflightRejectedCount` (flagged, not silent). The same was true of 8 KB articles before; it was not seen because long articles were rejected. A classifier cut near 7.5 KB would fit six on these packages; not changed (fixed at 8 KB by Kevin's rule).
- **Safe top of the article size range:** 32 KB can never fit six articles in the writer without trimming (six times 32 KB of text alone is 192 KB, over 128 KB). Without trimming, the room is about 5.5 KB per article with six articles, so no size of 8 KB or more is safe for six full articles. With the writer budget, any size in 2-32 KB runs without failing on size, but in a six-long-article run anything above about 16 KB is read and then trimmed away. Recommended safe top until Step 9F.2: 16 KB. The range was not changed.
- **Run time and cost:** the reader already downloads the whole page (up to 1.5 MB) before extracting; the read size only decides how much text is kept, so there is no extra download time. More long articles are now admitted, so the 6-admitted cap can be reached with fewer downloads. The writer budget search adds up to about 3 s when it trims (measured 2.8 s on the six-article package, 1.3 s on a three-article one) and nothing when the request fits. Writer and classifier both run Claude Haiku 4.5 ($1 per million input tokens). Writer input is bounded by its 131,072-byte cap: today 22,000-28,000 tokens, at most about 33,000, so at most about 5,000-11,000 extra tokens (+$0.005-0.011) per run. Classifier input is bounded by its 64 KB cap: today about 6,000-10,000 tokens, at most about 16,000 (+$0.006-0.010). Worst case about +$0.02 per run; a run whose articles are all under 8 KB is unchanged. Not a doubling.
- **Could not share:** the recap's CPI-excerpt handling (`recapArticleText`) is recap-specific (only the recap has excerpts); it uses the shared `firstBytes`. Everything else is shared: one setting, one cut function, one classifier cut, one budget search.
- **Tests:** full suite 1011/1011 (987 before; 24 new). New `tests/article-size.test.js` (17): a table for `ARTICLE_KB` 1, 2, 16, 32, 40 (2, 2, 16, 32, 32 KB) driving both the news reader and the recap reader on a 40 KB page and the recap evidence construction; an article within the size unchanged; character-boundary cuts; the classifier request from a 16 KB article identical to one built from its first 8 KB; classifier and repair grounding only in the first 8 KB; the repair accepting and cutting a 16 KB article; six rows for the budget search. Orchestration (2): six 16 KB articles all admitted with the classifier reading only the first 8 KB of each (the full texts would not fit) and the package keeping 16 KB; a sixth article that would exceed the classifier cap flagged as `REJECTED_CLASSIFIER_PREFLIGHT`. Writer (5): see the next entry. Updated to the new rule: the news and recap "too large" tests (now keep the first bytes), the recap CPI test (body cut, excerpt kept), the runtime bounds test (16,384 / 20,480), the classifier oversize test (now nine 8 KB articles, since one huge article is cut), and the writer oversize test (now also expects the budget's `DOES_NOT_FIT_AT_FLOOR` event first). Syntax 50/50. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `d915a5c` (via read-only `git show`) against the new code. Stand-in pages go through each tree's real Yahoo news reader and real recap reader; all eight saved packages, three page sets each. 0 failures.
  - Short pages (the packages' own articles, plus stand-ins of 3-6 KB): packages, classifier requests and writer requests identical, old and new.
  - Long pages (the packages' own articles extended to 20 KB, plus stand-ins of 17-24 KB): the old code admitted none (every one rejected as too large; the active runs failed assembly for lack of current news); the new code admitted 4-5 per package. Every new text is a prefix of its page; the old classifier request builder fed the first 8 KB gives exactly the new request. Classifier request 48,406-57,401 bytes; writer 137,948-165,585 bytes before the budget, 131,064-131,072 after.
  - Mixed pages (own articles plus stand-ins of 3-20 KB): long articles now admitted ahead of later short ones, so in some packages the 6-admitted cap or the classifier check is reached sooner and a short article the old code admitted is no longer reached (knock-on, expected). Classifier 31,596-52,670 bytes old to new; writer 80,998-100,585 old, 98,786-131,072 new.
  - Recap: the three saved recaps are identical old and new; extended past 16 KB, old rejected them, new keeps the first 16 KB.
- **Must not change:** as Step 9F.1e; plus the 8 KB classifier cut in one place, keeping (not rejecting) long Yahoo articles, and CNBC untouched.
- **Supersedes:** the 8 KB Yahoo article cap and its reject-when-longer rule (Steps 8K / 9F.1c caps list).

## Step 9F.1f (writer budget) — TEMPORARY until Step 9F.2: trim Yahoo articles to fit the writer cap (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f1-reading-window · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. **Temporary guard; remove or replace when Step 9F.2 raises the limits and ranks all sources.**
- **Why:** with 16 KB articles the writer request goes over its 131,072-byte cap on most saved packages, and an over-cap request fails with no report (413).
- **Kevin's rule:** keep every admitted article (never drop one to save space); before the writer call, if the request is over today's cap, trim Yahoo articles only, longest first, to a floor of 4 KB each; CNBC counts toward the size but is never trimmed here; if it still does not fit with every Yahoo article at the floor, flag it clearly and do not fail silently; audit which articles were trimmed and by how much.
- **Decision:**
  1. One shared search, `fitArticlesToBudget` (`lib/reading-window.js`): finds the highest common length that fits; every article longer than it is cut to it (longest first by construction), none below max(4 KB, its own minimum), none dropped. `WRITER_ARTICLE_FLOOR_BYTES` = 4 KB, fixed.
  2. `fitWriterArticleBudget` (`lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`) runs at the start of every writer attempt (so the Step 8L retry is identical). Trimmable: evidence items with `sourceId` `us.yahoo-finance` and category `news` (news list and recap). Each article's minimum is the shortest prefix that still contains every broad-market focus subject the package grounds in it, so the trimmed package always passes the input check. The size measured is the real writer request. Under the cap the input is returned unchanged and nothing is logged.
  3. Audit event `writerArticleBudget`: `outcome` (`TRIMMED` or `DOES_NOT_FIT_AT_FLOOR`), `limitBytes`, `floorBytes`, `requestBytesBefore`, `requestBytesAfter`, `yahooArticleCount`, `trimmedArticleCount`, and `trimmed` (evidence ref, from bytes, to bytes; no text). When even the floor does not fit, the articles are left at the floor and the existing `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` path follows (no writer call), after the flag.
- **Tests (5, `tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`):** under the cap nothing changes and no event; over the cap the three longest of six Yahoo articles are cut to a common length, shorter ones and the Reuters item untouched, every text a prefix of the original, the event's bytes exact and free of article text; a focus subject at 10 KB is never cut off and the trimmed input is valid; a request that cannot fit at the floor is flagged and the writer is not called; an over-cap request is sent under the cap.
- **Measured:** see Step 9F.1f above (all eight saved packages fit after trimming; search time up to about 3 s when it trims).
- **Must not change (until 9F.2):** no article dropped for size; CNBC never trimmed here; 4 KB floor; flagged when it cannot fit.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 9F.1g — Yahoo recap accepted by its content, not its address; recap downloads retried (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f1-reading-window · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule:** a good Yahoo recap must not be lost because Yahoo changes its address style. A page is the recap because of what it says. Publish time is the only time test. The recap downloads get the same 3-attempt retries as the news articles (parked in Step 9F.1d).
- **Findings before the build (Kevin decided both):**
  1. There was no "index close wording" check on the recap. The only page check (`lib/yahoo-recap-session-validation.js`) tested the article type, the publish date in New York time, and that the page's own address matched. Kevin chose a new close check (item 3 below).
  2. The paid web search step threw away every result not on the `stock-market-today` pattern before any download, so changing only the later steps would have had no live effect. Kevin chose to relax only that filter, with protections (item 4).
- **Decision:**
  1. One shared module, `lib/yahoo-recap-acceptance.js`, holds the recap rules. Address: https on `finance.yahoo.com` only, no credentials or other port, any path (`canonicalYahooRecapUrl`). It replaces four copies of the address pattern in discovery, the page check, the article reader and the evidence builder. The old pattern is kept only as `isOldStyleRecapAddress`, which decides which candidates are tried first.
  2. Acceptance, in the page check (`extractMetadata`): an `Article`, `NewsArticle` or `LiveBlogPosting` node, published on the target session date in the exchange's time zone (`America/New_York`, `exchangeDate`), whose page address and declared address are the requested one, and whose body passes the close check. Not the recap: `NOT_VALIDATED` with a `reason`: `WRONG_HOST`, `PAGE_ADDRESS_MISMATCH`, `NO_STRUCTURED_DATA`, `NOT_ARTICLE_TYPE`, `WRONG_PUBLISH_DATE` or `FAILED_CLOSE_CHECK` (the furthest check reached). A redirect to another page keeps its `INVALID_RESPONSE` failure and now carries `WRONG_HOST` or `PAGE_ADDRESS_MISMATCH`.
  3. Close check (`reportsIndexClose`, new): the body (normalized exactly as the reader stores it; `normalizePlainText` moved unchanged into the shared module) must report a move for at least two of the S&P 500, the Dow and the Nasdaq. Each must be named in a sentence that also has a move word (closed, ended, rose, fell, gained, declined, ...) and a size (a percentage, "percent" or points). The article reader does not repeat the check. It re-reads the same page, which must still match by link, publish time and headline.
  4. Discovery (`lib/claude-bounded-news-discovery.js`): the request, prompt, model, search count and cost are unchanged. `PATH_MISMATCH` is gone, so any https `finance.yahoo.com` result is a candidate; `HOST_MISMATCH`, `INVALID_URL`, `INVALID_TITLE` and `DUPLICATE` stay. Each result diagnostic adds `oldAddressStyle`. The research runtime orders candidates old style first (in search order), then the others (in search order), and checks at most 3 (`YAHOO_RECAP_MAX_CANDIDATES`, `orderRecapCandidates`). With three or more old-style candidates this is exactly the old choice. Others are tried only when no old-style candidate passes.
  5. Update time never decides. The page check records a readable update time at or after publication, otherwise null. It still prefers a node whose update time is absent or in order, as before. The article reader no longer rejects an unreadable, conflicting, reversed or older-than-checked update time; it records one readable in-order value or null. The orchestration's match (`yahooRecapArticleMatchesResearch`) no longer compares update times. The horizon (`horizonForArticle`, recap-only) and the evidence builder's horizon check use the publish time only.
  6. Retries: the page check and the article download both run through the shared `downloadWithRetries` (3 attempts, 0.25 s / 0.75 s pauses, temporary failures only). Both fetchers now put `httpStatus` on `HTTP_FAILURE`, so 429/5xx are retried and 404 is tried once. A thrown error is still not retried. A candidate whose download fails after its retries still ends the recap search, as before.
  7. Budget: the recap has **its own** reading budget, with the same 30 s setting (`readingBudgetSeconds`). It is created in `acquireAnalysisMaterial` when the recap stage starts and shared by the cache re-check, the candidate checks and the article download. It is not shared with the Yahoo news reading because that reading runs later, after CNBC, and has its own budget (Step 9F.1e). One shared instance would let a slow recap cut the news reading short. Once the budget is spent, no further candidate and no retry starts. The first candidate's check and the article's first attempt always run.
  8. Audit: each `yahooRecapSessionCandidateValidation` event adds `oldAddressStyle`, `attempts` and `rejectionReason`. A new `yahooRecapCandidateAudit` event (one per search, at most 3400 bytes, the Yahoo news audit limit; oversize entries are counted in `omittedEntryCount`) gives `chosenRank`, `chosenSlug`, and per discovered candidate: rank, slug, old style, attempts, decision (`CHOSEN`, `NOT_THE_RECAP` with reason, `DOWNLOAD_FAILED`, `NOT_CHECKED_RECAP_ALREADY_CHOSEN`, `NOT_CHECKED_CANDIDATE_LIMIT`, `NOT_CHECKED_AFTER_FAILURE`, `SKIPPED_READING_BUDGET`). A new `yahooRecapArticleDownload` event gives the article's `attempts` and `retriesStoppedByBudget`. No article text or full link in any of them.
  9. Unchanged by rule: CNBC acquisition and the shared timing functions it uses (`deriveCnbcResearchHorizons`, CNBC horizon checks), the recap's 16 KB size and CPI-excerpt handling (Step 9F.1f), the recap staying completed-states only, Neon storage, every `?rev=` token, the 4 s page timeout and 1.5 MB page cap.
- **Run time:** a normal day adds nothing (one page check and one article download). Worst case, the old recap stage made at most 4 downloads of up to 4 s (3 page checks and the article), 16 s after the web search. Now no download or retry starts after 30 s from the start of the recap stage, so the recap's downloads end by about 38 s (the last page check started before 30 s runs up to 4 s, then the article's first attempt up to 4 s). Worst case about 22 s more than before. If the web search itself runs past 30 s, the stage behaves as before with one page check and one article attempt (no retries).
- **Could not share:** the recap budget is its own instance (item 7); the setting, helper and retry rules are shared. The audit entry shape and the decision names are recap-specific, because the recap chooses one page among at most 3 rather than admitting up to 6. The CPI-excerpt handling stays recap-specific (Step 9F.1f). Everything about what makes a page the recap is in one module and used once per step; there are no per-state copies (the recap runs only in CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY through one path).
- **Tests:** full suite 1022/1022 (1011 before; 11 new). New `tests/yahoo-recap-acceptance.test.js` (9): a page-check table (old-pattern address accepted; a new-style address with the right content accepted; a page declaring another host `WRONG_HOST`; `WebPage` `NOT_ARTICLE_TYPE`; wrong publish date; a good update time with a wrong publish date rejected; New York date not UTC; a body failing the close check; an unreadable update time accepted); wrong hosts refused before download; the address rule; a close-check table; candidate order and the 3-candidate cap; old-style chosen ahead of a better-ranked new-style page, new-style chosen only when the old-style page fails; the full audit event; a retry table through the real runtime (fails once then succeeds: 2 attempts, pause 0.25 s; fails twice then succeeds: 3 attempts, 0.25 + 0.75 s; fails three times; 404: one attempt, no pause); the budget stopping a second candidate. Orchestration (2): a new-style recap through the real discovery, page check, reader and evidence builder into Further Readings; an article-download retry table (fails once, fails twice, 404 once) with the new event and gaps. Updated to the new rule: page-check fixtures now carry a body with the index moves; tests that pinned the address pattern, `PATH_MISMATCH`, update-time rejections (`INVALID_DATE_MODIFIED_FORMAT`, `DATE_MODIFIED_CONFLICT`, `DATE_MODIFIED_MISMATCH`, `DATE_MODIFIED_BEFORE_PUBLISHED`, update outside the horizon, removed update time) or a single page attempt on 503 now expect acceptance or 3 attempts. Syntax 51/51. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; no real waiting in tests.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `21ba40f` (via read-only `git show` into a scratch folder) against the new code. Each tree's real discovery parsing, page check, article reader, match and horizon rules (taken from its own orchestration) and evidence builder, with stand-in responses, no network. All eight saved packages: the three with a Yahoo recap (`CLOSED-2026-09-29`, `WEEKEND-2026-09-27`, `WEEKEND-2026-10-03-1600`), 13 scenarios each (39 runs). The five active-state packages have no recap.
  - Saved recaps at their saved address (no update time, updated 15 min later, update before publication), alone or ranked with a better-ranked new-style copy or single-stock story: 15 of 15 accepted old and new, same recap, identical text and horizon. Every saved report still picks the same recap.
  - New acceptances (12): the saved recap re-hosted at a new-style address (3, text identical to the saved one; old lost it at discovery as `PATH_MISMATCH`); an unreadable update time (3, old `NOT_VALIDATED`); published before the close and updated after it (3, now `COMPLETED_SESSION` by publish time; old `HORIZON_MISMATCH`); updated after the report time (3, old `HORIZON_MISMATCH`).
  - New rejections (3, stand-ins only): an old-style address whose body is a single-stock story fails the close check. Different recap picked (3, stand-ins only): that page ranked above a new-style recap; old took the single-stock page, new takes the recap.
  - Both rejected (6): a single-stock story at a new-style address (new: `FAILED_CLOSE_CHECK`), and a new-style recap with the wrong publish date (`WRONG_PUBLISH_DATE`).
- **Known limit:** the close check reads index names, move words and sizes, not tense. A pre-market page saying "Dow and S&P 500 futures rose 0.3%" would pass, as it would have under the old rules. The recap is only read in completed states, after the close.
- **Must not change:** as Step 9F.1f; plus acceptance by content (type, publish date in the exchange time zone, the close check) with host-only addresses, old-style candidates first and at most 3, update time never deciding, the recap's own 30 s budget, and CNBC untouched.
- **Supersedes:** the recap address pattern in discovery, page check, reader and evidence builder; the recap's update-time rejections and the update-time part of its horizon check; the recap's single download attempt.

## Step 9F.2c.1 — Headline word lists: approved terms, drop the blog-style publisher list, whole-word matching (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** this records the 3 October 2026 word-list decisions. A 10 October read-only audit found they had never reached the code: AI/gold/bonds-type headlines still ranked as generic (tier 5 or 3b, not the tier 2 macro bucket), and the Simply Wall St / Motley Fool / Insider Monkey "blog partner, ranked last inside its tier" rule from Step 8K.5 was still in force. Kevin's rule: publishers must not affect ranking, except that wire publishers still earn their tier 2w bucket.
- **Decision:**
  1. The four ranking word lists (tier 2 macro, tier 4a drop, tier 4b Asia/Europe, tier 3a benchmark/generic) and the wire-publisher list move into one shared module, `lib/headline-lists.js`, as named exports (plain term arrays, each entry `{term, caseSensitive}`). `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` imports them and builds the same `wholeWordPattern(...)` arrays it always has; the ranking logic itself (`wholeWordPattern`, `activeYahooCandidateTier`, `selectedActiveYahooCandidates`, the bucket order) is unchanged and stays in the orchestration file. This is a one-for-one move; nothing else in the repo held a copy of these lists.
  2. Tier 2 macro list additions: `AI`, `Artificial Intelligence`, `OpenAI`, `Anthropic`, `Nvidia`, `AMD`, `VIX`, `gold`, `silver`, `rare earths`, `bonds`, `EU`. `AI` and `EU` are case-sensitive (matching how `Fed` and `Dow` already worked); every other new term is case-insensitive.
  3. Tier 4a drop-list additions: `Singaporean`, `Malaysian`, `Indonesian`, `Thailand`, `Thai`, `Vietnam`, `Vietnamese`, `Philippines`, `Filipino`, `Brunei`, `Bruneian`, `Cambodia`, `Cambodian`, `Laos`, `Laotian`, `Myanmar`, `Burmese`, `ASEAN`, case-insensitive. `Singapore`, `Malaysia` and `Indonesia` are unchanged. The existing protection (a tier 1, tier 2 or tier 4b match in the same headline outranks a drop-list match) is untouched — it lives in the tier-decision order, not in the list.
  4. The blog-style partner list (`ACTIVE_YAHOO_BLOG_PARTNERS`: Simply Wall St, Motley Fool, Insider Monkey) and its ranking effect are removed. `selectedActiveYahooCandidates` no longer splits each bucket into a regular group and a blog group; every bucket is now one array in page order. The wire-publisher list and the tier 2w (wire market-wide wrap) bucket are unchanged. Effect on ranking: previously, inside the same tier/sub-tier bucket, a blog-partner headline was always placed after every non-blog headline in that bucket regardless of page position. Now it keeps its normal page position in that bucket, so a blog-partner story that appeared earlier on the page can outrank a non-blog story that appeared later in the same bucket. It still cannot cross tier/sub-tier boundaries, since that ordering is untouched.
  5. Whole-word matching: every existing term already used `wholeWordPattern` (boundary-anchored on non-alphanumeric characters); no existing term used substring matching, so nothing needed to change there. All new terms use the same `wholeWordPattern`, so `gold` does not match `Goldman`, `AI` (case-sensitive) does not match `said` or `AIG`, and `EU` (case-sensitive) does not match `Neutral`. Multi-word terms (`Artificial Intelligence`, `rare earths`) match as phrases (`wholeWordPattern` already collapses internal spaces to `\s+`).
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls):** the pre-change ranking logic (via read-only `git show HEAD:lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`, no branch switch) against the new code, over every headline/title found in the 8 saved `package-*.json` Replay Packages (plus the other Replay Packages JSON files), the orchestration test fixtures, and the headlines listed in this entry's examples — 124 unique headlines, each tried with 6 publisher variants (none, a wire publisher, and the three ex-blog publishers plus one other). 60 of the 744 combinations changed tier, sub-tier or dropped state; every one is explained by a new macro term (`AI`, `Nvidia`, `AMD`, `OpenAI` all appeared in saved/test headlines), a new drop term (`Thai`), or the blog-list removal (confirmed directly: a Simply Wall St headline ahead of a plain Yahoo headline on the page now keeps that page order instead of being pushed behind it). No unexplained change.
- **Tests:** full suite 1025/1025 (1022 before; 2 Step 8K.5 blog-only tests removed, replaced and extended with new Step 9F.2c.1 cases covering the brief's headline table, the whole-word negative cases, the protected-by-macro drop case, and publisher-does-not-affect-ranking). Syntax 52/52 (new `lib/headline-lists.js`). `git diff --check` clean.
- **Could not share:** nothing — every list the active-session Yahoo ranker reads now lives in `lib/headline-lists.js`; there are no per-state copies.
- **Must not change:** the tier structure and bucket order, the drop-list protections, the wire-publisher list and its tier 2w behavior, the ambiguous-ticker list, the active-session caps/retries/sizes (Step 8K.1/8K.5/9F.1d/9F.1f), and CNBC acquisition and its shared timing functions.
- **Supersedes:** the blog-style publisher ranking rule from Step 8K.5 (`ACTIVE_YAHOO_BLOG_PARTNERS` and the regular/blog bucket split in `selectedActiveYahooCandidates`).

## Step 9F.2c.1b — Stop the word "AMD" matching the eye disease (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-10 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** Step 9F.2c.1's free comparison turned up one false hit after the `AMD` macro-term addition went live: "Kodiak Sciences (KOD) Climbs Nearly 3x on Promising Wet AMD Trial" moved from tier 5 to tier 2, because here `AMD` means age-related macular degeneration, not the chipmaker. Real chip stories (e.g. an Nvidia/AMD data-center headline) must keep matching.
- **Decision:**
  1. `lib/headline-lists.js` adds one new named export, `AMD_EXCLUSION_PHRASES`: `Wet AMD`, `Dry AMD`, `age-related macular degeneration`, `macular degeneration`, same `{term, caseSensitive}` shape as the other lists, whole-phrase, case-insensitive. No other word, tier, drop-list rule, cap, retry, size or the CNBC code changed.
  2. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` is the one place the macro list is matched (`activeYahooCandidateTier`). The macro-pattern array now keeps each term alongside its compiled pattern; a new `macroTermMatches` helper suppresses only the `AMD` term's match when the headline also matches one of the exclusion phrases. Every other macro term in the same headline is evaluated as before and still counts — a headline with both "Wet AMD" and "Nvidia" is still macro, through Nvidia.
  3. The exclusion check is keyed to the literal term `AMD` only; no other macro, drop, Asia/Europe or benchmark term is affected.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls):** the pre-change (`7e851cf`, read-only `git show` into a scratch folder outside the repo) macro-match logic against the new code, over 471 unique headline literals found in the orchestration test file (the Step 9F.2c.1 comparison's own 124-headline scratch set, built from saved Replay Packages, was scratch-only and not committed, so is not available to re-run; the test-file literals are the closest available proxy and a superset of every headline this repo currently exercises). Exactly 2 of 471 changed (both `true -> false`): "Kodiak Sciences (KOD) Climbs Nearly 3x on Promising Wet AMD Trial" and "Dry AMD drug wins approval, shares jump" — both explained by the new exclusion. No unexplained change. The other two eye-disease test rows ("Study of age-related macular degeneration shows promise", "Wet AMD trial results, plus Nvidia earnings preview") were not already in the corpus and were new to this step's own tests, confirmed separately via the real pipeline (Step 9F.2c.1b test below).
- **Tests:** full suite 1026/1026 (1025 before; 1 new table-driven test, 7 rows: 3 eye-disease headlines confirmed non-macro, including one that still contains "Nvidia" plus an exclusion phrase so it stays macro through Nvidia, and 3 real chip-story headlines confirmed macro, including multi-term headlines naming AMD alongside Nvidia or Broadcom). Syntax 52/52. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Could not share:** nothing new — the exclusion list lives in the same shared module as every other word list, matched at the same single site.
- **Must not change:** everything listed under Step 9F.2c.1's "Must not change", plus: the exclusion check must stay scoped to the `AMD` term only, and must only suppress that one term's match, never drop the whole headline or suppress any other macro term in the same headline.
- **Supersedes:** nothing — this narrows the Step 9F.2c.1 `AMD` addition's false-positive surface; the addition itself stands.

## Step 9F.2a — One settings table with Admin tags (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule:** every number that is a limit, slot count, point value, time window, retry count or budget lives in one settings table with a default, a safe range and an Admin/Fixed tag, so Step 9G can move the table into Neon and Step 9H can add an Admin page without rebuilding anything. Values already live today keep today's value and today's behaviour; new settings for the Step 9F.2 ranking/budget redesign are registered but not wired into the pipeline.
- **Clarified during planning (10 Oct 2026 decision):** the Yahoo reading budget and the recap budget become Admin-tagged (env-overridable, 10-60 s, default 30 s) instead of Fixed, and download attempts per Yahoo article download also becomes Admin (1-5, default 3). This supersedes the part of Step 9F.1d/9F.1e's "must not change: the 30 s budget" that meant no environment override; the value itself (30 s / 3 attempts) is unchanged by default. The retry pause lengths and the classifier 8 KB cut stay Fixed. The Yahoo recap page's own 4 s timeout and 1.5 MB response cap (Step 9F.1g's separate protection) are moved into the table but kept Fixed, so that protection stands untouched.
- **Decision:**
  1. One new shared module with no per-market or per-state copies, `lib/settings-registry.js`: `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` (every setting's default, safe range, Admin/Fixed tag, env var, description and whether it is wired), `clampSetting` (moved here from `lib/reading-window.js`, one implementation), `clampBooleanSetting` (on/off settings), and the one reader, `settingValue(name, {env})`: applies an environment-variable override for Admin settings only, clamps numeric ones to the safe range, and falls back to the default when the value is missing, not a number, or the setting is Fixed. The module depends on nothing else in the repo, so `lib/section-rules.js` and `lib/reading-window.js` can both read from it with no require cycle.
  2. Existing values wired to read from the table, same default, same behaviour unless the new environment variable is actually set: `readingExtensionHours`/`articleKb` (`lib/section-rules.js` `READING_SETTINGS`, unchanged env vars `READING_EXTENSION_HOURS`/`ARTICLE_KB`); `yahooReadingBudgetSeconds`/`downloadAttempts` (`lib/reading-window.js`, now Admin, env vars `READING_BUDGET_SECONDS`/`DOWNLOAD_ATTEMPTS` -- the names the 9F.1d/9F.1e tests already referenced as ignored, now honoured); `recapReadingBudgetSeconds` (`lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` recap retry options, now its own setting instead of reusing the Yahoo news budget call); `recapMaxCandidates` (`lib/yahoo-recap-acceptance.js` `YAHOO_RECAP_MAX_CANDIDATES`); `yahooMaxArticleAttempts`/`yahooMaxAdmittedArticles`/`yahooStaleLabelToleranceHours` (`lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`); `pageTimeoutMs` (the `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 4000` duplicated across `lib/federal-reserve-monetary-policy-evidence-acquisition.js`, `lib/yahoo-current-news-article-content-acquisition.js`, `lib/yahoo-latest-news-discovery.js` non-US path, `lib/yahoo-market-data-evidence-acquisition.js`, `lib/yahoo-most-active-acquisition.js`); `usListingTimeoutMs`/`usListingMaxResponseBytes`/`usListingMaxCandidates`/`nonUsListingMaxResponseBytes`/`nonUsListingMaxCandidates`/`mergedListingMaxCandidates` (`lib/yahoo-latest-news-discovery.js`); `mostActiveMaxResponseBytes`/`mostActiveMaxCandidates` (`lib/yahoo-most-active-acquisition.js`); `writerRequestLimitBytes`/`classifierRequestLimitBytes` (`lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`/`lib/claude-evidence-role-classification.js`); `yahooRecapPageTimeoutMs`/`yahooRecapMaxResponseBytes` (`lib/analysis-package-runtime.js`, Fixed, per the protection above). `downloadWithRetries` (`lib/reading-window.js`) gained an optional `attempts` parameter (default still `DOWNLOAD_ATTEMPTS` = 3) so every untouched caller is unaffected; the Yahoo news reading (`lib/yahoo-news-reading.js`) and Yahoo recap (`lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`) call sites now pass the live `downloadAttempts` value so an override actually takes effect end to end. The fixed 2-pause retry list (`DOWNLOAD_RETRY_PAUSES_MS`) reuses its last pause for any attempt past 3, since attempts is now Admin up to 5.
  3. `lib/claude-news-materiality-selection.js`'s own 64 KB request cap is CNBC-related (feeds `lib/claude-bounded-cnbc-market-news-discovery.js`) and is left untouched, registered only (`cnbcMaterialityRequestLimitBytes`).
  4. New, unwired settings registered for the Step 9F.2 ranking/budget redesign: planned wider budgets, slots, spillover, per-source switches, ranking points and recency bonuses/thresholds (full list in the register below). None of these is read by any pipeline code in this step. A pure cross-check function, `checkArticleBudgetFits({articleTextBudgetBytes, nonArticleOverheadBytes, writerRequestLimitBytes})`, returns whether the planned article budget would fit the planned writer limit once lowered to fit; not called from any pipeline code, unit-tested only.
  5. The headline word lists (`lib/headline-lists.js`) and the CNBC bounds (`lib/cnbc-news-research-runtime.js`, `lib/cnbc-recap-research-runtime.js`) are registered as reference entries pointing at their real location rather than duplicated into the table (no second source of truth); neither file is edited. CNBC acquisition code and its shared timing functions (`deriveCnbcResearchHorizons`) are untouched by rule.
