## Step 9F.2b — Wire the writer and classifier request limits to the new values (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule (4 Oct 2026):** the writer request cap is 500 KB (192-600 KB safe range) and the classifier request cap is 300 KB (64-400 KB safe range), both live Admin settings.
- **Finding before the build:** every call site 9F.2b's task described wiring (the Yahoo-reading, active, CNBC and completed hand-off checks, the subject-repair step, and the writer's hard check and temporary guard) already read `writerRequestLimitBytes`/`classifierRequestLimitBytes` from the one settings table as of Step 9F.2a. Raising the table's two entries is therefore sufficient; there was nothing left to wire at each call site.
- **Decision:**
  1. `lib/settings-registry.js`: `writerRequestLimitBytes` default 128 KB -> 500 KB, range 64-256 KB -> 192-600 KB; `classifierRequestLimitBytes` default 64 KB -> 300 KB, range 32-128 KB -> 64-400 KB. Env vars unchanged (`WRITER_REQUEST_LIMIT_BYTES`, `CLASSIFIER_REQUEST_LIMIT_BYTES`).
  2. Removed the two duplicate "planned" entries from 9F.2a (`writerRequestLimitBytesPlanned`, `classifierRequestLimitBytesPlanned`, and their `_PLANNED` env vars), merged into the one live entry each. `articleTextBudgetBytesPlanned` and `nonArticleOverheadBytesPlanned` are unchanged (Step 9F.2e territory).
  3. New audit event, `requestSizeSummary`, one line per Anthropic call (not one combined event -- the classifier call happens in the `?analysisPackage=1` request and the writer call in the separate `?claudeAnalysis=1` request, so a single event spanning both does not exist as one piece of code; Kevin confirmed two single-metric lines, same stage name, each tagged with the run's `generationId` the existing logging already attaches, is the right shape): `lib/claude-evidence-role-classification.js` emits `{stage: 'requestSizeSummary', call: 'classifier', requestBytes, limitBytes, percentOfLimit}` right after the classifier request size is computed, before the oversize check. `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js` emits the writer's version the same way, plus `trimmedBytes`/`trimmedArticleCount` captured from the temporary writer guard's own `writerArticleBudget` event (0/0 when the guard did not fire). No article text in either event. To find both lines for one run: grep the run's `generationId` across the `[analysis-package.stages]` and `[claude-analysis.invocation]` console prefixes.
  4. The temporary writer guard (`fitWriterArticleBudget`, Step 9F.1f) is unchanged code; it now simply measures against the raised 500 KB constant, so it goes quiet on every saved package (confirmed by the free comparison below) and still trims when pushed past 500 KB.
  5. Cross-check sanity: `articleTextBudgetBytesPlanned` (150 KB) + `nonArticleOverheadBytesPlanned` (100 KB) = 250 KB, comfortably inside the new live 500 KB writer limit; neither planned default needs changing for this step.
- **Tests:** full suite 1100/1100 (1099 before; net +1: +1 `checkArticleBudgetFits` row at the new live limit, +2 classifier just-under/just-over-300 KB boundary tests, -2 auto-generated rows for the two deleted "planned" settings). Updated for the new event/limits: 4 classifier diagnostics-count/order assertions, 1 classifier oversize-article-count assertion (9 -> 40 max-size articles to clear the new 300 KB cap), 1 writer diagnostics-array assertion, 2 `api/quote.js` structured-route log-count/order assertions (`tests/quote.test.js`), 1 oversized-writer-request fixture size (140,000 -> 600,000 bytes), and the federal-filler item count/search-range in 4 `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` classifier-preflight tests (20 -> 40/45 filler items, search range 0-4000 -> 0-8192 bytes) because 20 items at the fixed 8 KB classifier cut (160 KB max) can no longer reach the new 300 KB limit the way they reached the old 64 KB one; one of those tests' "full texts would not fit" assertion was removed because six full 16 KB texts (96 KB) now fit under 300 KB on their own (the fixed 8 KB cut still applies and is still asserted). Syntax 60/60. `git diff --check` clean (LF/CRLF notices only).
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls):** old tree exported read-only from `f9ffd3b` (current HEAD, this step's "before"); new tree is the working tree after this step. Harness adapted from the Step 9F.2a replay harness (same `lib/` modules, same 8 saved Replay Packages), replaying each package through `createUsAnalysisPackageOrchestrationService` with six synthetic current-session Yahoo articles per run at a controlled size, in three sets, all 8 packages x 3 sets = 24 runs, 0 unexpected differences:
  - **Short pages (3-6 KB stand-ins, clear of both caps):** package, classifier request and writer request byte-identical old vs. new on every package, as expected.
  - **Long pages (17-24 KB stand-ins):** old admits 3-4 of 6 (richer than Step 9F.1f's isolated test fixture, these real packages already carry CNBC/federal/multi-symbol evidence, so even fewer than the "five of six" figure measured on a minimal fixture fit under the old 64 KB cap) and rejects the rest as `REJECTED_CLASSIFIER_PREFLIGHT`, and the writer request is trimmed; new admits 6 of 6 with 0 rejected, and the writer request is never trimmed. Classifier 59,223-88,220 bytes old, 78,824-88,220 bytes new. Writer 131,068-131,072 bytes old (at or against the old cap, trimmed down to it), 203,211-222,608 bytes new (well under the new 500 KB cap, untrimmed).
  - **Mixed pages:** old admits 4-5 of 6 and trims; new admits 6 of 6, untrimmed. Classifier 57,954-64,094 bytes old, 66,533-74,123 bytes new. Writer 131,068-131,072 bytes old, 157,128-176,525 bytes new.
  - Full per-package numbers saved to the session's scratch folder (not committed); available on request.
- **Cost note (arithmetic on the comparison's own request bodies, no paid calls, ~4 bytes/token matching Step 9F.1f's own measured ratio):** a typical run (mixed-size pages) costs roughly 2,500 extra classifier input tokens and 9,300 extra writer input tokens (~11,800 total, ~$0.012 at $1/M input tokens). The six-long-article worst case costs roughly 5,100 extra classifier input tokens and 20,800 extra writer input tokens (~25,900 total, ~$0.026). Both stay well inside the $0.20 ceiling; this step raises typical cost, it does not lower it -- the 3-5 cent target is the pipeline redesign's job (pinned next task), not this one's.
- **Could not share:** nothing new -- the four classifier size checks and the writer's two checks already shared the one setting as of Step 9F.2a, so there was no per-call-site code to write.
- **Must not change:** everything listed under Step 9F.2a's "Must not change"; plus the temporary writer guard's behaviour and the fixed 8 KB classifier cut (unchanged code, only the limit they measure against moved); the 150 KB/100 KB planned-budget defaults (not wired this step); no article text in the new `requestSizeSummary` event; CNBC acquisition and its own request caps (left in place).
- **Supersedes:** Step 9F.2a's register rows for `writerRequestLimitBytes` (128 KB, 64-256 KB) and `classifierRequestLimitBytes` (64 KB, 32-128 KB), and the two now-removed `*Planned` rows (merged into the live entries above).

## Step 9F.2c.2 — Shared article score (not wired in) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Kevin's rule (4 Oct 2026):** every article, from any source, is scored once in one shared place, using additive points and recency points, all Admin settings:
  - Points (additive, each list independent): US macro list 20, most-active stock 12, portfolio 8, wire publishers 5, include words 5, Asia/Europe 3, benchmark or generic 1. Maximum without recency 54; with the biggest recency bonus (+4), 58.
  - Recency is measured from the trigger time, never from the last close: published under 6 h before the trigger +4, 6-24 h +2, over 24 h 0. A small bonus only -- it can never overrule the US macro list.
  - The drop list removes a headline only when no other list scores it (today's protection stays).
  - Ties go to the newer article, then to the original listing order.
  - No publisher-based penalty (Step 9F.2c.1 removed the blog-style list). Wire publishers earn +5, including Reuters stories that arrive through Yahoo pages.
  - Include words are an Admin-added, promote-only list (+5, never drop); it doesn't exist yet, so the function accepts an optional include-words list, empty by default.
  - Recaps are not scored and are never dropped (unaffected -- nothing calls this function yet).
- **Decision:**
  1. Kevin corrected the first draft of this plan on two points before the build: (a) the matching helpers must be moved out of `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`, not duplicated, because that file is Yahoo ranking code (not CNBC acquisition code), so editing it is allowed as long as ranking behavior doesn't change; and (b) the recency safety check must compare the largest of the three recency values against the macro points, not only the under-6h one. Both are reflected below.
  2. New `lib/headline-matching.js`: the whole-word pattern builder, the AMD-exclusion check, the publisher-key normalizer, the portfolio/most-active identity matching, and the age-label lower-bound parser -- previously unexported inside `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` -- moved out verbatim (no logic changes), plus precomputed pattern lists built once from `lib/headline-lists.js` data (`US_MACRO_PATTERNS`, `DROP_TERM_PATTERNS`, `LAST_RANKED_PATTERNS`, `TIER_3A_PATTERNS`, `WIRE_PARTNERS_SET`). `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` now imports all of these instead of building them locally; its local `ACTIVE_YAHOO_*` names are kept as thin aliases so nothing downstream of them changes. The one thing that stayed put: the `/^\s*how\s+to/i` regex is tier-4a-specific wording, not a word-list term, so it's still inline in `activeYahooCandidateTier` and is not part of the new scoring drop-list check. `lib/headline-lists.js` itself is untouched.
  3. New `lib/article-score.js`, exporting `scoreArticle(candidate, {triggerAt, portfolioIdentities, mostActiveIdentities, includeWords, env})` and `compareScoredArticles(a, b)` (a tie-breaking comparator for later wiring; not called from anywhere). `scoreArticle` reads every point value, recency bonus and recency threshold live from `lib/settings-registry.js` on each call (no loose numbers); it imports its matching logic from `lib/headline-matching.js`, so there is exactly one implementation of the whole-word/macro/identity/publisher/age-label matching shared by the Yahoo ranker and the new score. Recency: uses `candidate.publishedAt` when known; falls back to `candidate.ageLabel`'s lower-bound age when the publish time isn't known yet (candidates are ranked before download); otherwise recency is 0 and `recencyBasis` is `'unknown'`. The drop check mirrors Kevin's rule exactly: excluded only when a `DROP_TERM_PATTERNS` match exists and every other list scored 0 (recency never rescues a drop).
  4. New `checkRecencyNeverOverridesMacro({env})` in `lib/settings-registry.js` (same style as the existing `checkArticleBudgetFits`): checks all three recency values (not just the near one) against `rankingPointsUsMacro`; any value that would reach or exceed the macro points is clamped to `macroPoints - 1` with a warning naming which setting. `scoreArticle` calls this and uses its already-clamped values, so the guarantee is live even though nothing is wired.
  5. No settings-registry numbers needed adding -- every point value, recency bonus and recency threshold Kevin specified today is already in the table exactly as registered (unwired) in Step 9F.2a: `rankingPointsUsMacro` 20, `rankingPointsMostActive` 12, `rankingPointsPortfolio` 8, `rankingPointsWirePublishers` 5, `rankingPointsIncludeWords` 5, `rankingPointsAsiaEurope` 3, `rankingPointsBenchmarkOrGeneric` 1, `recencyBonusUnder6h` 4, `recencyBonus6to24h` 2, `recencyBonusOver24h` 0, `recencyThresholdHoursNear` 6 h, `recencyThresholdHoursFar` 24 h. Confirmed by a dedicated test asserting the registry defaults equal Kevin's numbers and that they sum to 54/58.
  6. Nothing calls `scoreArticle`, `compareScoredArticles` or `checkRecencyNeverOverridesMacro` from any pipeline code. `selectedActiveYahooCandidates`/`activeYahooCandidateTier` are unchanged in behavior (only where their matching helpers live changed).
- **Tests:** full suite 1136/1136 (1100 before this step; 36 new: 29 in new `tests/article-score.test.js` covering every case in the task -- each list alone, 2-/3-list combinations, max 54 and 58, recency under/between/over and the exact 6h/24h boundaries, recency-from-age-label, unknown-recency, the fresh-vs-macro-outranking proof, drop-only and drop+macro, Reuters-via-Yahoo wire points independent of macro, include-words match/empty, portfolio match, and the tie comparator including an unknown-age fallback; 7 new rows in `tests/settings-registry.test.js` for `checkRecencyNeverOverridesMacro` -- safe at defaults, plus one "at the macro points" and one "exceeds the macro points" row for each of the three recency values. The pre-existing 1100 tests, including every `us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` test that exercises tiering/audit/order through the moved helpers, pass unchanged -- no test updates needed, since the move only changed where the code lives. Syntax 62/62 (new `lib/headline-matching.js`, `lib/article-score.js`). `git diff --check` clean.
- **Proof the move is behavior-preserving:** a line-by-line diff of `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` shows every removed line reappearing verbatim in `lib/headline-matching.js` (comments and wrapping only otherwise); the full pre-existing suite passes unchanged.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls):** the full HTTP-replay harness used for 9F.1g/9F.2a/9F.2b's live-package comparisons was scratch code from those steps and was never committed, so it wasn't available to rebuild here; reconstructing it blind was judged too large and too risky for a step that must be invisible. Instead: ~35 real headline/publisher/identity fixtures were pulled from the passing, already-ground-truthed assertions in `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` (8K.1 sub-tier, 8K.5 tier order, wire-partner, whole-word, "how to"-position, STI, tier3a/4b/3b ordering, and the 9F.2c.1b AMD-exclusion rows), each scored with the new, unwired `scoreArticle` and ranked both ways. Biggest pattern: a My Stocks/Watchlist headline with no macro/wire signal now ranks well below a bare US-macro headline (portfolio 8 pts vs. macro 20 pts) -- an intentional consequence of Kevin's point values, not a bug, since today's tier system always put a portfolio-name match first regardless of points. Also noted: "How to trade volatile markets" is excluded today (the inline "how to" rule) but scores 0/not-excluded under the new rule, because "how to" isn't one of Kevin's word lists -- flagged for Kevin's attention since the task didn't say whether that rule should eventually fold into scoring. Full ~35-row table available on request (not committed).
- **Could not share:** nothing -- Kevin's correction removed what would otherwise have been here (a duplicated copy of the matching primitives). The one remaining asymmetry: the inline `/^\s*how\s+to/i` tier-4a rule in `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` is not reflected in `scoreArticle`'s drop check, because it isn't a word-list term and Kevin's drop-list rule names only "the drop list" (the word lists).
- **Must not change:** everything listed under Step 9F.2c.1's and 9F.2a's "Must not change"; plus: `scoreArticle`/`compareScoredArticles`/`checkRecencyNeverOverridesMacro` stay uncalled by any pipeline code; `lib/headline-lists.js` untouched; the inline "how to" rule stays in `us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`, not moved into the shared score; CNBC acquisition and its own timing functions untouched; no `?rev=` token changed; nothing stored in Neon.
- **Supersedes:** nothing (first step to read the Step 9F.2a-registered ranking/recency settings).

## Step 9F.6a.1 to 9F.6a.3 — Shared source reader live, Yahoo first, byte-identical (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. First of two commits for Step 9F.6a (9F.6a.4 to 9F.6a.6 follow in a second entry).
- **Kevin's rule:** Yahoo's evidence-gathering method is the baseline. Every source adopts all its rules; the only differences allowed between sources are how a source finds its candidate articles and its per-site page reader. This step builds the shared reader and moves Yahoo onto it as the first source. Reports must come out byte-identical.
- **Decision:**
  1. **9F.6a.1 — settings.** New rows in `lib/settings-registry.js`: `yahooInitialCandidateAttempts` 5 (Fixed; only sets `backfillUsed` in the logs), `auditMaxCandidatesPerEvent` 10 (Fixed), `auditMaxEventBytes` 3400 (Fixed), `auditFieldLimits` (Fixed: headline 80 characters / 120 bytes, publisher 24/48, slug 48/48, age label 12/16, failure type 48/48, listing outcome 32/32), `maxClassificationEvidenceItems` 50 (reference row; the constant stays in `lib/claude-evidence-role-classification.js`). For Step 9F.2h, Admin and not wired: `furtherReadingMaxLinks` 15 (5-30), `furtherReadingExtrasBudgetSeconds` 10 (5-30), `furtherReadingExtrasMinScore` 12 (0-58). All Fixed values equal the literals they replace.
  2. **9F.6a.2 — moved helpers.** New `lib/source-audit.js` (text truncation, the audit slug, the 3400-byte / 10-candidate audit splitter, and a per-source table of log stage names) and new `lib/source-ranking.js` (the tier ranking, including the "how to" rule, the stale-by-label check and symbol tagging), moved out of `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`. A line-by-line comparison with `5fedfcf`, after the renames, shows the code is unchanged apart from two things: the slug limits and the audit stage name are now inputs (read from the registry, and from the stage-name table with today's name as the default). The unused bucket-to-tier table was not carried over.
  3. **9F.6a.3 — the shared reader.** New `lib/source-reader.js`: `readRunContext` fetches Most Active once, before any source, as shared run context; `readSource` is the Step 9F.1b-9F.1g Yahoo loop taking the source as an input (reading window, try-last for label-old candidates, per-source reading budget, attempt and admit caps, download retries, result-to-decision mapping, publish-time check, horizon, duplicates, article size cap, symbol tagging, evidence creation with the source's id, classifier fit check, final skipped decisions, summary and audit events). A source supplies only `sourceId`, `findCandidates({market, window, triggerAt})` and `readPage(url, {headline})`. New `lib/sources/yahoo-news.js` is the Yahoo source: the listing pages (plus its own listing counts, sanitised as before) and the article page reader, which is handed the listing headline because it checks the page headline against it. Each source's limits come from the registry through a per-source table (`sourceLimits`); the orchestration's `ACTIVE_YAHOO_*` names now read from it and stay exported. `lib/yahoo-news-reading.js` is a thin wrapper over the shared reader until 9F.6a.6 removes it.
  4. **Active and completed states in one reader.** One `horizonFor(publishedAt)` input always: active states pass "CURRENT_SESSION while the session window exists", so the old two-way "window unavailable" test becomes one rule (no reading window, or no horizon) with the same result. `mode` (ACTIVE or COMPLETED) decides whether `afterClose` and `subsequentDevelopmentCount` are written; the window-unavailable reason and the required items are inputs. Log stage names stay Yahoo-named in this step (neutral names come with CNBC in 9F.6b).
  5. **Shared article size cap.** The reader keeps at most `articleKb` of an article's text. This changes nothing for Yahoo, whose page reader already keeps no more than the same setting.
  6. This supersedes Step 9F.1d's note that the result-to-decision mapping "stays in `readYahooNews`": it is now shared, and every source must return the common result types.
- **Tests:** full suite 1154/1154 (1136 before; 18 new). `tests/settings-registry.test.js` (8): three new Fixed rows checked against today's values, the audit field limits and classifier item cap, the Further Readings rows, and the automatic default/clamp/override checks for the three new Admin rows. New `tests/source-reader.test.js` (10): a fake source through every decision path in an active state (admitted, timeout retried to 3 attempts, 404, headline mismatch, no publish time, before and after the window, duplicate, thrown error, missing body, other extraction failure) with the summary key order and timing stages; the completed state keeping after-close articles apart; window unavailable counted by reason; the admit cap, the attempt cap and the reading budget stop; label-old tried last; a failing candidate search; bad inputs rejected; Yahoo limits from the registry; the shared run context; the Yahoo source's listing counts, failure handling and headline hand-off. No existing test needed changes. Syntax 59/59. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js`, old tree `5fedfcf` exported read-only with `git show`, new tree the working tree, the 8 saved packages. The harness now also records the headline handed to each page download and has a third news scenario that exercises every extraction-failure decision. 40 runs per sub-step (8 packages x 5 scenarios: every download OK, failing stand-ins, extraction failures, recap timeout then success, recap busy server): 9F.6a.1, 9F.6a.2 and 9F.6a.3 each 40/40 byte-identical (package, writer-fitted package, diagnostics and audit, classifier request, writer request, download order with headlines, recap calls). Outputs saved as `compare-9F.6a.1-output.txt` to `compare-9F.6a.3-output.txt` in the harness folder.
- **Could not share in this step:** where admitted items go in the evidence list and the completed-state classifier re-check (stay in the orchestration until Step 9F.2e); the log stage names (kept Yahoo-named for byte identity until 9F.6b).
- **Must not change:** everything under Steps 9F.1c-9F.1g, 9F.2a and 9F.2c.2's "Must not change", except the location clause below; plus: a source supplies only how it finds candidates and how it reads a page; Most Active is shared run context; the Yahoo page reader is always handed the listing headline; the Yahoo stage names until 9F.6b; CNBC acquisition and its timing functions untouched; no `?rev=` token changed; nothing stored in Neon.
- **Supersedes:** Step 9F.2c.2's "Must not change" clause that the inline "how to" rule stays in `us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: it moves with the tier ranking to `lib/source-ranking.js` (approved in the 9F.6a plan). It is still not part of the shared score. Also supersedes Step 9F.1d's "could not share" note on the result-to-decision mapping (point 6).

## Step 9F.6a.4 to 9F.6a.6 — Candidate pool kept, source id in the audit, old Yahoo reader removed (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2-scoring-budget · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Second of two commits for Step 9F.6a; the first (9F.6a.1 to 9F.6a.3) is `bb60af4` and its entry above is unchanged.
- **Decision:**
  1. **9F.6a.4 — candidate pool.** The shared reader returns a pool: one frozen entry per candidate the source found, in ranked order (dropped candidates last, as in the audit), each with source id, rank, full url and headline, publisher, listed time, age label, edition, tier and sub-tier, whether its label was old, whether it was opened, attempts, publish time and its decision. The orchestration keeps the pools per source for the run and hands each to a new optional `onCandidatePool` hook (default: nothing; a failing hook is ignored, like diagnostics). The pool is never logged, stored in Neon or put in the package. Nothing reads it in this step; Further Readings is unchanged. Step 9F.2h's second validation pass for extra Further Readings works from it later (its package-contract question stays deferred to 9F.2h).
  2. **9F.6a.5 — source id in the candidate audit.** Each candidate-audit event carries the source's id once, right after the stage name, not once per candidate. It is counted in the reserved bytes, so no event can pass the 3400-byte limit. On the saved packages each event grows by 30 bytes; the largest is 3107 bytes and no event split into an extra part. The reading summary event is unchanged.
  3. **9F.6a.6 — direct call.** The orchestration builds the Yahoo source once and calls the shared reader directly (Most Active first as shared run context, then the reading), passing the mode, the horizon rule, the required items (active: Yahoo quote pages and the Fed; completed: those plus the recaps and CNBC), the window-unavailable reason and the session window. `lib/yahoo-news-reading.js` is removed. The orchestration's exported `ACTIVE_YAHOO_*` names stay.
- **Note for Step 9F.2e (Kevin, 11 Oct 2026):** when the new score is connected, the "how to" headline rule stays as an Admin-editable drop pattern. Nothing changes for it in this step; it still lives with the tier ranking in `lib/source-ranking.js`.
- **Tests:** full suite 1158/1158 (1154 before; 4 new). `tests/source-reader.test.js` (3): the pool lists every candidate in ranked order with the audit's decisions and the full fields (opened, attempts, label-old, never-opened candidates), and is not logged; every audit event names its source once and the summary does not; a byte-by-byte sweep across the split point shows no event over 3400 bytes with the source id counted (the splitter's per-candidate separator keeps the closest event a few bytes under the limit). `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` (1): the pool reaches the hook in the audit's order, a throwing hook changes nothing, the package is byte-identical with and without the hook, and a non-function hook is rejected. No existing test needed changes. Syntax 58/58. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js` against the original baseline `5fedfcf` (not `bb60af4`), 8 packages x 5 scenarios = 40 runs per sub-step. The harness now also checks the pool against the audit (same count, order, decisions, tiers and attempts) and, from 9F.6a.5, strips the one listed field (`sourceId` on the candidate-audit events), compares part counts separately and also compares with parts joined. 9F.6a.4: 40/40 fully byte-identical, pool matches the audit in every run. 9F.6a.5 and 9F.6a.6: 40/40 identical apart from the listed field; part counts unchanged in every run (32 runs one part, 8 runs two parts). Outputs: `compare-9F.6a.4-output.txt` to `compare-9F.6a.6-output.txt`.
- **Numbers added:** none in this batch (the 9F.6a numbers were all added in 9F.6a.1).
- **Could not share in this step:** unchanged from the batch-1 entry: where admitted items go in the evidence list and the completed-state classifier re-check (orchestration until 9F.2e); Yahoo-named log stages (until 9F.6b).
- **Must not change:** as the 9F.6a.1 to 9F.6a.3 entry; plus: the pool stays in memory only (not logged, not in Neon, not in the package) until a step that uses it says otherwise; the source id appears once per audit event and is counted against the 3400-byte limit; the hook never affects package assembly.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 9F.2d.1, 9F.2d.2, 9F.2e.1 — Score drop rule, portfolio-slot picker, shadow score in the audit (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Phase 1 of 9F.2d/9F.2e: nothing is connected to the ranking, the slots or the selection. The reader still ranks by tier. Phase 2 (9F.2e.2 onward) waits for Kevin's sign-off on the before-and-after table.
- **Kevin's answers (11 Oct 2026):**
  1. A drop-list or "how to" headline is protected only by a portfolio, most-active, US macro or Asia-Europe match, or by the include-words list (promote-only, empty today). Wire publishers and the generic benchmark/market list do NOT protect. This is today's tier behaviour.
  2. The attempt cap's planned value is 18 (safe range 6-24, Admin), not live until Step 9F.2e.5.
  3. Section 4 will cite Yahoo portfolio articles in completed states: Step 9F.2d.3 (later).
  4. **Recaps are never dropped by the slots or by the budget.** Binding for Steps 9F.2d.3 and 9F.2e.2-9F.2e.6.
  5. The AMD eye-disease exclusion for company matching is a separate small step, 9F.2d.4 (later).
  6. Portfolio **scoring** uses both lists (as the tier ranking does today). Portfolio **slots** use only the list that started the run, passed as one named input (`portfolioSlotSnapshots` / `portfolioSlotList`) so Step 21D can widen it.
- **Decision:**
  1. **9F.2d.1 — settings and the score's drop rule.** `lib/article-score.js`: the drop check now uses `DROP_PROTECTING_LISTS` (portfolio, mostActive, usMacro, asiaEurope, includeWords); a headline with a drop-list term or a "how to" start and none of those is excluded, whatever its wire, generic or recency points. The result gains `dropReason` (`DROP_LIST`, `HOW_TO` or null); `excludedByDropList` covers both. The "how to" rule moves from the inline regex in `lib/source-ranking.js` to the named list `HOW_TO_DROP_PREFIXES` in `lib/headline-lists.js`, compiled by the new `headlineStartPattern` in `lib/headline-matching.js` into exactly the old pattern (`/^\s*how\s+to(?![A-Za-z0-9])/i`; a test checks source, flags and the answer on every string literal in the test files). The tier ranking reads it from there; its tier position is unchanged. Still not called by any pipeline code.
  2. **The live attempt cap stays 12.** `yahooMaxArticleAttempts` is read on every run, so changing its default to 18 would let today's runs try more articles. It stays 12 (6-20, live); the planned value is a separate, unwired row, `yahooMaxArticleAttemptsPlanned` (18, 6-24, Admin, env `YAHOO_MAX_ARTICLE_ATTEMPTS_PLANNED`). Step 9F.2e.5 swaps them.
  3. **9F.2d.2 — portfolio-slot picker.** New `lib/portfolio-slots.js`, `pickPortfolioArticles({articles, portfolioSlotList, slots, firstPassPerCompany, alreadyChosen})`, pure and not wired. Eligible: names a company in `portfolioSlotList`, not already holding a general slot, not a recap. First pass in score order (ties: newer, then listing order): one article per company not yet covered by a portfolio pick (`portfolioFirstPassPerCompany`, Fixed 1). Second pass, only if a slot is still empty: the next best article, even for a covered company. An article naming two companies covers both. Returns picks (with symbols and pass) and passed-over articles with a reason (`RECAP`, `ALREADY_CHOSEN`, `NO_PORTFOLIO_COMPANY`, `SLOTS_FULL`).
  4. **9F.2e.1 — shadow score.** `lib/source-reader.js` scores every candidate with `scoreArticle`. The inputs are the same names the tier ranking uses (both lists through the shared `portfolioIdentitiesFrom`, Most Active), the trigger time (`acquisitionStartedAt`) and the headline, publisher and age label (before download, so recency comes from the label). Nothing about the order, the decisions or the caps changes.

## Step 9F.2c.3 — Three new macro terms, a "dollar" currency fix, "Wall Street" removed (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Context:** the real Weekend run on 11 October 2026 showed a market headline scoring 0 ("Update: Equities Rise Intraday as Traders Parse Consumer Sentiment Survey"), because "equities" and "consumer sentiment" are on no list. It also showed two accidental matches: "Costco is booming as Americans rethink where every dollar goes" matched `dollar` as if it were about the currency, and "The Dividend Battle Wall Street Didn't See Coming" matched `Wall Street` as if it were market news. Kevin approved all three changes on 11 October 2026; removing `Wall Street` from the macro list (rather than adding an exclusion list for it, as done for `dollar`) was his choice, made after the explanation of both options.
- **Decision:**
  1. `US_MACRO_TERMS` (`lib/headline-lists.js`) gains three terms: `equities`, `consumer sentiment`, `stock market` — whole-phrase, case-insensitive, same `wholeWordPattern` matching as every other term.
  2. `{term: 'Wall Street'}` is removed from `US_MACRO_TERMS`. No other entry on that list changed. `Wall Street` remains, unrelated, inside `WIRE_PARTNERS` (`'wall street journal'` — publisher-name matching, not touched).
  3. `lib/headline-lists.js` adds a new named export, `DOLLAR_EXCLUSION_PHRASES`, same shape and comment style as the Step 9F.2c.1b `AMD_EXCLUSION_PHRASES`: `every dollar`, `each dollar`, `per dollar`, `a dollar`, `Dollar General`, `Dollar Tree`, `Dollar Shave Club`, `billion-dollar`, `million-dollar`, `trillion-dollar`, `dollar store`, `dollar stores`.
  4. `lib/headline-matching.js`'s `macroTermMatches` helper, previously hardcoded to check only `term === 'AMD'` against `AMD_EXCLUSION_PATTERNS`, is generalized into a `TERM_EXCLUSION_PATTERNS` lookup (`Map` from term name to its compiled exclusion patterns), now holding both `AMD` and `dollar`. Behavior for every other term is unchanged (the lookup returns nothing, same as the old `false` short-circuit); only the matched term's own match is suppressed, every other macro term in the same headline still counts — unchanged from the AMD mechanism, just shared instead of duplicated.
  5. `"dollar"` does not match `"dollars"` today (the whole-word boundary regex requires a non-alphanumeric or end-of-string character after the term; a trailing `s` fails that), and this step does not change that.
- **Live ranking changes:** both `lib/source-ranking.js` (active-state tier ranking) and `lib/article-score.js` (shared score, not yet wired into the live report) read `US_MACRO_PATTERNS`/`macroTermMatches` generically — no caller change was needed, but this means the live active-state ranking changes for any headline containing `equities`, `consumer sentiment`, `stock market`, `dollar` or `Wall Street`.
- **Tests:** new table-driven block `STEP_9F2C3_ROWS` in `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` (12 rows, same pattern as `STEP_9F2C1B_ROWS`), covering the three new terms, dollar positives/negatives (including a "Dollar Tree ... earnings" row that stays macro through `earnings` once the dollar match is suppressed, and a "Dollar General and Fed rate hike" row that stays macro through `Fed`/`rate hike`), and both Wall Street rows (a false-positive headline that is no longer macro, and a genuine Wall Street headline that stays macro through `Nvidia`). Scanned `tests/article-score.test.js` and the rest of the repo for any test relying on `Wall Street` as the only macro match: none found (its one `Wall Street` fixture also contains `Fed`). Full suite 1190/1190 (1189 before). Syntax 46/46. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls):** `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js`, old tree `b1df462` (read-only `git show`/`git archive` export of `lib/`), new tree the working tree, 8 packages × 6 scenarios = 48 runs: 48/48 fully byte-identical (none of the 8 saved packages' stand-in headlines contain any of the five changed/added words). A second scratch script (`diff-macro-9f2c3.js`, old vs. new `macroTermMatches`/`US_MACRO_PATTERNS` only) over the 8 packages' evidence headlines plus the 27-entry `REAL_HEADLINES` listing (72 unique headlines): exactly 2 changed, both `false -> true`, both CNBC "...things to watch in the stock market..." headlines, both explained by the new `stock market` term. No headline containing `dollar` or `Wall Street` appeared in either corpus, so this comparison did not exercise those two changes directly; the new `STEP_9F2C3_ROWS` test above is the coverage for them. `Replay Packages\harness\compare-selection-order.js` was also run (`step-9f2c3.md`/`.json`, PASS, 27/90 rows changed) but it compares the old tier method (`rankCandidates`) against the new score method (`scoreArticle`) by design (built for Step 9F.2e.1), not old-vs-new word lists on the same method, so its changed rows reflect that method difference, not this step's word-list change, and are not a word-list regression signal.
- **Could not share:** nothing — both new lists live in the same shared module as every other word list, matched at the same two sites.
- **Must not change:** everything listed under Steps 9F.2c.1, 9F.2c.1b, 9F.2c.2, 9F.6a and 9F.2e.1's "Must not change". In addition: the dollar exclusion check must stay scoped to the `dollar` term only and must only suppress that one term's match; no other word, tier, drop-list rule, cap, retry, size or the CNBC code changed.
- **Supersedes:** nothing — this adds to and narrows the Step 9F.2c.1 macro list; the rest of that list and the Step 9F.2c.1b AMD exclusion stand.

## Step 9F.2e.2 — One shared article-selection step, built but not connected (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Built and tested only: nothing in the live pipeline calls it, and reports do not change.
- **Kevin's rules (11 Oct 2026):** one selection step for every source, with Yahoo's evidence rules as the baseline (sources differ only in how they find candidates and their page reader). Everything is scored with the shared score; ties go to the newer article, then listing order; no publisher penalty. General slots 7 Yahoo : 3 CNBC, plus 2 portfolio slots. Unused general slots pass to the best articles of other sources (Admin switch, up to 3 spare per receiving source). Portfolio slots cover BOTH My Stocks and the Watchlist (Step 21D); a general-slot article that names a company counts as covering it in the first pass. Article text budget 150 KB: over it, the lowest scores go first (general, then portfolio); recaps are never dropped and nothing is shortened. Classifier fit: at most 50 items and the 300 KB classifier limit; over either, the same order says what would be left out; recaps and protected items are never left out. Attempts (12 live, 18 planned) are not part of this step.
- **Decision:**
  1. New `lib/article-selection.js`, pure (no network, storage or paid calls), with pieces that each run on their own: `scoreCandidates` (all sources together, through `scoreArticle`/`compareScoredArticles`; recaps set aside unscored; the score's drop rule applied), `fillGeneralSlots` (per-source slots from `SOURCE_SLOT_SETTINGS`, then spare slots passed on), `pickPortfolioSlots` (both lists merged by `mergedPortfolioList`, each pick naming the list(s) its company is on), `applyWriterBudget` and `checkClassifierFit` (both take an already-downloaded list, so the duplicate-coverage step 9F.4a can run between download and the budget), `selectArticles` (the first three together) and `selectionSummary` (every decision with its reason, short headlines only, no article text, safe to log).
  2. `lib/portfolio-slots.js` gains one optional input, `coveredSymbols` (one entry per company named by a general-slot article; each counts as one first-pass article). Default empty, so its behaviour is unchanged for existing callers.
  3. Nothing under `api/`, `lib/` or `lib/sources/` requires the new module (a test checks this). `lib/source-reader.js`'s `portfolioSlotEligible` (memory-only pool field) still uses the starting list; it moves to both lists when Step 9F.2d.3 passes both lists.
- **Numbers:** none added — every number already had a row. Reused: `yahooSlots` 7 (0-10, Admin), `cnbcSlots` 3 (0-6, Admin), `portfolioSlots` 2 (0-4, Admin), `spareAdmits` 3 (0-5, Admin; now described as the most spare slots one source may receive), `slotSpilloverEnabled` on (Admin), `portfolioFirstPassPerCompany` 1 (Fixed), `articleTextBudgetBytesPlanned` 150 KB (32-200 KB, Admin), all still not wired, each row now naming the step that switches it on (slots and spare 9F.2e.5, portfolio slots 9F.2d.3, text budget 9F.2e.6); `maxClassificationEvidenceItems` 50 (reference row) and `classifierRequestLimitBytes` 300 KB (64-400 KB, Admin, live) are read, not changed; `auditFieldLimits` headline 80 characters / 120 bytes for the summary's short headlines.
- **Tests:** full suite 1240/1240 (1190 before; 50 new). New `tests/article-selection.test.js` (49): settings at Kevin's values; 16 safe-range clamping rows; 8 general-slot rows (plenty, Yahoo short, Yahoo far short, CNBC zero, CNBC zero with Yahoo short, both short, switch off, spare limit 1) plus the spare-slot order and reasons; tie-break; no publisher penalty, drop rule and recaps; unknown source and duplicate keys rejected; 5 portfolio rows (My Stocks only, Watchlist only, both, a company on both lists, no portfolio article — slots stay empty); a general-slot article covering a company; the merged list; 5 writer-budget rows (under, just over, general before portfolio, then portfolio, recaps alone over — kept and reported) plus the 150 KB default and unchanged text; 4 classifier rows (fits, 52 items, over 300 KB, protected items alone over) plus the rest-of-request bytes; the summary (every decision, 80-character headlines, no article text); and the not-imported check. `tests/portfolio-slots.test.js` (1): the covered-company input. Syntax 60/60. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js`, old tree `0530f3e` exported read-only with `git show`, new tree the working tree, 8 packages × 6 scenarios (failing stand-ins and real headlines included): 48/48 fully byte-identical. Output `compare-9F.2e.2-output.txt`. Dry run of the module (information only): `dry-run-selection-9F.2e.2.js` → `dry-run-9F.2e.2.md` / `.json`.
- **Could not share:** the classifier fit takes each item's byte size and the rest of the request as inputs; building the real classifier request to measure it stays with the caller (the reader does this today). The source on/off switches (`yahooSourceEnabled`, `cnbcSourceEnabled`, `portfolioSourceEnabled`) are not read by the module yet.
- **Must not change:** everything under Steps 9F.2a to 9F.2c.3, 9F.2d.1/9F.2d.2/9F.2e.1 and 9F.6a's "Must not change", except the clause superseded below. In addition: the module stays unconnected until Step 9F.2e.3; recaps are never dropped by the slots, the budget or the classifier fit; the budget never shortens an article; CNBC acquisition and its timing functions are untouched; no `?rev=` token is changed; nothing is stored in Neon.
- **Supersedes:** Step 9F.2d.1/9F.2d.2/9F.2e.1 answer 6, "Portfolio slots use only the list that started the run": the slots now cover both My Stocks and the Watchlist (Kevin, 11 Oct 2026). Portfolio scoring already used both lists. Note for Step 9F.2e.6: the Step 9F.1f temporary writer trim (articles shortened to fit) conflicts with "nothing is shortened" and must be superseded when the budget is switched on.

## Step 9F.2e.3.2 — The two Yahoo hand-off loops run through the shared selection step (no report change) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-11 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Reports are byte-identical to before in every market state.
- **Kevin's approvals (11 Oct 2026, not to be reopened):** (1) the Step 9F.2e.2 "nothing imports the selection step" test is replaced: only `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` may import `lib/article-selection.js`, and only `checkListOrderFit`; (2) Step 9F.2e.3.1 (commit `34531ac`) had no entry here, so it is recorded inside this entry, below; no older entry is edited; (3) no live Preview runs in this step, free checks only; (4) the stale "NOT wired" comment in `lib/article-selection.js` is updated (comment text only); (5) strict harness: raw diagnostics byte-identical with no "listed field" exceptions, plus a forced builder-error scenario; (6) the syntax check covers `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js` and must count 60 files.
- **Step 9F.2e.3.1 (commit `34531ac`, recorded here):** added three today's-mode pieces to `lib/article-selection.js`, none connected at the time: `checkListOrderFit` (the list-order fit check shared by today's three hand-off loops in STOP mode and by the reader's per-candidate check in SKIP mode; returns positions only, never copies), `tierTryOrder` (the reader's try order: `rankCandidates` plus the `staleByLabel` back-of-the-line move, both called unchanged) and `createReaderStopRule` (the reader's attempts cap, admitted cap and reading budget, checked in today's order). Tests: full suite 1253/1253 (13 new in `tests/article-selection.test.js`). Free comparison: `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js` 48/48 fully byte-identical; a same-answer script (`same-answer-9F.2e.3.1.js`, scratch) over 10,000 generated cases (7,000 list-order, 1,000 no-horizon, 2,000 stop-rule) found 0 differences; re-run against `34531ac` during this step: 10,000 cases, 0 differences.
- **Decision (this step):**
  1. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` gains one import, `const {checkListOrderFit} = require('./article-selection');`.
  2. **Active Yahoo hand-off:** the hand-written loop is replaced by one STOP-mode call over the original acquired articles, in order: `baseCount` = the required base evidence count, `maxItems` = `MAX_CLASSIFICATION_EVIDENCE_ITEMS` (50), `maxBytes` = `CLAUDE_EVIDENCE_ROLE_CLASSIFICATION_PROVISIONAL_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` (the live classifier limit), and `measure` = the same `classificationInputFor` + `buildClaudeEvidenceRoleClassificationRequest` + UTF-8 byte length today's loop built (base + trial items; base horizons + `CURRENT_SESSION` per trial article; base broad-market news + trial). With no classifier pre-check, `measure` is `null`, so only the 50-item test runs (today's `continue` path). The kept articles are the caller's own objects at the kept positions, same order. The `activeYahooEvidenceHandoff` log is unchanged.
  3. **Completed Yahoo hand-off:** each acquired article gets a lookup card `{item, horizon, noHorizon}` (horizon from `completedYahooNewsHorizon`, exactly as before); one STOP-mode call with `baseCount` = the provisional CNBC evidence count and a `measure` that builds exactly today's request from the cards' articles and horizons (the cards themselves never reach the classifier input). Each kept card's article and horizon are pushed onto the existing two arrays, so every later reader of them is unchanged. The `completedYahooEvidenceHandoff` log is unchanged.
  4. A measuring error is a misfit that stops the list, the same as today's catch-then-break.
  5. `checkListOrderFit` cannot add a new failure: `items` is always an array, `mode` is the literal `'STOP'`, `maxItems` is the constant 50, `baseCount` is an array length, and the byte limit comes from `settingValue`, which always returns a finite number between 64 KB and 400 KB (non-numbers fall back to 300 KB). No input was found that could be invalid. One order-of-evaluation difference, with no effect: the completed loop now works out every acquired article's horizon before the check (today it stopped working them out at the first misfit); `completedYahooNewsHorizon` is a pure comparison of two numbers and the articles are evidence objects the reader built, so this cannot throw or change anything.
  6. Comments: the header and the today's-mode comment in `lib/article-selection.js` now say only `checkListOrderFit` is wired in; the test-file header likewise.
- **Not connected (unchanged on purpose):** the CNBC hand-off loop and the provisional CNBC values (waits for Step 9F.6d); the Yahoo reader `lib/source-reader.js` (Step 9F.2e.3.4), so `tierTryOrder` and `createReaderStopRule` are still unused by the pipeline. `classificationInputFor` is called, not changed. `lib/cnbc-*.js`, `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`, `monotonicNow` and `sleep` are not touched.
- **Why a "no horizon" article never reaches the completed loop's check (proof):** in completed states the reader is given `horizonFor: completedYahooNewsHorizon` — the same function the completed loop uses — and calls it on the same publish time. An article whose horizon is `null` is rejected as `REJECTED_WINDOW_UNAVAILABLE` at `lib/source-reader.js` lines 344-352, before the classifier size check at line 391 and before it can be admitted (line 398). So every article the completed loop receives has a horizon; the `noHorizon` stop is kept only to match today's `!horizon` check exactly. The free comparison's "no canonical close" scenario confirms it: the reader admits 0 articles and the completed loop receives an empty list.
- **Numbers and settings:** none added. No new setting, no new number, no new log event, no selection summary logged; every existing log event keeps the same fields, order and counts. Today's mode (STOP) is written in code by the caller; there is no switch. No `?rev=` token changed; nothing is stored in Neon; the Step 9F.1f temporary writer trim stays.
- **Tests:** full suite 1253/1253 (same count: the Step 9F.2e.2 "nothing under api/ or lib/ imports the selection step" test is replaced by "only the orchestration imports the selection step, and only checkListOrderFit", which also checks the exact import line). Syntax check over `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js`: 60/60 (1 + 58 + 1). `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Syntax count history (46, 48, 60):** each figure is the number of files the check covered at that time, not a change in method. 46 is `api/*.js lib/*.js` at `4127b19` (1 + 45), the number written in the local working notes; Step 9F.2c.3's "46/46" repeated that stale figure (at `0530f3e` the three folders held 1 + 57 + 1 = 59 files). 48 is the Step 9D figure, when `lib/` had grown to 47 files (1 + 47). 60 is today's three-folder count (1 + 58 + 1), the same 60 Step 9F.2e.2 reported. Step 9F.2a/9F.2b's earlier "60/60" and 9F.2c.2's "62/62" covered a different file set than these three folders and are not comparable.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls):** old tree = read-only `git archive` export of `34531ac`'s `lib/`; new tree = the working tree.
  1. Strict replay (`strict.js` + `strict-core.js`, adapted from `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js`; the classifier request builder is wrapped before the orchestration loads, so every call — the reader's and both hand-off loops' — is counted and its input hashed): 8 packages × 6 scenarios = 48/48 strictly identical (run twice; the second run, after the pre-check scenario was corrected, 316/316 identical across the 48 and the extra scenarios b-e): raw diagnostics byte-identical with no exceptions, plus the assembled package, writer-fitted package, candidate pools, classifier request, writer request, download order, recap calls, builder call count and builder input hashes.
  2. Extra scenarios, all identical old vs new: (a) squeeze, run in a child process with `CLASSIFIER_REQUEST_LIMIT_BYTES=65536` loading both trees (the parent kept 300 KB): 16 KB article stand-ins on all 8 packages, plus a sweep of 79 article sizes (600 bytes to about 9 KB) with and without 8 extra CNBC records — 1,672 pairs, all identical, NOT EXERCISED: the reader admitted only 3-5 articles and the hand-off never stopped on bytes, because the reader's own size check measures a request at least as large as the hand-off's (it counts every CNBC record and the same articles) and turns the article away first. (b) item cap (32 extra CNBC records, 14 Yahoo articles): identical, NOT EXERCISED — the reader applies the same 50-item limit to the same or a larger base first (completed: CNBC filled the classifier to 50 and the reader admitted 0 Yahoo articles; active: the reader's 6-article cap keeps the list well under 50). (c) pre-check off: a mismatched benchmark date cannot survive the freshness check (the mutated benchmark is dropped and the pre-check stays on), so the scenario uses missing benchmark prices or benchmarks with no completed date: EXERCISED in the active states (6 articles handed off with no measuring); in completed states the reader admits nothing in that case, so the completed loop only receives an empty list. (d) no canonical close (completed packages; two ways): EXERCISED — reader admitted 0, all 8 articles rejected as window unavailable, completed loop received 0 articles. (e) forced builder error at call N, every N from 1 to (calls + 1), same N on both trees, ALL_OK and MANY_OK on all 8 packages (222 runs): 0 differences; EXERCISED — a hand-off was stopped by the forced error in 96 of them (6 per package and list).
  3. Same-answer script (`same-answer.js`): the two old loops (with their log blocks) are cut word for word from `34531ac` and the two new call sites word for word from the working tree, and run as function bodies with the same inputs. 10,000 generated cases (500 with the real request builder over real telemetry from the saved packages, 9,500 with a fast size stand-in): 0 differences in kept articles (compared by object identity), the horizon array, both log payloads (JSON) and the sequence of builder calls. Coverage: 4,909 active and 5,091 completed cases; pre-check off in 2,037; stopped by the 50-item limit in 2,964, by the byte limit in 4,240, by a forced builder error in 448, by a `null` horizon in 582; 634 empty lists; the real builder was called 1,910 times without throwing on its own. A self-test with three deliberately wrong new sides (SKIP instead of STOP; base count off by one; horizon check removed) was caught every time (439, 61 and 47 differences in 1,000 cases).
  4. Combinations excluded or allowed although the live pipeline cannot produce them: the active loop gets an empty list in completed states and the completed loop does not run in active states (as in the pipeline). Allowed on purpose, because Kevin asked for them, although the reader prevents them live: base counts of 30-55 with up to 15 articles (the reader admits at most 6 and already applies the 50-item limit), and a `null` horizon at a random position (live it is all-or-nothing and the reader rejects those articles first).
- **What these checks cannot prove:** real Yahoo pages (stand-in listings and article bodies only), real timing (the clock and sleep are stubbed; the reading budget is not exercised under real delays), and real Claude output (the classifier and writer are never called; only their request bodies are built and compared). The live Yahoo hand-offs have never been seen to drop an article on the byte or item limit, so those branches are covered by the scratch scripts, not by a live run.
- **Could not share:** the request building and byte measuring stay with the caller (the `measure` function), because they need the caller's evidence, horizons and broad-market list; the shared step only decides positions. The completed loop's lookup card is built by the caller because only the caller knows the horizon.
- **Must not change:** everything under Steps 9F.2a to 9F.2e.2's "Must not change", except the clause superseded below. In addition: both Yahoo hand-offs stay in STOP mode with today's limits until a later step and Kevin decide otherwise; the CNBC hand-off loop, the reader, the CNBC modules and the shared timing functions stay untouched until their own steps (9F.6d, 9F.2e.3.4); `checkListOrderFit` returns positions only and callers keep their own objects.
- **Supersedes:** Step 9F.2e.2 decision point 3, "Nothing under `api/`, `lib/` or `lib/sources/` requires the new module (a test checks this)": now only `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` requires it, and only `checkListOrderFit` (Kevin, 11 Oct 2026). The rest of point 3 (the reader's `portfolioSlotEligible` still uses the starting list) stands.

## Step 8J.1 — Plain blank-section wording in the frontend (repo: MarketBrief)

- **Date:** 2026-10-12 · **Branch:** step-8j1-blank-section-wording · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Staging only; nothing on Production (`main` @ `610fa07`) is touched.
- **Context:** on the live Pre-market run of 12 Oct 2026, Section 3 showed "No supported analysis is available from the supplied package." A read-only check of `claude-analysis.js` found this is the frontend's one fixed placeholder for any section whose content is blank, used inside the function that renders the Market Brief. The frontend never shows the proxy's own wording, so the plain Step 8J wording Kevin asked for on 28 Sep never reached the reader, and the PDF export (which copies the on-screen HTML) showed the same generic sentence.
- **Intent:** replace the single fixed sentence with the approved plain-language wording, per section, text only. No validation, data-shape, or proxy change.
- **Kevin's approval (12 Oct 2026):** the wording below, as Step 8J.1, Staging only.
- **Decision:**
  - The renderer already validates, before this point, that each section's `name` strictly equals the fixed `REPORT_SECTIONS[index].name` for that position — so the section's name is already a stable, pre-checked identifier. The blank-section message is chosen by looking up `section.name` in a small table; any section not in that table (today: Sections 1 and 4, and any future/unknown section) falls back to the generic sentence below. Section 8 (Further Readings) is a separate branch and keeps its own unchanged placeholder ("No validated Further Readings were supplied.").
  - Wording (before → after), by section name:
    - KEY MARKET DRIVERS (Section 2): "No supported analysis is available from the supplied package." → "Not enough data to name the main drivers of the market."
    - STOCKS & SECTORS IN FOCUS (Section 3): same before → "Not enough data to point out specific stocks or sectors."
    - MARKET INTERPRETATION (Section 5): same before → "Not enough data to give an interpretation."
    - KEY RISKS & OPPORTUNITIES (Section 6): same before → "Not enough data to point out risks or opportunities."
    - WHAT TO WATCH FOR NEXT (Section 7): same before → "Not enough data to say what to watch next."
    - Any other blank section, including EXECUTIVE MARKET SUMMARY (1) and MY STOCKS & WATCHLIST - MATERIAL MOVEMENTS (4) if they ever come back blank, and any unrecognized section name: same before → "Not enough data to produce an analysis."
  - Same markup, class and escaping as the sentence it replaces (`<div style="color:var(--mut);">...</div>`), so layout and the PDF export (which copies this HTML) are visually unchanged apart from the wording.
- **Must not change:** validation logic, the data shape the frontend accepts, anything the proxy sends, the `uncertainties`/`evidenceGaps` handling (stays validated and unused), the visible version string (`v2.20260921.25.F`, held in `app.js` and `marketbrief.html`, plus a synchronization test — bump deferred to release Step 12B), and anything on Production (`main` @ `610fa07`).
- **Files:** `claude-analysis.js` (new `BLANK_SECTION_TEXT` lookup and `BLANK_SECTION_FALLBACK_TEXT`, used in `renderMarketBriefAnalysis`'s per-section blank branch). `tests/claude-analysis.test.js` (two existing assertions updated to the new wording for the DEGRADED-status KEY RISKS & OPPORTUNITIES case; two new tests added: one table-driven test covering all 7 non-Further-Readings sections' blank wording plus the fallback case, confirming every other section in the same response stays populated and byte-unchanged; one test confirming the Further Readings placeholder is unchanged and that whitespace-only or missing section content still throws the existing validation error exactly as before, rather than silently rendering as blank).
- **Evidence/tests:** full suite 163/163 (161 before: 9 repo test files run in isolated `node --test` processes per file, one new test added net in `claude-analysis.test.js`, 37 → 39). `git diff --check` clean.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no paid calls, no live network):** old tree = read-only `git show 3c951ac:claude-analysis.js` export; new tree = the working tree. Both loaded in a small Node/vm harness (adapted from the test file's own fixtures) and run against: (a) all 7 sections populated; (b) each of Sections 2, 3, 5, 6, 7 blanked one at a time; (c) all 7 sections blanked together; (d) Section 1 or Section 4 blanked. 72 section-outcomes checked across 9 scenarios: every populated section and the Further Readings placeholder were byte-identical old vs new; every blanked section differed only by the approved wording (old generic sentence vs the new per-section text). All checks passed.
- **What this cannot prove:** the real PDF rendering (the PDF exporter copies this HTML, but the exporter itself was not exercised) and real proxy replies (no live network call was made). Needs one live Staging run to confirm the wording appears correctly in both the on-screen brief and the exported PDF.
- **Rejected options:** none — Kevin specified the wording and the per-section approach directly.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 9F.2e.3.4a — Three additions to the shared selection pieces for the reader connection (not connected, no report change) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-12 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** COMMITTED in `be8d65b` without an entry; recorded here in the Step 9F.2e.3.4b commit (no older entry is edited). Nothing was connected by it and reports did not change.
- **Decision:** three additions to `lib/article-selection.js`, so the reader can be connected safely in Step 9F.2e.3.4b:
  1. `createReaderStopRule` takes an optional `budget`: when supplied, the caller's own reading budget object is reused as is (no second stopwatch, no extra clock read); when omitted, it makes its own with `createReadingBudget`, exactly as before.
  2. `createReaderStopRule` accepts decimal caps (an Admin value such as 12.5), compared raw with `>=` exactly like the reader's loop header; only negative, non-finite or missing caps are rejected.
  3. `tierTryOrder` also returns `oldLabelPositions`: the try-last part of `order` on its own, same order, never a dropped position, so the reader can mark its audit entries before the loop.
- **Tests:** full suite 1269/1269 (1253 before; 16 new in `tests/article-selection.test.js`). No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only):** `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js` against `7ab80e1`: 48/48 byte-identical (`compare-9F.2e.3.4a-output.txt`). `stop-rule-same-answer-9F.2e.3.4a.js`: the reader's loop-header decision copied word for word from `7ab80e1` against the new stop rule over 10,000 generated cases: 0 differences; its self-test caught all three planted mistakes (second stopwatch, budget before caps, caps rounded down). `try-order-compare-9F.2e.3.4a.js`: order, dropped and tiers unchanged on every saved package; the new old-label positions match the reader's own try-last logic in every case.
- **Must not change:** as Step 9F.2e.3.2; the reader stayed unconnected in this commit.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 9F.2e.3.4b — The Yahoo reader runs through the shared selection step (no report change) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-12 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Reports, audit decisions, counts and logs are byte-identical to before in every market state.
- **Kevin's approvals (12 Oct 2026, from the read-only planning session for Step 9F.2e.3.4):** the plan and all its decisions; no live Preview runs in this step, free checks only.
- **Decision:** `lib/source-reader.js` gains one import, `const {tierTryOrder, createReaderStopRule, checkListOrderFit} = require('./article-selection');` (and no longer imports `staleByLabel`).
  1. **Try order:** the hand-written try-first/try-last loop is replaced by one `tierTryOrder` call with the same inputs as before: the listing candidates, Most Active, `stockSnapshots` as the portfolio list (the same input the reader's own `rankCandidates` call uses, not `portfolioSlotSnapshots`), and the label window and generation time worked out exactly as before. Each returned position is translated back to the reader's ranked row through its candidate object (Yahoo builds one fresh object per listing row, so no object appears twice). Tier 4a candidates are not in the order and keep their `SKIPPED_BELOW_RELEVANCE_FLOOR` decision. The old-label positions set the audit `labelOld` flag and the stale-by-label count before the loop, so every audit entry keeps its field order.
  2. **Stop rule:** the reading budget is made once, at the same place, with the same single clock read; the SAME budget object goes to both `createReaderStopRule` and `downloadWithRetries` (one stopwatch). The caps are passed unrounded. The check order is unchanged: attempts cap, admitted cap, then the budget (the clock is read only when neither cap is reached). Attempts or admitted cap reached → plain stop; budget spent → `readingBudgetStopped = true`, then stop.
  3. **Per-article classifier fit:** the count test, the byte comparison and the try/catch are replaced by one `checkListOrderFit` call in SKIP mode, inside the same try block, at the same place: the one new article as the list, base count = required items + already admitted, the 50-item constant, the live classifier byte limit, and `measure` = the same request building as before (now `classifierRequestBytes`, the old function body without its count test, comparison or catch) over required + admitted + the new article, with the admitted horizons plus the new one and the same broad-market rule. With no classifier pre-check, `measure` is `null` (only the count test runs, as before). A measuring error is a misfit (`REJECTED_CLASSIFIER_PREFLIGHT`, same two counts), never rethrown. `noHorizon` is never passed (those articles are rejected earlier).
  4. **Not moved:** window set-up, candidate search, the ranking for the audit and the pool, the shadow score, audit entries, the duplicate set, the download loop and `downloadWithRetries`, result-to-decision mapping, publish-time, horizon and window checks, duplicate check, evidence item build, admit, `REJECTED_OTHER`, end-of-loop skip labels, stats, emitted events, the candidate pool and `sourceLimits`.
  5. Comments only: `lib/article-selection.js`'s header and today's-mode comment now say which pieces are wired in; the test-file header likewise.
- **Check order inside the loop (proof, `lib/source-reader.js`):** before (`be8d65b`): stop rule 281-286 → count considered 287 → download 289-294 → error handling 295-333 → publish time 334-342 → horizon/window 343-353 → before/after window 354-365 → duplicate 366-370 → build item 371-390 → fit check 391-396 → admit 397-401. After: stop rule 290-294 → count considered 295 → download 297-302 → error handling 303-341 → publish time 342-350 → horizon/window 351-361 → before/after window 362-373 → duplicate 374-378 → build item 379-398 → fit check 399-413 → admit 414-418. Same order; every block between the stop rule and the fit check is unchanged text.
- **Why nothing changes:** `rankCandidates` and `staleByLabel` are pure and are called with the same inputs (the ranking now runs twice per read: once for the audit and pool, once inside `tierTryOrder`); the caps come from `settingValue` (attempts 6-20, admitted 0-10, always finite) and the byte limit is a finite number between 64 KB and 400 KB, so none of the shared pieces' input checks can fail on live inputs; the byte length is a whole number, so "over the limit" and "not within the limit" are the same test.
- **Not connected (unchanged on purpose):** the CNBC hand-off loop and the provisional CNBC values wait for Step 9F.6d. `lib/cnbc-*.js`, `lib/reading-window.js` (`createReadingBudget`, `downloadWithRetries`, `readingSettings`), `timed`, `monotonicNow` and `sleep` are not edited (a read-only export of `be8d65b` matches the working tree for every file except `lib/source-reader.js` and `lib/article-selection.js`).
- **Could not share:** the request building and byte measuring stay in the reader (`classifierRequestBytes`), because they need the reader's evidence, horizons and market context; the audit and pool still call `rankCandidates` directly, because they need every ranked row, dropped ones included, in the reader's own order; the translation from positions back to ranked rows stays in the reader.
- **Numbers and settings:** none added. No new setting, number, log event or selection summary; every existing log event keeps the same fields, order and counts. No `?rev=` token changed; nothing is stored in Neon; the Step 9F.1f temporary writer trim stays; the candidate audit stays in logs only.
- **Tests:** full suite 1271/1271 (1269 before; 2 new). `tests/article-selection.test.js`: the Step 9F.2e.3.2 import test is replaced by "only the orchestration (`checkListOrderFit`) and the reader (`tierTryOrder`, `createReaderStopRule`, `checkListOrderFit`) import the selection step, and nothing under `api/`", which checks both exact import lines; new: a candidate object listed twice breaks the position mapping (impossible for Yahoo today; documents the limit). `tests/source-reader.test.js`, new: every audit decision (14), every audit field (`labelOld`, `attempts`, `retriesStoppedByBudget`, `failureType`, `publishedAt`, `afterClose`, `scoreDrop`) and every count in the reading summary is still produced, `labelOld` sits before the download fields, and only the budget stop sets `readingBudgetStopped`. Syntax check over `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js`: 60/60. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls; old tree = read-only `git show` export of `be8d65b`, new tree = the working tree; each stage in its own process under 5 minutes, progress written to a file):**
  1. Strict core (`reader-stages.js core`, using `strict-core-9F.2e.3.4b.js`: the selection step's records split by mode so the reader's SKIP calls do not shift the hand-off labels; clock reads and sleep calls added to the comparison): the 48 core runs (8 packages) strictly identical, 48/48, plus 6/6 on the newer `package-PRE-2026-10-12.json`: raw diagnostics, assembled package, writer-fitted package, pools, classifier and writer requests, download order, recap calls, builder call count and input hashes, clock reads, sleep calls.
  2. Reader scenarios (all old vs new identical): (1) 12 failed downloads: EXERCISED on all 9 packages (12 attempts, `SKIPPED_MAX_ATTEMPTS`); (2) quick admits: EXERCISED on all 9 (6 admitted, `SKIPPED_MAX_ADMITTED`); (3) step clock (4 step sizes × 3 profiles × 9 packages): 108/108 identical, clock-read counts equal in every run, EXERCISED in 72 (`SKIPPED_READING_BUDGET`, budget stopped); (4) old labels mixed with fresh: EXERCISED on all 9 (old-label articles downloaded after the fresh ones); (5) size misfit mid-list with later articles still tried, `CLASSIFIER_REQUEST_LIMIT_BYTES=65536` in child processes, 200 pairs: 200/200 identical, NOT EXERCISED — the reader keeps at most the first 8 KB of each article and admits at most 6, so the request peaks near 50 KB and never crosses 64 KB with these packages (the same-answer script covers this branch); (6) forced builder error at every call N (same N on both trees): 248/248 identical, the reader's fit check turned the forced error into a misfit in 108 runs; (7) no publish time: EXERCISED on all 9; no horizon in a completed package: EXERCISED (6 runs); active "no session window": NOT EXERCISED by the packages (33/33 identical), covered by the same-answer script.
  3. Same-answer script (`reader-same-answer.js`): both trees' real `readSource`, builder wrapped before loading; 10,000 generated cases in 5 processes of 2,000 with byte limits 64 KB, 96 KB, about 146 KB, 300 KB and 400 KB; 500 with the real builder over the saved packages' telemetry (it never threw on its own), 9,500 with a fast size stand-in whose scale moves the effective limit per case: 0 differences in emitted events (byte for byte), admitted items (the caller's own array returned, same order, same content), horizons, pool, page-read order, timing stages, sleep calls, clock-read count, builder call count and input hashes. All 7 targets EXERCISED: attempts cap 2,685 cases, admitted cap 1,364, reading budget 2,911, old label downloaded last 1,768, size misfit then a later admit 478, measuring error as misfit 101, no publish time or no horizon 2,556. Coverage: 6,665 active, 3,335 completed, pre-check off 1,986, decimal caps 4,416, forced builder error 1,016, empty list 384, active no session window 545, completed no close 269, window null 322, retries stopped by the budget 477, tier 4a 8,129, duplicates 1,154, item-build throws 1,751.
  4. Excluded (the pipeline cannot produce them): negative, non-finite or missing caps (the settings table clamps them; the new stop rule would throw where the old loop never stopped); the same candidate object listed twice; non-object candidates; a mode that does not match the package's market state; duplicate links among the required items; a non-empty admitted list at the start. Allowed although not live, as asked: a null reading window (invalid trigger time), caps below the attempts safe minimum, garbage publish-time strings.
  5. Self-test (`reader-same-answer-selftest.js`, 1,000 cases each): all 8 planted mistakes caught — budget checked before the caps (390 cases differ), a second stopwatch (1,000), try-last ignored (391), tier 4a not dropped (476), base count off by one (46), a stop instead of a skip after a size misfit (293), every stop reason setting the budget flag (385), `labelOld` set after the loop (185, key order).
- **What these checks cannot prove:** real Yahoo pages (stand-in listings and bodies), real timing (the clock and sleep are stubbed), and real Claude output (only request bodies are built). Needs one live Preview run in an active state and one in a completed state, checking that the Yahoo reading summary and candidate audit keep their fields and order, 6 articles admitted or 12 attempts made, old-label articles tried last, and no new log event.
- **Must not change:** everything under Steps 9F.2a to 9F.2e.3.2's "Must not change", except the clause superseded below. In addition: the reader stays in SKIP mode with today's limits; one budget object drives both the stop rule and the download retries; `labelOld` is set before the loop; the shared pieces return positions only and callers keep their own objects; the CNBC hand-off loop waits for Step 9F.6d.
- **Supersedes:** Step 9F.2e.3.2 approval (1) and its import test, "only `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` may import `lib/article-selection.js`, and only `checkListOrderFit`": now the orchestration (`checkListOrderFit`) and `lib/source-reader.js` (`tierTryOrder`, `createReaderStopRule`, `checkListOrderFit`) may import it, and nothing under `api/` (Kevin, 12 Oct 2026).

## Step 9F.2e.4a — The Yahoo reader tries articles by score, behind an Admin switch (reports change while it is on) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-13 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. With the switch on (the default) the order the reader tries Yahoo articles in changes, so which articles reach the report can change; with it off, everything is byte-identical to Step 9F.2e.3.4b.
- **Evidence (12 Oct 2026, first live Pre-market run of the Step 9 work, Step 9F.2e.3.4a code):** the reader tried Yahoo articles in tier order, which puts every headline naming a stock on My Stocks or the Watchlist first. All 6 downloads were Nvidia or AMD single-company stories; Section 3 came out blank and the report had no macro or weekend news. The logged shadow scores already ranked the Goldman global-stocks story, the European shares and dollar wire stories and the oil stories above most of those single-company pieces.
- **Kevin's approvals (13 Oct 2026, from the read-only planning session for Step 9F.2e.4):**
  1. An Admin on/off switch for score ordering, on by default. Off gives today's behaviour, byte-identical.
  2. Lower points for AI-theme headlines are NOT in this step (Step 9F.2e.4b, later).
  3. A general AI story with no other market word may miss the 6 picks: accepted.
  4. Ties: higher score first, then the newer article, then listing position. An article with no age label counts as the oldest within its score. This rule lives in the new function only; `compareScoredArticles` is not changed (unwired code still uses it).
  5. Build in two parts (4a, 4b); one live checkpoint after both. No live checks in this step.
- **Decision:**
  1. `lib/article-selection.js`: new `scoreTryOrder`, same inputs and same return shape as `tierTryOrder` (`order`, `dropped`, `tiers`, `oldLabelPositions`); `tierTryOrder` is unchanged. The dropped positions and the tiers come from the same `rankCandidates` call (tier 4a), so the same headlines keep `SKIPPED_BELOW_RELEVANCE_FLOOR`. Every other candidate is scored with `scoreArticle` on the same inputs the shadow score uses (headline, publisher and age label; both lists via `portfolioIdentitiesFrom(stockSnapshots)`; Most Active; the trigger time), so recency before download comes from the age label only ("53m ago" = 0.88 h, "12h ago" = 12 h, "1d ago" = exactly 24 h and 0 points, "2d ago" = 48 h). The old-label split is the same `staleByLabel` test: the try-first group in score order, then the try-last group in score order. Ties as approval 4; a missing or unreadable label counts as the oldest within its score.
  2. `lib/source-reader.js`: `readSource` reads the switch once per run (`settingValue('scoreTryOrderEnabled', {env})`, with a new optional `env` input that defaults to `process.env`; the orchestration does not pass it) and calls `scoreTryOrder` when it is on, otherwise the same `tierTryOrder` call as before. Nothing else in the reading loop changes: download loop, stop rule (12 attempts, 6 admitted, 30 second budget), per-article fit check, every audit decision and count, the window checks.
  3. `lib/settings-registry.js`: one new row, `scoreTryOrderEnabled` (boolean, Admin, env `SCORE_TRY_ORDER_ENABLED`, default on, wired), read with the existing on/off clamp (true/1/on, false/0/off, anything else the default).
  4. Logs, switch on only: `tryOrder: 'SCORE'` in the Yahoo reading summary right after `staleByLabelCount`; `tryRank` (1-based place in the try order) in each candidate-audit entry right after `rank` (which stays the tier rank); dropped rows get none. No new events. With the switch off neither field appears.
- **Tests:** full suite 1285/1285 (1271 before; 14 new).
