New `tests/fixtures/yahoo-listings.js`: the 12 Oct listing (37 logged rows, the 3 drop-rule rows and 34 rows scoring 10 or less, two old-labelled: 74 candidates), the harness's REAL HEADLINES list and the no-material package's headlines and lists. `tests/article-selection.test.js`: same shape and same dropped set, tiers and try-last set as the tier order; the 12 Oct order; the score's drop rule equals the tier drop rule on all three listings; the REAL HEADLINES top six; ties (newer first, then listing position, a missing or unreadable label oldest, "1d ago" = 24 h); a full tie goes by listing position, not tier rank; old-label rows last in score order; `compareScoredArticles` keeps its own tie rule; the import test now lets `lib/source-reader.js` also import `scoreTryOrder`. `tests/source-reader.test.js`: the 12 Oct listing opened by score with the switch on and by tier with it off; switch default and clamping; `tryOrder` and `tryRank` present (in place) when on, absent when off, every other audit field unchanged; a 74-candidate audit with long fields stays under 3400 bytes per event, on and off. `tests/settings-registry.test.js`: the new row. Three Step 8K.1/8K.5/9F.1c orchestration tests and one Step 9F.2e.1 reader test now run with the switch off (they check the tier order) and each also checks the score order with it on. Syntax check over `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js`: 60/60. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **12 Oct fixture result:** every logged score is reproduced except row 22 ("Oil Prices Decline as Investors Await US Inflation Data and Bank Earnings Amid Gulf tensions"): as listed it has no generic word and scores 24, not the logged 25. Score order, first 6: rows 1, 3, 5, 6, 4, 7 (the Goldman market-wide story sixth): 1 market-wide, 1 sector, 4 single-company (today: rows 1-6, 0 market-wide). Top 10 adds rows 2, 8, 9 and 33 (with the logged 25 for row 22 it would be row 22, giving 4 market-wide, 1 sector, 5 single-company, and row 25 twelfth; as listed, row 25 is eleventh and row 22 fourteenth).
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls; old tree = read-only `git archive` export of `7945f15`; new tree = the working tree; package names matched case-insensitively so `Package-CLOSED-2026-10-11.json` is included; each stage in its own process under 5 minutes, progress written to a file):**
  1. **Switch off:** the 48 core runs 48/48 strictly identical, plus 12/12 on the two newer packages; reader scenarios all identical: attempts cap 10/10, admitted cap 10/10, step clock 120/120, old labels 10/10, size misfit 200/200, forced builder error 274/274, no publish time / no horizon / no window 36/36 (raw diagnostics, package, requests, download order, audit events, summary, clock reads, sleeps, builder hashes). The old-vs-new generated-case script with the switch off: 10,000 cases, 0 differences (the same script finds 300/300 different with the switch on). `tierTryOrder` unchanged on every package.
  2. **Switch on, order only:** the old tree's reader with its `tierTryOrder` swapped for the new `scoreTryOrder` before it loads, against the new tree: every core and scenario run identical apart from `tryOrder` and `tryRank` (events, audit rows, package, pools, requests, download order, clock reads, sleeps, builder hashes); 10,000 generated cases, 0 differences. Adding `tryRank` split the audit into one more part in 8 package runs and 89 generated cases (allowed; every event stayed under 3400 bytes). The download order really changed from today's in 40/40 core runs (package evidence in 11/40).
  3. **Same answer** (`reader-same-answer-9F.2e.4a.js`): 10,000 generated listings (macro, AI-theme, portfolio, Most Active, drop, "how to", Asia/Europe and generic words; wire and other publishers; missing, fresh, old and unreadable labels; twins for full ties) against an independent reference (drop the tier 4a rows; sort by score, then newer, then listing position, no label oldest; old-label rows at the back): 0 differences in the order, the dropped set, the old-label positions, the reader's `tryRank`, the reader's download order (switch on) and the tier download order (switch off). The score's drop set equalled the tier drop set in every case. Coverage: 9,082 cases where the score order differs from the tier order, 7,719 with dropped rows, 8,866 with old-label rows, 62,241 ties settled by age, 27,269 involving an unknown age, 74,905 by listing position.
  4. **Self-test:** all 7 planted mistakes caught (500 cases each): older-first ties, tier rank instead of listing position, old-label rows not last, a dropped row tried, switch off but score order used, the unknown-age ordering loop (the `compareScoredArticles` rule), the unknown-age rule missing.
- **What these checks cannot prove:** real Yahoo listings, pages and timing, and real Claude output. The live checkpoint (after Step 9F.2e.4b) should check, in an active and a completed state: `tryOrder: 'SCORE'` in the reading summary; `tryRank` on every non-dropped audit row, with the first downloads matching `tryRank` 1, 2, 3...; the same `SKIPPED_BELOW_RELEVANCE_FLOOR` rows as the scores' `scoreDrop`; old-label rows still last; 6 admitted or 12 attempts; market-wide and wire stories among the first downloads; Section 3 no longer blank for lack of macro news; and that one run with `SCORE_TRY_ORDER_ENABLED=off` gives the old order with no new fields.
- **Not touched:** `lib/cnbc-*.js`, the CNBC hand-off loop, `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`, `monotonicNow`, `sleep`, `compareScoredArticles`, `lib/article-score.js`, Section 4 rules, recaps, the Fed lane and the writer. No `?rev=` token changed; nothing stored in Neon; the candidate audit stays in logs only; the temporary writer trim stays. No AI-theme points (Step 9F.2e.4b).
- **Numbers and settings:** exactly one new setting (`scoreTryOrderEnabled`); no other new setting or number.
- **Could not share:** the translation from try-order positions back to the reader's ranked rows, and the `tryRank` labelling, stay in the reader (they need its audit rows); the score is computed a second time inside `scoreTryOrder` (the reader's shadow score is computed per ranked row for the audit and the pool, the try order works per listing position).
- **Must not change:** everything under Step 9F.2e.3.4b's "Must not change", except the clauses superseded below. In addition: with the switch off the reader behaves exactly as Step 9F.2e.3.4b; the dropped set always comes from the tier ranking (tier 4a); old-label rows always go last; the tie rule above stays in `scoreTryOrder` only.
- **Supersedes:**
  - Step 9F.2d.1 / 9F.2e.1 "the reader ranks, tries and admits by tier until Step 9F.2e.4": while the switch is on, the reader tries by score; dropping still follows the tier ranking and the audit `rank` stays the tier rank.
  - Step 9F.2c.2 "`compareScoredArticles` stays uncalled": it still is; the reader's score order uses its own tie rule (approval 4), in which a missing age label counts as the oldest within its score.
  - Step 9F.1c "label-old candidates are tried last, in their usual order (rank order kept within each group)": while the switch is on, the usual order inside each group is score order.
  - Step 8K.5 tier order as the try order: while the switch is on, the tier order no longer sets the order articles are tried in (it still sets the dropped rows and the audit rank).

## Step 9F.2e.4b — Lower points for AI-only headlines; the "not wired" ranking-point/recency labels corrected (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-13 · **Branch:** step-9f2d-selection · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. This step CHANGES scores and therefore which articles are picked, for AI-only headlines, whenever `scoreTryOrderEnabled` is on (the default).
- **Evidence (12 Oct 2026, first live Pre-market run, Step 9F.2e.4a code):** the 6 articles tried first were all Nvidia or AMD single-company stories. Step 9F.2e.4a made the reader try by score, but score order alone barely helped: Nvidia and AMD are on the US macro list (20 points), and each also earns Most Active (12) and portfolio (8) points for the same company, so a single company could be counted three times.
- **Kevin's approvals (from the read-only planning session, evidence above):**
  1. A headline whose ONLY matched macro term(s) are AI-theme words (AI, Artificial Intelligence, OpenAI, Anthropic, Nvidia, AMD) scores the lower AI-theme value instead of the full macro value. This revisits the Step 9F.2c.1 (3 Oct 2026) decision that these five/six words are macro words worth 20 in every case, and the later "keep AI at 20 for now" decision.
  2. Default AI-theme value: 8 (options considered: 0, 5, 8, 12, 20 — see the fixture results below). Admin-editable, safe range 0-20.
  3. Accepted side effect: a general AI-bubble story with no other market word will usually still miss the 6 picks (the AI-theme value is below the benchmark/generic and Asia/Europe points' combined pull in most headlines, same as today).
  4. AI-theme words keep protecting a headline from the drop rule by match, not by points, so protection holds even at AI-theme value 0.
  5. Exactly one new numeric setting (`rankingPointsAiTheme`); one new word list (`AI_THEME_TERMS`, a subset of `US_MACRO_TERMS`, not a new list of its own words).
- **Decision:**
  1. `lib/headline-lists.js`: new `AI_THEME_TERMS` — `{term:'AI',caseSensitive:true}`, `{term:'Artificial Intelligence'}`, `{term:'OpenAI'}`, `{term:'Anthropic'}`, `{term:'Nvidia'}`, `{term:'AMD'}` — each copied verbatim (spelling and `caseSensitive`) from the matching `US_MACRO_TERMS` entry. All six planned words were already present on `US_MACRO_TERMS` (added in Step 9F.2c.1); nothing else was added. `lib/headline-matching.js` exposes `AI_THEME_TERMS_SET`, a term-identity lookup (by the macro term's own `term` string) used only to pick the AI-theme-vs-macro point value; the matching regexes themselves are unchanged.
  2. `lib/article-score.js` `scoreArticle`: collects every `US_MACRO_PATTERNS` entry that matches the headline (same shared matcher, same AMD/dollar exclusions as today). Empty set: 0 macro points, as today (`matched.usMacro = matched.ai = 0`). At least one matched term outside `AI_THEME_TERMS`: the full `rankingPointsUsMacro` value, as today (`matched.usMacro`, `matched.ai = 0`). Non-empty and every matched term is AI-theme: `rankingPointsAiTheme` instead, in `matched.ai` (`matched.usMacro = 0`) — unless that value equals `rankingPointsUsMacro`, in which case it is scored and labeled exactly as the macro case (`matched.usMacro`, `matched.ai = 0`), so a 20/20 setup reproduces Step 9F.2e.4a's logs byte for byte. Either/or, never both. Drop-rule protection (`isProtected`) now also checks "any macro term matched" directly (`macroMatched`), not just `matched.usMacro > 0`, so an AI-theme-only headline is protected even when `rankingPointsAiTheme` is 0. Maximum scores (54 without recency, 58 with) are unchanged: `ai` and `usMacro` are mutually exclusive and `ai`'s own safe range (0-20) does not exceed the macro default. `lib/article-selection.js` `scoreTryOrder` gained an optional `env` input (default `undefined`, which `scoreArticle` already treats as "use `process.env`"), threaded through to its internal `scoreArticle` call; `lib/source-reader.js` now passes its own `env` input to both its shadow-score `scoreArticle` call and its `scoreTryOrder`/`tierTryOrder` call (previously neither read the reader's `env` input, only `process.env` directly — a gap that did not matter while every ranking-point/recency default already matched Kevin's specified values, but would have silently ignored any future Admin override of those settings; this is a wiring fix, not a behaviour change at today's defaults). `lib/source-reader.js` `SCORE_PART_LABELS` gained `ai: 'ai'`, so the candidate audit's score-parts text shows e.g. `"ai 8"` instead of `"macro 20"` for an AI-only headline.
  3. `lib/settings-registry.js`: one new row, `rankingPointsAiTheme` (numeric, Admin, env `RANKING_POINTS_AI_THEME`, default 8, safe range 0-20, `wired: false` like its neighbours), plus one new reference row, `headlineListAiThemeTerms`, pointing at `AI_THEME_TERMS` (same style as the other word-list reference rows). Exactly one new numeric setting, confirmed by `tests/article-score-ai-theme.test.js`.
  4. **"Not wired" labels (checked, not changed):** the `wired` field is read only inside `lib/settings-registry.js` itself (`numericSetting`/`booleanSetting`/`fixedSetting`/`referenceSetting` construction) — nothing in the pipeline reads `.wired` at runtime; it exists purely as documentation, checked by `tests/settings-registry.test.js` (which asserts specific `wired` booleans per setting, and that at least 30 settings are unwired). Flipping the 7 ranking-point and 5 recency settings' `wired` field from `false` to `true` would still leave the unwired count at exactly 30 (it is currently 43: 28 explicit + 15 reference-setting defaults), so the count assertion would still pass, but by a margin of exactly 1 — a single future addition or removal elsewhere in the table could flip it either way with no connection to this step's change. Per the task's own rule ("if changing it could change any behaviour, including the settings reference tests, leave the flag as it is and tell me"), the `wired` field is left at `false` on all 12 settings; told here. Their **descriptions** are documentation only (no test matches description text for these rows) and are corrected: each now says it is read live by `lib/article-score.js` and steers the reader's try order whenever `scoreTryOrderEnabled` is on, with a `location` pointing at the real call chain. The stale "NOT wired... nothing in the pipeline calls this yet" header comment in `lib/article-score.js`, and the stale "not-yet-wired shared article score" comment in `lib/headline-matching.js`, are also corrected (comment text only).
- **Tests:** full suite 1316/1316 (1286 before; 30 new, all in new `tests/article-score-ai-theme.test.js`). Fixed fallout from the new `ai` field on `scoreArticle`'s `matched` object and from `rankingPointsAiTheme`'s new default (8, not 20) in 7 pre-existing tests across `tests/article-score.test.js`, `tests/article-selection.test.js` and `tests/source-reader.test.js`: added `ai: 0` to expected `matched`/`scoreBreakdown` objects where the headline has no AI-theme-only match, and set `RANKING_POINTS_AI_THEME: '20'` explicitly on the Step 9F.2e.4a fixture tests that assert the AI-at-20 result (`scoreTryOrder`'s 12 Oct and REAL HEADLINES order tests, and the reader's switch-on/default-value tests), per the task's instruction; their expectations are otherwise unchanged. New tests cover: the word list (exactly the six planned words, each a verbatim copy); the new setting's default/range/clamping; an AI-only headline at AI-theme 0/5/8/12/20 (mutual exclusivity, the 20/20 macro-label equivalence); drop protection at every value including 0; AI-plus-a-real-macro-word (earnings, Fed, S&P 500, oil) always scoring macro, never AI-theme; the AMD/eye-disease exclusion combined with a remaining AI-theme term; a no-macro-at-all headline; maximum scores unchanged at every AI-theme value; the score's drop set equalling the tier drop set at every value; the either/or rule holding over the whole 12 Oct listing; and the 12 Oct fixture's ranked order at AI-theme 20 (reproduces Step 9F.2e.4a's top six exactly) and at 8/5/12 (see fixture result below). Syntax check over `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js`: 60/60. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls, no live network calls.
- **12 Oct fixture result (row 22, the InvestorsHub oil headline, scored via a test-only stand-in headline with one added generic-market word reproducing its logged score of 25, per the task; the fixture row's own text and the Step 9F.2e.4a expectations are untouched):**
  - AI-theme 20 (today): top 6 = rows 1, 3, 5, 6, 4, 7 (1 M, 1 S, 4 C) — matches Step 9F.2e.4a exactly.
  - AI-theme 8 (default): top 6 = rows 1, 3, 7, 8, 9, 5 (3 M, 1 S, 2 C); top 10 adds 6, 33, 22, 25 (5 M, 2 S, 3 C); Goldman (row 7), European shares (8), dollar (9) and the oil row (22) rank 3, 4, 5, 8 — matches Kevin's expected numbers exactly.
  - AI-theme 5: top 6 = rows 1, 7, 3, 8, 9, 22 (4 M, 1 S, 1 C); those four rank 2, 4, 5, 6 — matches exactly.
  - AI-theme 12: top 6 = rows 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 (2 M, 1 S, 3 C); those four rank 5, 6, 7, 8 — matches exactly.
- **Full proof harness (run; old tree = read-only `git archive` export of `94d461f`; new tree = the working tree; scratch only, not committed, no network, no paid calls; scripts and outputs copied into `Replay Packages\harness`):**
  1. **Stage 1** (AI-theme 20, switch OFF and switch ON, strict `compareRuns`, no exceptions): 48 core runs + 12 extra (both modes) fully byte-identical, including the candidate pool's `scoreBreakdown` — the new `ai` key is present but exactly 0 everywhere, since AI-theme 20 reproduces the macro case byte for byte (`stage1-output-9F.2e.4b.txt`).
  2. **Stage 2** (AI-theme 8, the default, switch OFF): 48 core + 12 extra byte-identical EXCEPT the candidate audit's `score`/`scoreParts` text and the candidate pool's `score`/`scoreBreakdown.usMacro`/`.ai` values, and ONLY on AI-only headlines (32 pool rows, 2 distinct headlines in the core set: "Microsoft's Trillion-Dollar Quarter..." and the Nvidia/Broadcom/AMD story) — every such difference listed and confirmed AI-only by the new tree's own matcher; nothing else differs (`stage2-output-9F.2e.4b.txt`).
  3. **Stage 3** (score comparison, old tree's `scoreArticle` vs. the new tree's at AI-theme 0/5/8/12/20, over every headline available — 35 fixture rows, 32 real package headlines, the 74-candidate 12 Oct fixture, the 28-headline REAL HEADLINES scenario, the 3 no-material headlines: 172 total): 0 tier mismatches, 0 drop-set mismatches, 0 drop-protection failures at AI-theme 0, 0 non-AI-only score changes; 29 unique headlines changed score, every one AI-only (`score-compare-9F.2e.4b-output.txt`).
  4. **Stage 4** (AI-theme 8, switch ON, order only: the old tree's `scoreTryOrder` swapped for the new tree's before its reader loads, same technique Step 9F.2e.4a used one level up): 48 core + 12 extra identical apart from the same, listed AI-only pool/audit fields as Stage 2 — confirming `tryOrder`/`tryRank` themselves come out byte-identical (both trees use the literal same swapped-in function), so the ONLY effect of this step's code beyond the AI-theme split is the display fields (`stage4-output-9F.2e.4b.txt`).
  5. **Stage 5** (same-answer script, 10,000 generated listings split evenly across AI-theme 0/5/8/12/20, checks A order / B drop-set / C reader-switch-on / D reader-switch-off against an independent reference): 0 differences. EXERCISED counts: AI-only headline 73,510; AI-plus-real-macro-word 28,721; exclusion case (AMD eye-disease/dollar-store phrases) 30,999; value 0 exercised in all 2,000 of its cases (`reader-same-answer-9F.2e.4b-output.txt`).
  6. **Self-test** (8 planted mistakes): all 8 caught. 6 by the same-answer script or Stage 3's score-compare script (AI points added on top of macro; AI-only headline still given macro 20; AI points given when a real macro word is present; drop protection lost at value 0; the AMD exclusion bypassed; the maximum score exceeded; the Step 9F.2e.4a tie-ordering rule changed — 7 named here, the 8th below). 2 mistakes change only which field the points land in, never the total score, so no score-sum check (same-answer OR score-compare) can see them; a direct in-process check added for exactly that reason catches both: an AI-only headline still scoring macro 20 (not AI-theme 8) at the default value, and the 20/20 case showing `"ai 20"` instead of `"macro 20"` (`reader-same-answer-9F.2e.4b-selftest-output.txt`).
  7. **The size-failure reader scenario (5):** run for real (not skipped) at `CLASSIFIER_REQUEST_LIMIT_BYTES=65536` across all 4 modes: byte-identical every time, but NOT EXERCISED in any run — this exactly matches the Step 9F.2e.4a precedent (also NOT EXERCISED in 100/100 pairs on both of its runs), so it is a pre-existing characteristic of this check against these saved packages, confirmed unchanged by this step, not a gap this step introduced (`reader-stages-9F.2e.4b-s5-output.txt`). A same-answer-script version of this check was attempted (to use the task's own leave-it-out allowance) and removed: the classifier request builder's validation requires a real five-session benchmark snapshot and a non-null completed-session date, which this script's generated cases never carry (only real saved packages do), so the check could only throw, never measure — it would have reported a fake "always rejected" result, not a real signal, so it was not kept.
  8. **The one approved, documented exception (Kevin's decision, this step):** read-only, confirmed first: the candidate pool (`lib/source-reader.js` `candidatePool`) is kept in memory only — never logged, never put in the assembled package, never sent in the classifier or writer request, never written to Neon; its only consumer is the `onCandidatePool` test hook, never wired from `lib/analysis-package-runtime.js` or any `api/*.js` file. The harness comparison (never production code) ignores the pool's new `scoreBreakdown.ai` key ONLY when it is exactly 0, or (Stages 2/4 only) when the headline is AI-only and the usual either/or arithmetic holds; every other field, and a non-zero `ai` anywhere else, still fails the comparison. Every use of the exception across all four stages is listed in its output file, never silently absorbed.
- **What these checks cannot prove:** real Yahoo listings, pages and timing; real Claude output; the size-failure reader scenario's REJECTED-then-ADMITTED pattern specifically (confirmed not reachable with these saved packages, before or after this step). The live checkpoint should check, at AI-theme 8 (the default): the candidate audit's score-parts text shows `"ai 8"` (not `"macro 20"`) for an AI-only headline; a market-wide or wire story is now reachable among the first downloads even with Nvidia/AMD stories present; Section 3 is not blank for lack of macro news; and a run with `RANKING_POINTS_AI_THEME=20` reproduces Step 9F.2e.4a's order exactly.
- **Not touched:** `lib/cnbc-*.js`, the CNBC hand-off loop, `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`, `monotonicNow`, `sleep`. No `?rev=` token changed; nothing stored in Neon; the candidate audit stays in logs only. Section 4 rules, recaps, the Fed lane and the writer. `compareScoredArticles`, the try-order function (`scoreTryOrder`'s ordering logic itself, only its inputs gained an `env` pass-through), every other word list, tier, drop rule, cap, retry and size. `DROP_PROTECTING_LISTS`'s own five names (unchanged; protection for AI-theme-only headlines is additional, via `macroMatched`, not a sixth name added to that list).
- **Numbers and settings:** exactly one new numeric setting (`rankingPointsAiTheme`, default 8, range 0-20); one new reference row (`headlineListAiThemeTerms`); no other new or changed setting value. The `wired` field of the 12 pre-existing ranking-point/recency settings is unchanged (left `false`); only their `description`/`location` text changed.
- **Supersedes:**
  - Step 9F.2c.1 (3 Oct 2026) "AI, Artificial Intelligence, OpenAI, Anthropic, Nvidia and AMD are macro words worth 20 in every case": now true only when a non-AI-theme macro term also matched, or when `rankingPointsAiTheme` is set equal to `rankingPointsUsMacro`.
  - The later "keep AI at 20 for now" decision: superseded by the evidence above; AI-theme now defaults to 8.
  - Step 9F.2e.4a approval 2 ("lower points for AI-theme headlines are NOT in this step"): done in this step.
- **Must not change:** everything under Step 9F.2e.4a's "Must not change" and "Supersedes", except as superseded above. In addition: `usMacro` and `ai` are never both positive on the same headline; AI-theme protection is by match, not by points; the six AI-theme words stay a subset of `US_MACRO_TERMS`, never a separate list with its own matching rules.

## Step 9C.1 — Shared proof kit, raw writer reply saving, free replay test (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 15 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9-integration · **Status:** COMMITTED. No report behaviour change (this repo's `api`/`lib` are byte-for-byte unchanged by this step).
- **Context:** every step touching the US analysis pipeline had repeated 2-3 hours of one-off proof work: a bespoke `compare*.js`/`same-answer*.js`/`selftest*.js` trio copied and tweaked per step. `Replay Packages\harness` already held a validated lineage of these scripts, but running the full standard proof for a step still meant finding, chaining and re-reading several of them by hand. Separately, nothing anywhere (repo or harness) had ever saved a raw Claude writer reply -- every `package-*.json`/`.pdf` pair in `Replay Packages` was produced by hand through the live app, so there was no way to replay a past writer reply for free.
- **Decision:** built, all outside the repo in `Replay Packages\harness` (no overwrites of existing files, no per-state copies):
  - `proof.js` -- one command, 5 sequential stages (no parallel scenarios), each a child process with a 5-minute timeout, logging to a progress file: (1) `compare.js` unmodified, full-pipeline byte-identical across every saved package, plus `proof.js`'s own case-insensitive, typo-tolerant day-type inventory (independent of `compare.js`'s own case-sensitive glob); (2) `reader-same-answer-9F.2e.4b.js` with a configurable case count; (3) `reader-same-answer-9F.2e.4b-selftest.js all` (8 planted mistakes, must all be caught); (4) new `paid-replay-selftest.js` (proves the raw-reply save/no-save logic, no network, no key); (5) new `free-replay-selftest.js` (proves a saved raw reply replays deterministically, no network). Stage 2/3's scripts are swappable per future step via `--same-answer-script`/`--same-answer-selftest-script`; everything else is step-agnostic.
  - `paid-replay.js` -- the first writer-step harness tooling. Calls the repo's real `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js` `invokeClaudeAnalysis` (required read-only, never edited) against a saved package through the exact production request/validation code, with a real network call; wraps `fetchImpl` (via `.clone()`) to capture the real upstream reply without altering what `invokeClaudeAnalysis` sees, and saves it next to the package as `<package-basename>.writer-raw.json` -- only when the upstream call returned a parseable 2xx body (nothing is saved on a network failure or non-2xx status). The key comes only from `process.env.MB_PAID_REPLAY_API_KEY` (the capped Haiku-only Anthropic key), never printed or written to a file.
  - `free-replay.js` -- loads a saved `*.writer-raw.json` and calls the same `invokeClaudeAnalysis` with a stand-in `fetchImpl` that returns the saved envelope instead of hitting the network, so the full post-processing path (transport conversion, reference localization, section rules, the one-retry-on-CONTRACT_FAILURE logic) runs against a real past reply, deterministically, for $0.
  - `synthetic-writer-reply.js` -- builds a minimal-but-real transport (grounded Section 1 citing one of a package's own market-data evidence refs, every other section DEGRADED/null) used only by the two self-tests above, to exercise the real conversion/validation path before any real paid reply exists; never presented as a real Claude reply.
- **No paid call made in this step:** `paid-replay.js` was built and tested only against a stand-in `fetchImpl` (`paid-replay-selftest.js`). Kevin runs the first real paid replay himself, with the capped key, when ready.
- **Day-type package inventory (read-only, this step):** found in `Replay Packages`: PRE (5), POST/After-Hours (1), CLOSED (2), WEEKEND (2). **REGULAR (Trading) and HOLIDAY have no saved package** -- `proof.js` reports both MISSING, as designed, rather than skipping them.
- **Evidence/tests:** full backend suite 1316/1316 (unchanged -- no `tests/*.js` added or edited); syntax check `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js` 60/60 (unchanged file count); `git diff --check` clean. Validated `proof.js` run (old tree == new tree == working tree, since this step makes no `lib`/`api` change): all 5 stages PASS, total 4m02s, well inside the 45-minute target (`proof-9C.1-output.txt`, progress log `proof-progress.txt`, both in `Replay Packages\harness`). Stage 4/5 self-tests exercise 3 and 2 cases respectively (success/upstream-failure/network-throw; two deterministic free-replay runs) -- all passing, no network.
- **Numbers (harness-only CLI parameters, not product/Admin settings; nothing in the repo or its settings table changed):** `--cases` (same-answer case count): default 2000, range 500-10000, fixed CLI flag, overridable per run. `--seed`: fixed constant default, overridable per run. Per-stage timeout: 300s (5 min), fixed, not overridable. `MB_PAID_REPLAY_API_KEY`: harness-only env var, unset by default, never logged or persisted.
- **Must not change:** CNBC acquisition code; `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`; any `?rev=` token; Neon/persistence code; every existing protected checkpoint listed in CLAUDE.md. This step touches none of them -- it only adds new files outside the repo plus this entry.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8P.2(b) + 8P.3 — Pre-market, Trading and After-Hours reports lead with what has happened since the last close (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 15 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-8p2b-since-close · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. With the switch on (the default) the writer request for PRE, REGULAR and POST changes, so report wording can change; with it off, the writer request is byte-identical to `9ef8c38`. Closed, weekend and holiday requests are byte-identical either way.
- **Why:** a Pre-market report read like a closing report. It said nothing about what had happened between the close and the run (this morning's yields, early moves, data due today). In a side-by-side test a normal chat summary covered this and MarketBrief did not. The articles were already admitted: since Step 8K.2 the active reading window starts at the last close, and in every saved Pre-market/After-Hours package every admitted Yahoo article is after the close. What was missing was in the writer instructions:
  1. the writer was never told the last close time, and no article was flagged as published after the close (only a raw UTC `publishedAt`);
  2. the shared base prompt is written for a completed day ("completed results", "closing-session breadth", "[Index] closed higher" style example), and the Step 8P.2(a) active sentence asks for the "live move" — but PRE packages carry no live prices (no `currentOverlay` in any saved package), so the only numbers the writer had were the close;
  3. the Step 8K.2 sentence "Items with ageHoursAtGeneration above 12 are overnight background ... not fresh news" labels a just-after-close article (about 14 hours old at a 17:00 SGT run) and a whole weekend's news as background.
- **Decision:**
  - One new Admin switch, `sinceCloseLeadEnabled` (env `SINCE_CLOSE_LEAD_ENABLED`), default **on**, in `lib/settings-registry.js`. Off = previous writer request, byte for byte.
  - `lib/us-active-session-evidence.js` (the shared active-session module) gains `sinceLastCloseContext(input)`: the last regular close (PRE and REGULAR: previous trading day's close; POST: today's close; always worked out with the V2 close rule, so a replayed V1 package still names the real close) and the admitted current-session Yahoo articles published at or after it. Nothing new is admitted; this is a subset of the existing current-session list. Also `exchangeTimeLabel` — a fixed ET text label ("Thu 15 Oct, 6:33 am ET") so the writer never converts a timestamp itself.
  - `lib/claude-model-input-projection.js`: with the switch on and an active request, a top-level `sinceLastClose` entry (`market`, `marketState`, `lastCloseEt`, `liveTelemetry`, `evidenceRefs`) and, on each listed article, `sinceLastClose: true` and `publishedEt`. Nothing else in the projection changes.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: one `sinceCloseLeadInstruction`, the same text for PRE, REGULAR and POST, **appended** after the existing active-session instruction (the Step 8P.2(a) sentence and the Step 8K.2 12-hour sentence are kept, not rewritten). It says: open Sections 1 and 2 with what has happened since the close, from those articles only, each with its `publishedEt` time; then the last close briefly, as "where we left off"; state no yields, futures, prices, percentages, data or causes unless a cited since-close article states them; when no live prices are supplied, never imply a pre-market/live/after-hours price or move (with a live overlay: live figures only from it); tie a cause to a move only when a cited since-close article states it and is a principal catalyst, with attribution ("according to [publisher]") — otherwise the move with no cause (8P.3); an after-close article over 12 hours old still counts as since the close but is not called new; Section 3 leads with companies and sectors in the news since the close (8P.4), all Section 3 rules unchanged; never present a since-close article as the cause of the earlier completed-session move. With no article after the close: Section 1 opens with one plain sentence saying so, then the last session as the latest picture.
  - No validator, section rule, localizer, Further Readings rule, classifier, acquisition or reading-window code changed.
- **Numbers:** one new setting, `sinceCloseLeadEnabled`: boolean, default on, Admin. No other new number. (The ET label format and the 12-hour figure in the instruction are not new settings: the format is fixed text, and 12 hours is the existing Step 8K.2 figure.)
- **Not shared / limits:** the since-close list covers only current-session Yahoo news articles (the same set that already feeds Further Readings); a Federal Reserve release published after the close is not flagged. Publish times are shown in exchange time (ET), not the user's display time zone. Extended-hours prices are still not supplied (a later step); the instruction forbids implying them. The writer request grows by about 2.2–2.6 KB on active days with articles (about 0.6 KB with none); the existing writer-budget trim still applies.
- **Proof (all free, no network, no paid call; old tree = `git archive` export of `9ef8c38`):**
  - 9C.1 kit, switch **off**: all 5 stages PASS (6m03s): stage 1 54/54 runs fully byte-identical; stage 2 same-answer PASS; stage 3 all planted mistakes caught; stages 4–5 PASS (`proof-8P2b-OFF-output.txt`, `proof-progress-8P2b-OFF.txt`).

## Step 8P.2(b).2 — since-close lead, second pass: firmer order, named sources (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 15 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-8p2b-since-close · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Same switch as Step 8P.2(b) (`sinceCloseLeadEnabled`, default on). Off: the writer request is byte-identical to `a9bdf3e`. Closed, weekend and holiday requests are byte-identical to `a9bdf3e` either way.
- **Why:** a real writer run on the saved 15 Oct Pre-market package (`pacakge-PRE-2026-10-15.writer-raw.json`) with the `a9bdf3e` wording showed: (1) Section 1 still opened with the full Wednesday close (all three indices, points and percentages) and only then "since then, on Thursday morning" — backwards; (2) Section 2 mixed since-close and close items with no time on the lead item; (3) Section 2 wrote "according to reports" with no named source; (4) Section 4 gave UnitedHealth's gain a cause ("investors rotated away from technology into healthcare") that appears only in the S&P 500 outlook article (cited in Sections 5 and 6), not in any article linked to UnitedHealth. Everything else passed (no invented numbers, Section 3 led with since-close news, time labels present on since-close items).
  - Why the first-pass wording could be read as optional: "Open Section 1 and Section 2 with what has happened since the last close" never said the *first sentence*, and "in Section 1 keep it to one or two sentences" let the close sit anywhere. The shared base prompt still pulls the other way — its style example opens with "[Index A] closed [higher/lower]", Section 1 asks for "the distinction between completed results and developing conditions", and the index-movement pattern is required "in the Executive Market Summary". The attribution example "according to [publisher]" was satisfied in form by "according to reports". Nothing tied a Section 4 cause to the stock's own linked article.
  - Why the UnitedHealth cause passed the checks (read-only finding, no check changed): the causal-claim rule (rule 16, `CAUSAL_CLAIM_CATALYST_REQUIRED`, Sections 1 and 3–7) (a) does not treat "as investors rotated ... into healthcare" as causal wording — its detector returns false on that sentence — and (b) works per section, not per stock: Section 4 cited `e31`/`e32`/`e34` (Nvidia's linked articles, all principal catalysts, all in the Section 4 allowlist), which would satisfy any causal sentence in Section 4 about any stock. The Section 4 allowlist check only verifies that cited refs are on the initiating list's allowlist; it never checks which stock a sentence's cause belongs to. This is a gap in the checks; raised with Kevin, not changed here.
- **Decision:** one change, in the shared `sinceCloseLeadInstruction` (`lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`), the same text for PRE, REGULAR and POST; no per-state copies. The with-articles text keeps every first-pass sentence in order except two replaced clauses ("in Section 1 keep it to one or two sentences"; the "according to [publisher]" example) and adds:
  1. **Order is required:** "This order is required, not optional, and it overrides any other instruction or example about how Sections 1 and 2 begin."
  2. **Section 1:** the first sentence must be a since-close item with its publishedEt day and time; never open with the close or index figures. Section 1 ends with exactly one short last sentence, "where we left off": the S&P 500, NASDAQ Composite and Dow Jones Industrial Average, each with points and percentage in the index pattern, no other detail; no other close index figures in Section 1. That one sentence may run over 25 words (the base style check otherwise caps sentences at 25 words).
  3. **Section 2:** the first sentence gives the main since-close driver with its publishedEt day and time; close-session drivers come only after every since-close driver, marked as from the close.
  4. **Named sources, every section:** any cause names its source in the same sentence ("[Publisher] reported that ..."), using the outlet the cited article credits (such as a news agency named at the start of its summary), otherwise its `provenance.publisher`; never "according to reports", "reports said" or "analysts said"; if the source cannot be named from a cited admitted article, drop the cause and give the move alone.
  5. **Section 4 causes:** a cause for a stock's move only when an article linked to that same stock (its own `evidenceRefs` or `upcomingEvents` refs in `portfolioContext`) states it and is cited in Section 4, source named; an article linked to another stock, or cited only in another section, never supplies it; otherwise the move alone.
  6. **One worked example, last, marked "Format example only — it shows the order and nothing else; do not copy its wording, and the bracketed placeholders are not data or facts."** Its quoted text uses placeholders only ([time], [day], [Publisher], [Index A/B/C], [points], [percent], [level]) and contains no digit, so no number can leak into a report.
  - With no admitted article after the close, the one-sentence note is unchanged word for word.
  - No validator, section rule, localizer, Further Readings rule, classifier, projection, acquisition or reading-window code changed. The model input (user message) is byte-identical to `a9bdf3e` in every saved package.
- **Numbers:** none new. Same switch, same 12-hour figure. The writer system text grows by 2,508 bytes on active days with since-close articles (measured on every saved PRE/POST package); the existing writer-budget trim still applies.
- **Not shared / limits:** nothing left unshared (one function, all three active states). Yahoo articles carry `provenance.publisher` "Yahoo! Finance"; the original outlet (e.g. Reuters) is only in the article text, so the writer must read it from there. The instruction cannot force the writer: the order and attribution rules are not checked by code, and the Section 4 cause gap above remains until Kevin decides on a check. The "where we left off" sentence names the three main indices only (Russell 2000 is not in it).
- **Proof (all free, no network, no paid call; old tree = `git archive` export of `a9bdf3e`):**
  - 9C.1 kit, switch **off**: 5/5 stages PASS (6m27s): stage 1 54/54 runs fully byte-identical; stage 2 same-answer PASS; stage 3 8/8 planted mistakes caught; stages 4–5 PASS (`proof-8P2b2-OFF-output.txt`, `proof-progress-8P2b2-OFF.txt`). REGULAR and HOLIDAY: no saved package (reported MISSING).
  - Kit stage 1 (`compare.js`), switch **on**: 18/18 completed-day runs byte-identical; 36/36 PRE/POST runs differ in the writer request only (`compare-8P2b2-ON-output.txt`).
  - New `since-close-proof-8P2b2.js` over all 11 saved packages (case/typo tolerant, `.writer-raw.json` excluded) plus a derived "no article after the close" thin-PRE copy: switch off byte-identical to `a9bdf3e` off (12/12); switch on byte-identical on completed days and on the no-article copy; on active days with articles, model input byte-identical, system text identical up to the since-close lead, every first-pass sentence kept in order, all six rules present, example once/last/format-only/no digits; free replay of the 9C.1 synthetic reply and of the real saved 15 Oct writer reply gives identical results on old, new-off and new-on: 12/12 PASS. Self-test plants 5 mistakes (format-only marker removed, a real number in the example, a first-pass sentence dropped, model input changed, second-pass text leaking into switch off): 5/5 caught (`since-close-proof-8P2b2-output.txt`, `since-close-proof-8P2b2-selftest-output.txt`, progress `proof-progress-8P2b2-extra.txt`).
  - Not covered: real writer wording under the new instruction — needs a live run on the Preview proxy for this branch.
- **Evidence/tests:** full backend suite 1333/1333 (1326 before + 7 new Step 8P.2(b).2 tests in `tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`: one per rule 1–6, plus switch-off/completed-day absence); syntax check `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js` 60/60, no FAIL; `git diff --check` clean.
- **Must not change:** CNBC acquisition code; `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`; any `?rev=` token; Neon/persistence code; completed-session (CLOSED/WEEKEND/HOLIDAY) writer requests; the no-article one-sentence note; every existing check (rule 16 and the Section 4 allowlist are unchanged — any per-stock Section 4 cause check needs Kevin's approval first); every protected checkpoint in CLAUDE.md. None of these are touched.

## Step 9F.2e.5 — General article slots on: 7 Yahoo + CNBC's 3 unused slots passed on, 18 download attempts (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2e5-slots · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. One new switch, `articleSlotsEnabled` (Admin, default on). Off: byte-identical to `4f2b571`. Step 9F.2d.3 (portfolio slots, Section 4 citing them) is split into its own step and is NOT in this entry.
- **Why:** on the 15 Oct live runs only 6 Yahoo articles were admitted from at most 12 downloads. Market-wide stories were left out, and the Watchlist (AMD, META, AAPL, CRM, MA, V, VRSK, WM) got no Section 4 coverage. The approved slot design (Kevin, 10–11 Oct 2026; Step 9F.2e.2) was built but switched off.
- **Decision:**
  1. `lib/article-selection.js`: new `generalSlotCapacity({sourceId, settings})`. It works out one source's admitted cap by running the shared `fillGeneralSlots` itself on that source's slots plus the most spare it can receive. A source not yet in the selection step (CNBC today) brings no articles, so its slots are unused and pass on through the existing spare switch (`slotSpilloverEnabled`), at most `spareAdmits` per receiving source. Defaults: Yahoo 7 + 3 passed on from CNBC = **10 admitted**.
  2. `lib/source-reader.js` `sourceLimits`: while `articleSlotsEnabled` is on, the admitted cap is that capacity and the attempt cap is `yahooMaxArticleAttemptsPlanned` (**18**, 6–24). It also returns `articleSlots` ({generalSlots, spareSlots}). Off: exactly the previous object (12 / 6 / 5). `readSource` adds four fields to the Yahoo reading summary, right after `tryOrder`, only while on: `generalSlots`, `spareSlots`, `maxArticleAttempts`, `maxAdmittedArticles`. The reading loop, the try order (score order, Step 9F.2e.4) and every audit decision are unchanged. The admitted cap is simply larger. Articles past it keep `SKIPPED_MAX_ADMITTED`.
  3. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: the reader's limits are read once per run (`sourceLimits('us.yahoo-finance')`), so the switch takes effect without a reload. The load-time constants are kept as exports.
  4. **Every market state changes the same way** (PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND, HOLIDAY), because one shared reader runs in all of them. In CLOSED/WEEKEND/HOLIDAY, CNBC's own unchanged code still adds its CNBC news on top: 0–2 items in the saved packages. CNBC's 3 slots are passed to Yahoo in every state because CNBC is not in the selection step yet, as instructed. Counting CNBC's real articles against its slots belongs to the later step that connects CNBC.
  5. Unchanged: recaps (never in the reader; never dropped by slots, budget or duplicate flags), the drop rule (wire publishers and the generic market-word list still do not rescue a drop-list headline), pick-by-score order, the 30 s reading budget, 3 download tries, the classifier 50-item and byte checks (still applied per article), the 150 KB article budget (still OFF, Step 9F.2e.6), and the temporary writer trim (still in place).
- **"Drop-list fix":** the handover named one for this step. No definition was found in this log or in the settings table, and the only drop-list rule (Step 9F.2d.1) is already live. Left out, not invented.
- **Numbers (new or changed):** `articleSlotsEnabled` on (new, Admin, boolean); `yahooMaxArticleAttemptsPlanned` 18 (6–24, Admin, now wired); `yahooSlots` 7 (0–10, Admin, now wired); `cnbcSlots` 3 (0–6, Admin, now wired); `spareAdmits` 3 (0–5, Admin, now wired); `slotSpilloverEnabled` on (Admin, now wired). `yahooMaxArticleAttempts` 12 (6–20) and `yahooMaxAdmittedArticles` 6 (0–10) are unchanged and used only while the switch is off. `portfolioSlots` 2, `portfolioFirstPassPerCompany` 1 (Fixed) and `articleTextBudgetBytesPlanned` 150 KB stay not wired.
- **Size, cost, time (projected; no live run):** the 15 Oct Trading writer request is 126,271 bytes with 6 articles, about 3.3 KB per extra article, so about 140 KB with 10 (limit 512,000; all-16 KB worst case about 245 KB). Haiku 4.5 at $1 per million input tokens: typical about +$0.006 per run (writer about +3,300 tokens, classifier about +2,700), worst case about +$0.025. Run time: up to 6 more downloads, but the 30 s reading budget still bounds the article stage at about 34 s. Typical extra about 4–8 s when the cap is reached.
- **Tests:** full suite 1344/1344 (1333 before; 11 new: 6 orchestration, 1 selection, 2 reader, 1 settings, plus the settings table's automatic on/off row for the new switch). Nine existing tests that pin the old caps now run with the switch off (`slotsOffTest`, same precedent as Step 9F.2e.4a). Four tests that recorded "not wired" now record the wired rows and the new import line. Suite also 1344/1344 with `ARTICLE_SLOTS_ENABLED=off`. Syntax check over `api/*.js lib/*.js lib/sources/*.js`: 60 files, no FAIL. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Proof (all free, no network, no paid call; old tree = `git archive 4f2b571`; outputs in `Replay Packages\harness`):**
  - 9C.1 kit, switch **off**: 5/5 PASS (323 s). Stage 1 60/60 fully byte-identical; stage 2 reader same-answer PASS; stage 3 8/8 caught; stages 4–5 PASS. HOLIDAY has no saved package (MISSING).
  - New same-answer script (`slots-same-answer-9F.2e.5.js`, 2,000 cases): slot allocation and spare passing, the caps over generated settings (off also equal to the old tree), and the real reader end to end in both modes (off: events byte-identical to the old tree). 0 differences. Covered: more than 6 admitted in 137 cases, more than 12 attempts in 58, spare passed to CNBC in 151.
  - Planted mistakes (`slots-same-answer-9F.2e.5-selftest.js`): 9/9 caught, control clean. The first "donor can be itself" mutant could not change any result, so it was replaced by "donor gives beyond its unused slots".
  - Pipeline replay, switch **on**: 60/60 differ. With the four summary fields removed, 23/60 are fully identical, and the other 37 are exactly the runs with more than 6 usable articles (downloads 7→8; real headlines 6→11).
- **Could not share:** nothing per state. One gap: no 15 Oct candidate audit is saved, so which extra articles the 15 Oct runs would have admitted is not known before a live run.

## Step 9F.2d.3 — Portfolio slots on for My Stocks and the Watchlist; Section 4 offered Yahoo news in completed reports (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2e5-slots · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. One new switch, `portfolioSlotsEnabled` (Admin, default on). Off: byte-identical to `3a8b655`.
- **Why:** on the 15 Oct live runs Section 4 gave causes for only a few My Stocks names (only Nvidia had company news) and the Watchlist (AMD, META, AAPL, CRM, MA, V, VRSK, WM) got almost nothing. The portfolio-slot picker (Step 9F.2d.2) was built but not connected, the reader's "names a portfolio company" flag used only the list that started the run, and in completed reports no news article was ever linked to a stock, so Section 4 could cite quote pages only. Kevin's decision of 11 Oct 2026: the portfolio slots cover BOTH lists.
- **Finding before the build (read-only, saved 15 Oct packages):** both 15 Oct runs were started from My Stocks. Section 4 covers only the starting list by design: the project rule, the writer instruction (use only the starting list, never substitute the other list), the Section 4 reference allowlist (starting list only) and the Section 4 checker (drops other-list references and removes sentences naming an other-list company). The Watchlist data was supplied (all 8 quote pages; in the Trading run the "Bank of AMD" article was tagged AMD) and still could not be used. So the gap was not only slots. Kevin (17 Oct 2026): build the slots only; **keep the Section 4 starting-list-only rule unchanged.** Widening Section 4 to both lists is **Step 21D (planned, needs Step 20B first)**; when approved it will supersede the starting-list-only rule.
- **Decision:**
  1. `lib/portfolio-slots.js`: new `createPortfolioSlotTracker`, the one copy of the slot rules (names a headline's companies once each, counts general-slot coverage, first pass up to `portfolioFirstPassPerCompany` per company, fill, full). `pickPortfolioArticles` now runs on it (same results; its tests unchanged).
  2. `lib/source-reader.js` (shared reader, every state): `sourceLimits` adds `portfolioSlots` ({slots, firstPassPerCompany}) while the switch is on. `readSource` then raises the admit cap by the portfolio slots. The general slots fill first in try order (score order unchanged). After that only an article naming a My Stocks or Watchlist company not yet covered is tried (FIRST_PASS); one naming only covered companies waits; one naming no company is passed over (`SKIPPED_MAX_ADMITTED`). If a slot is still empty when the list runs out, the waiting articles are tried in order (SECOND_PASS). A general article naming a company covers it. Each portfolio pick's audit entry gets `portfolioSlot`; the reading summary gains `portfolioSlots`, `portfolioSlotsFilled`, `portfolioCoveredSymbols` (on only). The 18-attempt cap and the 30 s reading budget still bound everything; the per-article classifier check still applies.
  3. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: the reader's limits are read once per run before the reader. While on, the names given to the reader (slots and the pool's `portfolioSlotEligible` flag) are BOTH lists; off, the starting list as before. While on, completed-day (CLOSED, WEEKEND, HOLIDAY) Yahoo news naming a company is added to that company's `evidenceRefs` in `portfolioContext`, as active-day Yahoo news already is, so Section 4 can cite it. This includes general-slot articles naming the company (they are what "a general slot covers the company" means). Horizons are unchanged: an after-close article stays a subsequent development.
  4. **States:** PRE, REGULAR (Trading) and POST change in the reading only (up to 2 more articles naming My Stocks/Watchlist companies; Section 4's offer works as before). CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY change in the reading and in Section 4's offer. Section 4 still covers only the starting list in every state. CNBC news is not linked to Section 4 (CNBC code untouched).
  5. Unchanged: recaps (never in the reader; never dropped), pick-by-score order, the 10 general Yahoo slots (7 + 3 passed on from CNBC), 18 attempts, the 150 KB article budget (OFF), the temporary writer trim, Further Readings rules, every Section 3/4/6 grounding rule and checker.
- **Numbers (new or changed):** `portfolioSlotsEnabled` on (new, Admin, boolean); `portfolioSlots` 2 (0–4, Admin, now wired); `portfolioFirstPassPerCompany` 1 (Fixed, now wired). Most Yahoo articles admitted: 12 (10 general + 2 portfolio), within 18 attempts; completed days still add CNBC's own 0–2.
- **Size, cost, time (projected; no live run):** saved 15 Oct writer requests 117 KB (PRE) and 126 KB (Trading); about 3.3 KB per extra real article, so about 146 KB at 12 articles; worst case (every article at 16 KB) about 300–350 KB, under the 512,000-byte limit. Replay stand-ins (short article text): largest writer request 116,085 bytes. Haiku 4.5 at $1 per million input tokens: about +3,000 input tokens (writer + classifier) for 2 more articles, about +$0.003 per run; worst case about +$0.01. Run time: at most 2 more downloads inside the same 18-attempt cap and 30 s reading budget; typically +1–3 s when both slots fill.
- **Could not share:** nothing per state (one reader, one tracker). The pipeline's both-lists input and the completed-day Section 4 offer live in the orchestration (one place each). Limit: a company whose price data failed has no snapshot, so its name cannot be matched (per-symbol fail-soft, as for scoring today).
- **Tests:** full suite 1353/1353 (1344 before; 9 new: 5 reader, 2 pipeline, 1 tracker, plus the settings table's automatic row for the new switch). Six existing tests updated: three recorded the two portfolio rows as not wired, two pin the reader limits (now with the portfolio switch named), one lists the summary fields (now run with the portfolio slots on). Suite also 1353/1353 with `PORTFOLIO_SLOTS_ENABLED=off`. Syntax check 60 files, no FAIL. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Proof (all free, no network, no paid call; old tree = `git archive 3a8b655`; outputs in `Replay Packages\harness`):**
  - 9C.1 kit, switch **off**: 5/5 PASS; stage 1 60/60 fully byte-identical; stage 2 reader same-answer PASS; stage 3 8/8 caught; stages 4–5 PASS. HOLIDAY has no saved package (MISSING).
  - New `portfolio-slots-same-answer-9F.2d.3.js` (2,000 cases, independent reference, ground-truth company names): the picker, both-list selection (company on both lists, empty lists, general coverage, Watchlist picks), the limits (off also equal to the old tree), and the real reader end to end (on: reference walk with both passes; off: events byte-identical to the old tree). 0 differences.
  - Planted mistakes (`portfolio-slots-same-answer-9F.2d.3-selftest.js`): 11/11 caught, control clean. One candidate ("second pass ignores a full set of slots") was left out because it cannot change a result.
  - Pipeline replay, switch **on** (`compare.js`): 60/60 differ. With the three new summary fields removed, every run outside the real-headlines scenario is identical (package, classifier and writer requests, downloads); the only other change is the pool flag on a Watchlist headline (Mastercard, POST package). The 10 real-headlines runs admit 12 instead of 10.

## Step 9F.2d.4 — The Wet/Dry AMD exclusion also covers stock matching (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2c4-small-fixes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Word-list/exclusion fix, no switch (reuses the existing exclusion phrase list).
- **Why:** Step 9F.2c.1b stopped "AMD" the eye disease (Wet/Dry age-related macular degeneration) from matching the AMD macro ranking term, but `headlineMatchesIdentity` — the shared ticker-identity check used by the portfolio/most-active scoring bonus and the Section 4 slot picker — had no such exclusion. A user holding AMD stock would get a false portfolio/most-active match (and a false slot) from a "Wet AMD" drug-trial headline.
- **Decision:** `lib/headline-matching.js`'s `headlineMatchesIdentity` now checks a new `IDENTITY_EXCLUSION_PATTERNS` map (currently just `AMD` -> the existing `AMD_EXCLUSION_PATTERNS`) before the ticker-symbol branch; the company-name-variant branch is unaffected. No new phrase list; the map reuses `AMD_EXCLUSION_PHRASES` from `lib/headline-lists.js` verbatim.
- **Must not change:** the macro-term exclusion mechanism (`macroTermMatches`, `TERM_EXCLUSION_PATTERNS`) and the company-name-variant identity match are unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/article-score.test.js` — two new tests: a Wet AMD and a Dry AMD headline score 0 portfolio/most-active for an AMD identity (previously 20 combined); a real AMD chip headline is unaffected (28, unchanged). Full suite 1356/1356 (see the bundle-wide total below).
- **Proof:** `Replay Packages\harness\score-check-9F2d4-9F2c4b.js` (907aacf vs. this tree): "Kodiak Sciences (KOD) Climbs Nearly 3x on Promising Wet AMD Trial" and "New therapy targets Dry AMD" both 20 -> 0; a real AMD chip headline and "Nvidia, Broadcom, and AMD Investors..." both unchanged at 28. `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js` (switch off; the 9F.2f switch doesn't touch this fix): every saved package carries AMD as a tracked stock, so the REAL HEADLINES stand-in scenario (which contains the Wet AMD headline) differs from 907aacf in exactly this way on all 8 packages; every other scenario on every package is unchanged.
- **Supersedes:** none (9F.2c.1b's macro-term exclusion is unchanged; this closes the identity-match gap it did not cover).

## Step 9F.2c.4(b) — "trillion-dollar" dollar-exclusion also covers compounds like "multitrillion-dollar" (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2c4-small-fixes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Word-list/exclusion fix, no switch.
- **Why:** on the 16 Oct Trading run, a Fortune headline ("Does a robot cost $2 or $35 an hour? A multitrillion-dollar market depends on how...") scored a false macro-20 match on "dollar". The existing `trillion-dollar` exclusion (Step 9F.2c.3) requires a non-letter boundary immediately before the phrase, which a compound prefix like "multi" never has, so it never fired for this headline. Kevin approved part (b) only: broaden the "trillion-dollar" exclusion to any compound ending in it. Part (a) (removing the bare "earnings" macro term, which caused the other 5 false matches on that run) was explicitly NOT approved for this step — reported with a before/after impact list instead, pending Kevin's decision.
- **Decision:** `lib/headline-matching.js` adds `dollarExclusionPattern`, which special-cases the `trillion-dollar` phrase to a boundary-after-only pattern (no boundary required immediately before it); every other `DOLLAR_EXCLUSION_PHRASES` entry (including `billion-dollar` and `million-dollar`, intentionally left alone — not part of this approval) keeps the original whole-word pattern.
- **Must not change:** `billion-dollar`/`million-dollar` exclusions, the bare "earnings" macro term (unchanged pending Kevin's decision on part (a)), the macro/AI-theme scoring split.
- **Tests:** `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` — new "Step 9F.2c.4(b)" row set: the real Fortune-style multitrillion-dollar headline drops out of tier 2; a multitrillion-dollar headline that also names Nvidia stays tier 2 (via Nvidia, confirming the dollar suppression doesn't swallow an unrelated macro term); the existing bare "trillion-dollar" exclusion (word boundary present) is unaffected.
- **Proof:** `score-check-9F2d4-9F2c4b.js`: the exact 16 Oct Fortune-style headline 21 (usMacro 20 + benchmarkOrGeneric 1) -> 1 (benchmarkOrGeneric only); "Microsoft's Trillion-Dollar Quarter..." (already correctly excluded, has a boundary) unchanged at 8; "Start-up raises billion-dollar round" unchanged at 0 (confirms the fix did not broaden past "trillion-dollar").
- **Part (a) (not built — before/after list for Kevin's decision):** the bare `earnings` macro term (`lib/headline-lists.js` line 13) is the sole cause of the other 5 false matches on the 16 Oct run (Equity Bancshares, Demae Can, Avarda Bank, Valvoline, Sirius Real Estate — all matched on "Earnings"). Removing it would also drop the macro match from every other "...Stock... After/On Earnings..." headline in `tests/fixtures/yahoo-listings.js` (several similar rows there score macro 20 on "earnings" alone today); none of the 8 saved `Replay Packages\package-*.json` files' own stored Yahoo-news candidate listings were saved with per-article tier/score data, so a before/after list against them cannot be built without a live or replay run — only the fixture file's rows are available for a before/after today.

## Step 9F.2f — CNBC's post-classifier materiality/role gate removed, behind `cnbcNoSpecialConditionsEnabled` (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2c4-small-fixes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. One new switch, `cnbcNoSpecialConditionsEnabled` (Admin, default on). Off: byte-identical to `907aacf`.
- **Why:** Kevin's 10 Oct 2026 decision was that CNBC gets no special conditions. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` had a CNBC-only gate after the evidence-role classifier: a general CNBC item was kept only when the classifier gave it HIGH/MEDIUM materiality AND a MATERIAL_EVENT/PRINCIPAL_CATALYST role. No equivalent gate exists for Yahoo news or the base evidence items — they are concatenated unconditionally regardless of what the classifier said about them. This gate is in the orchestration path, not CNBC acquisition code (`lib/cnbc-*.js`), so it is in scope; the "do not touch CNBC acquisition" rule does not cover it. The decision log has no entry naming this specific gate under the 10 Oct decision (the nearby entries protect only CNBC's acquisition timing), so this reading is this step's own, flagged for Kevin to confirm or correct.
- **Decision:** `retainedCnbcConstructedEvidence` in `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js` is now `admittedCnbcConstructedEvidence` unfiltered while `cnbcNoSpecialConditionsEnabled` is on (the classifier still roles and scores every CNBC item; nothing drops it afterward for materiality/role alone). Off reproduces the exact 907aacf filter.
- **Numbers (new or changed):** `cnbcNoSpecialConditionsEnabled` on (new, Admin, boolean, `CNBC_NO_SPECIAL_CONDITIONS_ENABLED`).
- **Must not change:** CNBC acquisition code (`lib/cnbc-*.js`), the classifier itself, Further Readings eligibility (7cad52f) and every other evidence source's admission rule.
- **Tests:** `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` — the two pre-existing tests that exercised this gate ("filters five provisional CNBC items...", "roleless low-materiality CNBC provisional evidence adds no final evidence or gap") now run with the switch off via a new `cnbcFilterOffTest` helper, preserving their original 907aacf-identical assertions unchanged; two new sibling "Step 9F.2f on (default)" tests assert the same CNBC evidence is now admitted. Nine further pre-existing tests that incidentally relied on the default harness's one low-materiality roleless CNBC item being dropped (unrelated to what they test — Yahoo recap integration, package assembly, the Federal Reserve gap) also run with the switch off via the same helper, so their assertions are unchanged. Full suite 1356+/1361 passing (see bundle total).
- **Proof:** not exercised by the `Replay Packages\harness` pipeline-replay kit (none of its stand-in scenarios seed CNBC provisional evidence), so the sibling on/off tests above are this fix's before/after proof instead, matching the task's "extend... where it applies" instruction.
- **Supersedes:** none; records what the 10 Oct 2026 "no special conditions" decision changes in code for the first time.

## Step 8X — Fixed a doubled subject in the plain-English "risk appetite" rewrite (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2c4-small-fixes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Pure regex fix, no switch.
- **Why:** Section 5 of a generated report showed the doubled phrase "investor investors' willingness to take risk...". The plain-English rewrite's `risk appetite` / `risk-taking appetite` patterns matched only the bare phrase, but their replacement text hardcodes a subject ("investors' ..."). When the model's own prose already carried a subject immediately before the phrase (e.g. "investor risk appetite"), the old subject was left untouched and the new one was bolted on in front of it.
- **Decision:** `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`'s `PLAIN_ENGLISH_REPLACEMENTS` entries for both phrases now optionally consume a leading `investor`/`investors`/`investor's`/`investors'` subject (plus the whitespace after it) before the phrase, so nothing is left behind to double. The replacement text is unchanged.
- **Must not change:** every other `PLAIN_ENGLISH_REPLACEMENTS` entry, `hasAnalystDeskJargon`, `PLAIN_ENGLISH_STYLE_PATTERNS`.
- **Tests:** `tests/us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js` — new test covers "investor risk appetite", "investors' risk appetite", "Investor's risk-taking appetite" and a non-investor subject ("Market risk appetite"), each now normalizing without a doubled subject.
- **Proof:** before: `normalizePlainEnglishText("Strong investor risk appetite drove the rally.")` -> `"Strong investor investors' willingness to take risk drove the rally."` (doubled). After: `"Strong investors' willingness to take risk drove the rally."`.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 8N — Diagnostic logging for a rejected Yahoo telemetry overlay (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2c4-small-fixes · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Diagnostics-only; no result, report, package or settings change; nothing reaches Neon.
- **Why:** in the saved 17 Oct Trading package, ^GSPC's live overlay was null while ^DJI, ^IXIC and ^RUT all had one (the header fell back to the S&P 500's prior close). `lib/yahoo-telemetry-acquisition.js`'s `createOverlay` has several validation checks that can reject an otherwise-present overlay fact (session/date mismatch, invalid price, invalid/future/too-early timestamp, exchange-date mismatch), but logged nothing when one fired, so a future one-index-null case like this one can't be diagnosed from the logs alone.
- **Decision:** `createOverlay` now returns `{overlay, rejection}`; `rejection` is null on success or when the session type carries no overlay at all (CLOSED/WEEKEND/HOLIDAY — expected, not logged), and otherwise names which check failed and the values compared. The caller (`acquireSnapshot`) emits a `yahooTelemetryOverlayRejected` diagnostic (market, symbol, reason, compared) only when `rejection` is non-null, wrapped in the same try/catch-and-ignore pattern this file already uses elsewhere so observability can never affect the acquired snapshot. The null/non-null overlay result itself is unchanged.
- **^RUT's ~15-minute-older overlay:** confirmed not to matter — nothing in the pipeline compares overlay timestamps across indices; the existing staleness tolerances (`yahooStaleLabelToleranceHours`, `STALE_COMPLETED_SESSION_TELEMETRY_GAP`) apply only to article/headline freshness and completed-session gaps.
- **Must not change:** the overlay accept/reject result for every existing case.
- **Tests:** `tests/yahoo-telemetry-acquisition.test.js` — existing tests pass unchanged (confirming no result change); the one test whose diagnostics array is asserted exactly now also implicitly confirms CLOSED-session rejections stay unlogged (no `NO_OVERLAY_FACT_FOR_SESSION` noise).
- **Proof:** `Replay Packages\harness` stage 1 (switch off): every non-real-headline scenario on every package remains byte-identical to 907aacf, confirming this diagnostics-only change produces no result difference anywhere else in the pipeline.
- **Root cause of the actual ^GSPC null (recorded, not fixed further):** confirmed to be one of `createOverlay`'s validation checks rejecting Yahoo's returned price/time (not Yahoo supplying nothing) — which specific check fired for that one fetch cannot be determined from the saved package alone, since it does not retain Yahoo's raw `meta` response. The new diagnostic will name the exact check the next time this occurs.
- **Supersedes:** none.
