## Step 21D.1 — Section 4 gives a reason for a stock only when an article linked to that stock supports it (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-21d1-cause-check · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. One new switch, `sectionFourCauseCheckEnabled` (Admin, default on, env `SECTION_FOUR_CAUSE_CHECK_ENABLED`). Off: byte-identical to `3d5f595`.
- **Why:** Section 4 gave reasons for stock moves that no article supported. In the saved Weekend package of 17 Oct (`package-CLOSED-2026-10-17.json`) none of the 12 admitted articles mention Capri, Copart or Veeva, yet the report said Capri rose "amid luxury goods optimism", Copart was "benefiting from non-tech rotation", Veeva fell "as software found dip-buying", UnitedHealth climbed "on healthcare rotation", Novo Nordisk fell "in line with pharma sector pressure" and Microsoft fell "tracking technology sector weakness". The Trading run of 16 Oct did the same for UnitedHealth, Veeva and NVIDIA. The checks let it through for two reasons, found in Step 8P.2(b).2: the causal-claim rule works per section, not per stock (one stock's cited article clears every stock's reason), and its detector only knows market wording, so "as investors rotated…", "amid…" and "tracking…" are not read as causes at all. The 8P.2(b).2 writer instruction (active days only) was ignored in the 16 Oct Trading run, so an instruction alone is not enough.
- **Decision (Kevin, 17 Oct 2026, option 3 of the read-only finding):**
  1. A stock is supported when Section 4 cites a news item from that security's own `evidenceRefs` in `portfolioContext` (the same link that decides what Section 4 may cite: an admitted Yahoo article whose headline names the company), or a cited `upcomingEvents` ref of that security (none supplied today). Its market-data page never supports it; an article linked to another stock, or cited only in another section, never does.
  2. Each Section 4 sentence is matched to the starting-list stocks it names: ticker, instrument name, the name without its legal suffix, and without trailing generic words ("UnitedHealth Group Incorporated" → "UnitedHealth", "Novo Nordisk A/S" → "Novo Nordisk", "Veeva Systems Inc." → "Veeva"). A sentence naming none carries on the previous sentence's stocks; with none before it, it is about the whole list. A sentence keeps its wording only when every stock it is about is supported.
  3. Any other sentence keeps only its opening run of move facts. The cut is at the first word that is not a recognised move fact (numbers, prices, percents, points, move words, days and times, prior close, 52-week highs and lows, index and market names for a comparison with the index, the list's own names), taken back to the start of that phrase; a dangling joining word or a lone trailing word is removed. Nothing in the allowed table names a cause, so whatever the wording ("as", "amid", "on", "tracking", "in line with", "benefiting from", or anything new) the reason is removed. If the kept part names no stock (or the list) or no move, the sentence is dropped. A missed name can only lead to a cut, never to a reason let through.
  4. The cut adds no gap and does not set DEGRADED (event `UNSUPPORTED_STOCK_CAUSE`, `action: 'TRIMMED'`, counts only: `shortenedSentenceCount`, `removedSentenceCount`, `allowedReferenceCount` = supported stocks). If nothing is left, Section 4 is emptied with the existing "Not enough data to comment on the stocks in this list." Refs are left as cited, so Further Readings is unchanged.
  5. The writer is told the same rule in all six states: one instruction after the Section 4 allowlist (717 bytes), before the active-session and since-close instructions.
  6. Accepted for now (Kevin): a reason supported only by a market-wide article is cut (the 16 Oct Trading NVIDIA example: its only article does not name Nvidia in the headline, so it is not linked to NVIDIA and Section 4 cannot cite it). Step 21D.2 may let Section 4 cite those articles.
- **States:** the same code in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY. Only the set of linked articles differs: active days always link that day's Yahoo articles naming the company; completed days do so while `portfolioSlotsEnabled` is on (default on). CNBC is never linked to Section 4.
- **Where:** `lib/section-rules.js` (the shared module): `sectionFourNameVariants`, `sectionFourNamesStock`, `sectionFourStocks`, `sectionFourMoveFactsOnly`, `sectionFourCauseCut` and the allowed-word table. `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: one step after the cleanup rule loop in `normalizeDynamicReferenceViolations` (so it sees Section 4 as the starting-list rule left it), and `sectionFourCauseInstruction`. `lib/settings-registry.js`: the switch.
- **Could not share / not added:** the new step is not a registry rule (rule 31) and is not in `SECTION_RULE_CLEANUP_ORDER`, because the Step 9D.5a/9D.5b guard tests pin the registry to rules 1–30 and the order to eleven rules; it runs as one follow-on step after the loop, with its logic in the shared module. The final checker has no counterpart (the cut makes the condition true by construction; a checker error would only risk blanking a section). Nothing per state.
- **Known limits:** the cut cannot check that a supported stock's cited article actually states that particular reason (seen in the saved 1 Oct PRE report: Microsoft's cited article was about tax breaks). A sentence carried on from a supported stock keeps its wording. Harmless sentences with no move fact (for example "No major new catalysts … are supplied for these holdings.") are removed when a stock in the list has no linked article.
- **Numbers (new):** `sectionFourCauseCheckEnabled` default on (Admin, boolean, on/off). No other new number. Writer request +717 bytes (system) / +729 bytes (whole request) while on: about 180 input tokens, about US$0.0002 per run on Haiku 4.5. No new call.
- **Must not change (kept):** the Section 4 starting-list-only rule (writer instruction, allowlist, checker, Step 9B trim) is untouched; every existing check and its order; the 8P.2(b).2 Section 4 rule. CNBC acquisition code, `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`, every `?rev=` token and Neon code are untouched.
- **Tests:** full suite 1378/1378 with the switch on and 1378/1378 with `SECTION_FOUR_CAUSE_CHECK_ENABLED=off` (1364 before; 13 new in `tests/section-four-causes.test.js`, plus the settings table's automatic row). Two existing tests changed: the Step 9D.1 writer-request-size guard now checks its 19,700/31,893 figures with the switch off and +717/+729 with it on; "Section 4 accepts selected refs and (Step 9B) trims…" runs with the switch off (its general sentence names no stock, so with the check on it is about the whole list, and its Watchlist case has a stock with no linked article). Syntax check 60 files, no FAIL. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Proof (all free, no network, no paid call; old tree = `git archive 3d5f595`; outputs in `Replay Packages\harness`):**
  - 9C.1 kit, switch **off**: stages 2–5 PASS. Stage 1: every old-vs-new check identical in all 78 runs; its own "candidate pool vs audit" consistency check fails in 18 runs (real headlines on every package, all six POST scenarios), exactly the 18 that fail when `3d5f595` is compared with itself, so that failure was already there before this step. HOLIDAY has no saved package (MISSING).
  - `compare.js`, switch **on**: 78/78 differ in the writer request only (the one instruction), plus the same 18 pre-existing pool checks.
  - New same-answer script (2,000 cases, independent reference): the shared cut; the real pipeline on all 14 saved packages (196 runs; on equals the reference cut of off, same counts, other sections unchanged); switch off byte-identical to the old tree (196 runs, 14 writer requests); the 16 saved names. Covers a stock with a linked article, a stock with none, two stocks in one sentence, a stock named only in another stock's article, sentences naming no stock, and Kevin's six example sentences. 0 differences.
  - Planted mistakes (new self-test, 400 cases each): 14/14 caught, control clean. They cover support (market-data page counted, linked article not cited, upcoming event ignored), which stocks a sentence is about (one of two enough, no carry-over, unnamed sentence kept, no shortened names), the cut (at the unknown word, lone trailing word kept, no subject needed, "rotation" counted as a fact), and the pipeline (counts swapped, switch off ignored by the cut, instruction sent while off). The first run missed two (a lone trailing word before a reason, and a bare "on rotation" reason, never generated); the generator gained both and every check was rerun.
  - Before and after on every saved report (Weekend, Closed, Pre-market, Trading, After-Hours), and the real 15 Oct PRE raw reply end to end: `section4-before-after-21D1-output.txt`.
- **Supersedes:** none. Adds to Step 8P.2(b).2's Section 4 rule (now enforced, in all six states).

## Step 20B — A ticker lives in one list only: My Stocks or the Watchlist, never both (repo: MarketBrief)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-8-runtime-cost (from `fd2453f`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed.
- **Intent:** Step 21D (one Section 4 for both lists) needs a rule first: one ticker, one list. No earlier written definition of 20B existed (searched both repos and this log).
- **Finding before the build (read-only):** both lists are stored together in the browser's saved settings, each split US / SG / HK. Settings search was the only way to add, and it blocked a duplicate only within the same list (and the fixed index anchors), so a ticker could sit in both lists. Export/import replaced both lists wholesale with no checks. The Market Brief package already sends both lists and the backend tolerates a ticker in both.
- **Decision (Kevin, 17 Oct 2026, option (a)):**
  1. Adding a ticker that is already in the other list is blocked, with a short message under that list in Settings: "AAPL is already in My Stocks." / "D05.SI is already in Watchlist." (symbol compared ignoring case and spaces, across all markets). To switch lists the user removes it and adds it again.
  2. Saved data from before 20B (this browser, another device, a backup code): on load and on import the **My Stocks copy is kept** and the Watchlist copy dropped. On load the cleaned lists are saved and Settings shows one line above My Stocks for that session: "AAPL, D05.SI were in both lists; kept in My Stocks only." On import the same sentence is added to the import message and the reload waits 4 s instead of 1.2 s so it can be read.
- **Rejected:** (b) move the ticker to the new list with a notice (silent data change; easy to lose a My Stocks holding by accident); (c) allow with a warning (does not give 21D the rule it needs).
- **Where:** `app.js`: `tickerListLabel`, `tickerListHas`, `listOverlapNotice`, `removeCrossListDuplicates`, `listOverlapText` (new, next to `addTickerDirect`); `addTickerDirect`, `importSettings`, `loadSettings` and `renderSettingsPanelTo` (one message slot per list, the notice line) changed.
- **Must not change (kept):** fixed benchmark anchors and their exclusion from Settings rows; Dashboard, Market Brief package, Investment AI and PDF code; per-list saved briefs (switching lists still shows each list's own brief; 21D decides whether that stays); every version string and `?rev=` token.
- **Tests:** 170/170 across nine files run in isolated processes (163 before; 7 new Step 20B tests in `tests/settings-persistence.test.js`). One existing test changed: "list-aware add and remove mutate only the requested list" used AAPL in both lists; it now adds MSFT to My Stocks. `node --check app.js` OK. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Supersedes:** none.

## Step 21D.2 — Section 4 covers My Stocks and the Watchlist together (proxy) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 17 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9-integration (from `ad61dd6`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. One new switch, `sectionFourBothListsEnabled` (Admin, default on, env `SECTION_FOUR_BOTH_LISTS_ENABLED`). Off: the starting-list-only Section 4 of `ad61dd6`. Byte-identical proof is Prompt 2.
- **Why:** Step 21D (Kevin): a brief started from either tab should cover the user's whole set of stocks. Section 4 is already headed "MY STOCKS & WATCHLIST", but it covered only the list that started the brief. Two briefs, one per list, cost two runs for one market story. Step 20B (one ticker, one list) is done, so the two lists can be joined without duplicates. Step 21D.1 has made the per-stock reason rule strict enough to run across both lists.
- **Decision (Kevin, 17 Oct 2026, Step 21D.2 plan, approved with changes):**
  1. Section 4 covers every My Stocks security, then every Watchlist security, each in its saved order, as one section with no headings or subsections. This applies whichever list started the brief. A security in both lists counts once, in My Stocks. Step 20B prevents that case anyway.
  2. The writer is told the order (`stockOrder` in the Section 4 allowlist) and may begin the Watchlist part with the plain lead-in "On your Watchlist," and nothing more.
  3. Section 4 may cite the references of every stock in both lists. References from outside both lists (an index, broad-market news, another stock's telemetry) are still dropped, and sentences naming a stock in neither list are still removed (Step 9B, unchanged).
  4. The Step 21D.1 reason check runs over both lists. A stock gives a reason only when Section 4 cites an article linked to that stock. A sentence that names no stock is about the whole of Section 4 (both lists).
  5. A stock with no linked article shows its move only, grounded by its own market-data reference. Section 4 is never emptied for lack of articles.
  6. Both lists empty: exactly "No securities are configured in My Stocks or the Watchlist." One list empty: the other list is covered and the empty one is not mentioned.
  7. When nothing is left after the checks (for example, only unsupported reasons with no move facts), Section 4 is emptied with "Not enough data to comment on your stocks." This replaces "…on the stocks in this list."
  8. Market-wide reasons stay strict (Kevin): a market-wide article never supplies a stock's reason in Section 4. Nothing was built for them.
  9. **Step 9L (planned, not built; the empty-section retry that will replace 8R.B): Section 4 is never retried by 9L.**
  10. The app still sends the starting list, and the proxy still requires and checks it, so older app versions keep working. While the switch is on, the starting list no longer decides Section 4's scope. The app's one-shared-brief change is Prompt 3. Removing the field is later work.
- **Where:** `lib/section-rules.js` (the shared module): `sectionFourPortfolio`, `sectionFourEmptyContent`, `sectionFourNoDataMessage`, `sectionFourBothListsOn` and the two new texts. The empty-list detector (rule 23) now reads `sectionFourPortfolio`.
  - `lib/claude-model-input-projection.js`: the both-lists allowlist (`lists`, `stockOrder`, refs) and the Section 4 purpose the writer reads. The canonical input and its validated `outputRequirements` are unchanged.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: four Section 4 writer lines and one Section 3/4 phrase swapped while on, plus the allowlist instruction. The cleanup, the Step 9B trim, the Step 21D.1 reason check and every empty-list path read the shared helpers.
  - `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`: the final check reads the same helpers.
  - `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: the candidate pool's portfolio flag uses both lists even with the portfolio slots off. With the slots on (the default) it already did.
  - `lib/settings-registry.js`: the switch.
  - The same code runs in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY, with no per-state copies.
- **Numbers (new):** `sectionFourBothListsEnabled` default on (Admin, boolean, on/off). No other new number.
  - Writer request on the rich test fixture (one stock per list): system +132 bytes, whole request +204 bytes.
  - 16 stocks (8 + 8): under 2 KB more than off (test-checked). Both lists' prices and articles were already sent.
  - Section 4 output grows with the Watchlist, about 250–450 words for 16 stocks. That's within the 2,500-word cap and the 4,000 output tokens.
- **Known limits:** as in Step 21D.1, a stock is recognised in prose by its ticker (two letters or more), its name, or its name without the legal suffix and trailing generic words. A one-letter ticker written alone ("V rose …") is not recognised; "Visa" or "(V)" is. A name like "Verisk" for "Verisk Analytics, Inc." is not recognised either. A reason sentence using such a name is dropped rather than shortened.
- **Must not change (kept):** the Step 21D.1 rule and its switch; the Step 9B trim for stocks in neither list; the exact empty-list text when the switch is off; the Step 8J empty wording; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; the Step 8L one-retry rule; the frozen 8-section structure; Section 7 empty when unsupported. CNBC acquisition code, `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`, every `?rev=` token and Neon code are untouched.
- **Tests:**
  - Full suite 1391/1391 with the switch on and 1391/1391 with `SECTION_FOUR_BOTH_LISTS_ENABLED=off` (1379 before).
  - 12 new tests in `tests/section-four-both-lists.test.js`: the switch; order and allowlist for either starting list; writer text; a My Stocks brief keeping a Watchlist stock; the reason check across both lists; move-only stocks; one list empty (either brief); both lists empty; the both-lists empty wording; a stock in neither list trimmed; 16 stocks (8 + 8) in order with 12 reasons cut and 4 kept, plus the request-size bound; switch off restoring the starting-list behaviour.
  - 54 existing tests pin the starting-list rule through their fixtures (a Watchlist stock with no article beside prose about "the list", the old writer text and request size, other-list trims). They run with the switch off, by name, through `tests/fixtures/section-four-switch.js`. Five fixture builders now take the empty-list text from the switch (`tests/fixtures/section-four-empty.js`).
  - Syntax check 59 files (`api/*.js`, `lib/*.js`), no FAIL. `git diff --check` clean.
- **Proof still to do (Prompt 2):** byte-identical against `ad61dd6` with the switch off (9C.1 kit + `compare.js`); the full replay with the switch on and off; up to 8 planted mistakes with the same-answer script; before and after Section 4 on the saved packages.
- **Supersedes:** the starting-list-only Section 4 rule ("Section 4 covers only the list that initiated generation", and "Section 4 covers only the initiating list" in the must-not-change notes of Steps 8I, 8U.7, 9B and 21D.1). Section 4 now covers both lists while the switch is on. The rest of those entries is kept.

## Step 9F.2e.6 + 9F.2g — The 150 KB writer text budget switched on; the Step 9F.1f temporary trim retired behind the switch (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 18 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f2e6-article-limit (from `72ac47c`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. One new switch, `articleTextBudgetEnabled` (Admin, default on, env `ARTICLE_TEXT_BUDGET_ENABLED`). Off: behaves exactly as `72ac47c` (the Step 9F.1f temporary trim only; `applyWriterBudget` never called).
- **Why:** Step 9F.2e.2 built `applyWriterBudget` (the 150 KB article-text budget, drop whole articles by score, never shorten) but never connected it. The Step 9F.1f temporary trim (shorten Yahoo articles, longest first, 4 KB floor, Yahoo-only) was explicitly marked temporary pending this step. Both were due to be settled together (Kevin, this prompt).
- **Scope finding before the build (reported to Kevin, build approved to proceed with full scope):** `applyWriterBudget`'s drop-by-score rule needs a `slot`/`score` on each article, which the pipeline did not carry past the reader (only used there to size the admit *cap*, never tagged onto the evidence item itself, which `createEvidenceItem`/`validateEvidenceItem` enforce an exact-keys shape against). Placing the budget check *before* classification (as Step 9F.2e.2's own comment already intended -- "the duplicate-coverage step can run between download and the budget") means a dropped article never gets an `eN` reference and never enters `broadMarketFocus`, Section 4's reason check or Further Readings -- there is nothing to clean up afterward, by construction, not by added cleanup code.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Slot/score tagging (`lib/source-reader.js`):** the reader already scores every candidate (`scoreArticle`, Step 9F.2e.1's shadow score) and already knows which admission phase admitted it (general vs. portfolio). `tryCandidate` now takes a `slot` parameter (`'GENERAL'` default, `'PORTFOLIO'` at both portfolio call sites) and pushes `{score, ageHours, slot}` onto a new parallel array, `acquiredMeta` (mutated like `acquiredItems`, same push site, same order), read by nothing else in the reader. `readSource` also returns it as `meta`.
  2. **The shared wrapper (`lib/article-selection.js`, new `applyWriterTextBudgetToNewsItems`):** takes the reader's `items`/`meta` and a `neverDropBytes` count (CNBC news already admitted for the run, plus both recaps -- text size only, no items of their own), builds the `{key, slot, score, ageHours, listIndex, textBytes}` rows `applyWriterBudget` expects (plus one synthetic `PROTECTED`-slot row for `neverDropBytes`, since `applyWriterBudget`'s own drop order already excludes `PROTECTED`/`RECAP`), calls the existing, unmodified `applyWriterBudget`, and returns the kept items/meta (same objects, same order, nothing shortened) plus drop detail (headline, slot, score, bytes, reason) for the log line. No change to `applyWriterBudget` itself.
  3. **Orchestration wiring (`lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`):** one call per path, both before any card, horizon, or `eN` reference is built for the Yahoo items:
     - ACTIVE session: on the Yahoo items from `acquiredActiveYahooItems`/`acquiredActiveYahooMeta` (no CNBC or recap in active states, so `neverDropBytes` is 0), before the existing classifier-size fit (`activeYahooFit`/`checkListOrderFit`) runs on the (possibly smaller) result.
     - COMPLETED session: on `acquiredYahooNewsItems`/`acquiredYahooNewsMeta`, with `neverDropBytes` = the summary bytes of `admittedCnbcConstructedEvidence` (CNBC already admitted for this run, pre-classification-gate) plus `yahooRecapItems`/`cnbcRecapItems`, before the existing `completedYahooFit` runs.
     - Both call sites are gated on `articleTextBudgetEnabled`; off, `budgetedActiveYahooItems`/`budgetedYahooNewsItems` are exactly `acquiredActiveYahooItems`/`acquiredYahooNewsItems`, so every downstream reference, count and diagnostic is unchanged from `72ac47c`.
     - A new diagnostic event, `writerTextBudgetDrop` (emitted only when something is dropped): `budgetBytes`, `totalBytesBefore`, `totalBytesAfter`, `droppedCount`, and `dropped: [{headline (truncated, same audit field limits as every other headline in the logs), slot, score, textBytes, reason: 'OVER_WRITER_TEXT_BUDGET'}]`. No full article text in the event.
  4. **CNBC's own admitted-set used for `neverDropBytes` is `admittedCnbcConstructedEvidence` (pre-classification-gate), not `retainedCnbcConstructedEvidence` (post-gate, only differs while `cnbcNoSpecialConditionsEnabled` is off).** The two sets are identical while that switch is on (today's default), and using the pre-gate set avoids a circular dependency (CNBC's final retained set is only known after classification, which itself needs the final Yahoo list). Known, accepted limit: with `cnbcNoSpecialConditionsEnabled` off, the Yahoo budget is very slightly more conservative than strictly necessary (counts a little CNBC text that classification would later drop anyway) -- never less safe, never under the 500 KB request cap.
  5. **The Step 9F.1f temporary trim (`fitWriterArticleBudget`) is skipped entirely while the switch is on** (`lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js attemptClaudeAnalysis`): no article is ever both dropped and shortened. The code itself is untouched and still runs, unchanged, while the switch is off. The independent `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` guard (checks the real serialized request against `writerRequestLimitBytes`) is unconditional either way and still protects against a request that is somehow still oversize.
  6. **Settings:** `articleTextBudgetBytesPlanned` (150 KB, 32-200 KB, Admin) is now wired (was registered unwired in Step 9F.2a); `nonArticleOverheadBytesPlanned` stays unwired (cross-check only, unchanged). New `articleTextBudgetEnabled` (Admin, boolean, default on, env `ARTICLE_TEXT_BUDGET_ENABLED`).
- **What the reader would see (plain English, Kevin's point 4):**
  - A section whose only source article gets dropped by the budget looks exactly like a day with no admitted news for it: Section 6/7 simply has nothing to say there, Further Readings doesn't list it. This is the same, already-tested fail-soft path as "no current Yahoo news today" -- nothing new needed.
  - A stock (My Stocks or Watchlist) whose only linked article gets dropped shows no reason sentence for it this run, only its price move (the existing Step 21D.1 rule: a reason is shown only when an article linked to that stock is cited). The report still assembles; nothing fails.
- **Could not share:** nothing new -- `applyWriterBudget` itself needed no changes; the wrapper is the one new piece of shared code, called identically from both market-state branches.
- **Tests:** full suite 1402/1402 with the switch on, 1402/1402 with `ARTICLE_TEXT_BUDGET_ENABLED=false` (1393 before this step: 9 new).
  - `tests/article-selection.test.js` (4 new): `applyWriterTextBudgetToNewsItems` drops lowest-scored general then portfolio and keeps order; counts `neverDropBytes` but never returns or drops it; flags (never drops) when the never-drop weight alone is over; returns the input unchanged when nothing needs dropping.
  - `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js` (5 new): CLOSED drops the lowest-scored Yahoo article, keeps CNBC, and logs the drop (with no article text in the event); a generous budget drops nothing and logs no event; the switch off drops nothing even at a tiny budget; REGULAR (active) also drops the lowest-scored article (PRE and POST share the identical `analysisMode`/`completedSessionMode`-only code path, confirmed by reading the orchestration, so one active-state run plus the three real completed-state market-state tests already in the suite -- CLOSED, WEEKEND, HOLIDAY -- cover every distinct code path the budget touches); dropping a Watchlist stock's only article leaves no Section 4 reason for it and the report still assembles.
  - 6 pre-existing tests pinned to the switch off, by name, because they exercise the Step 9F.1f temporary trim directly: `tests/article-selection.test.js` "Step 9F.2e.2: every number comes from the settings table..." (now asserts the budget row is wired, not unwired) and "...only the orchestration and the reader import the selection step..." (import-line text updated); `tests/settings-registry.test.js` "Step 9F.2e.5 settings registry: articleSlotsEnabled..." (the "budget stays unwired" assertion removed, replaced by a new "Step 9F.2e.6 settings registry" test); `tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js` "accepts the provisional request-size boundary...", "Step 9F.1f writer budget: when even the 4 KB floor does not fit...", "...an over-cap request is sent trimmed, under the cap" (all three now pass `env: {ARTICLE_TEXT_BUDGET_ENABLED: 'false'}`).
  - Syntax check 60 files (`api/*.js`, `lib/*.js`, `lib/sources/*.js`), no FAIL. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls.
- **Must not change:** CNBC acquisition code and its shared timing functions (untouched); `lib/reading-window.js`'s `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep` (untouched; `fitArticlesToBudget`/`WRITER_ARTICLE_FLOOR_BYTES` unchanged); every `?rev=` token; Neon; `applyWriterBudget` itself (unchanged); the frozen 8-section structure; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`; one Anthropic request per structured generation (Step 8L unchanged -- the budget never adds a call); the Step 21D.1/21D.2 reason and both-lists rules.
- **Supersedes:** Step 9F.2a's register row for `articleTextBudgetBytesPlanned` ("not wired"; now wired) and the `tests/article-selection.test.js`/`tests/settings-registry.test.js` assertions that pinned it unwired. The Step 9F.1f temporary trim's decision log entries stand unchanged for the switch-off path; this entry adds the switch and retires the trim only while the switch is on.

## Step 9L — Writer retry for an empty section, a guided whole-report retry, one per-run writer call cap (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 18 Oct 2026 · **Branch:** step-9f4c-retries (from `c3c7ee5`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Build A of two (9F.4c and 8W are Build B). Two new switches, both Admin, default on: `writerSectionRetryEnabled` (env `WRITER_SECTION_RETRY_ENABLED`) and `writerRetryGuidanceEnabled` (env `WRITER_RETRY_GUIDANCE_ENABLED`). Both off: the c3c7ee5 writer call, byte for byte. The full proof against `c3c7ee5` is a separate step.
- **Why:** Section 7 came back blank on the 17 Oct Trading run, and nothing retried a single section; 8R.B (the try-hard Section 3 sentence) was never built and was waiting for this step. The 15 Oct identifier-leak failure was not saved by the Step 8L retry. No log of it was kept, so the cause can't be proven. The likely reason: the retry repeats the identical request, so the writer can repeat the same mistake. The logs also never said which section and field held the leak.
- **Decision (Kevin, 18 Oct 2026, Step 9L plan approved with decisions 1–6):**
  1. **One writer call per retry round**, covering every still-empty section; never one call per section.
  2. **One per-run cap of writer calls**, `writerCallsPerRunMax` (default 3, range 1–4, Admin). The first call, the Step 8L whole-report retry and every 9L round all count. If the whole-report retry has fired, 9L gets only what is left; at 1 there is no retry of any kind.
  3. **The whole-report retry names the broken rule** (section and rule category only; no report prose, no article text) instead of repeating the identical request. Behind `writerRetryGuidanceEnabled`; off = the identical retry of Step 8L. With the switch on, a whole-report failure also logs `writerContractFailure` (attempt, and for each broken rule: section index and name, field — content, uncertainties, evidenceGaps, evidenceRefs, status, report or reply — and category). The `contractRetry` line gains `guided: true`.
  4. **Section 7 is retried even when the writer left it empty on purpose**; the retry may still return it empty.
  5. **Eligible sections, Fixed:** 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7. Section 4 and Section 8 are never retried. A section the code blanks by design is never retried: rule 9 (no drivers → Section 2, no broad-market focus → Section 3; this also covers a failed labelling step, which leaves no roles), the fixed no-current-news report (the writer is not called), and any market whose section rules are off (SG, HK).
  6. **A blank section beats a made-up one.** A round's result is accepted only when every section that already had text comes out byte-identical and at least one retried section now has text. Otherwise the previous report is kept. A failure, an upstream or network error, an unusable reply or a timeout in a round also keeps it.
- **How a round works (`lib/writer-section-retry.js`, shared, no per-state copies):**
  - It sends the same package and system text, plus one appended instruction. The instruction names each empty section and, as fixed plain text, why it is empty: the writer left it empty, or the rule category from that attempt's cleanup events. Where the last-resort step gives no category, the reason comes from the same leak, broken-prose and no-evidence detectors run on the raw section.
  - Round 1: rewrite these sections under every rule; return any other slot empty; return a section empty if the evidence does not support it.
  - Round 2 and later (stricter): the same, plus the refs that section may use (Section 2: material events and principal catalysts; Section 3: broad-market focus refs; others: refs the rules allow), at most `writerSectionRetryMaxSentences` sentences, and no cause unless a listed principal catalyst is cited. A retried section over the sentence limit is not used.
  - Only the retried raw slots replace the empty ones in the previous raw reply. Every other slot, the status and the writer's evidence gaps stay as they were. The merged reply then runs through exactly the same cleanup and final check.
  - The post-reply cleanup was moved unchanged out of `attemptClaudeAnalysis` into `processWriterTransport`, so the first reply, a guided retry and a merged round take the same path.
- **Logging:** one `writerSectionRetry` line per round. It records the round, the sections (index, name and reason categories), the outcome (`ACCEPTED`, `REJECTED_NO_TEXT`, `REJECTED_CHANGED_SECTION`, `FAILED` with `failureType`, `SKIPPED_CALL_CAP` or `SKIPPED_TIME_LIMIT`), the filled sections, writer calls used, round time in ms and token usage. No prose and no article text. A round's own cleanup events are not forwarded; the round line is its record.
- **Numbers (all in `lib/settings-registry.js`):**
  - `writerSectionRetryEnabled`: on/off, default on, Admin.
  - `writerSectionRetryCount`: default 2, range 0–3, Admin (0 = no rounds).
  - `writerSectionRetryMaxElapsedMs`: default 150,000, range 60,000–200,000, Admin. No new round starts once this much time has passed since the writer stage began.
  - `writerSectionRetryMaxSentences`: default 4, range 2–8, Admin (round 2 and later).
  - `writerCallsPerRunMax`: default 3, range 1–4, Admin.
  - `writerRetryGuidanceEnabled`: on/off, default on, Admin.
  - Eligible sections: Fixed (`WRITER_SECTION_RETRY_SECTIONS` in `lib/section-rules.js`).
  - The Step 8L 150 s guard on the whole-report retry is unchanged.
- **Cost and time (estimate; no live run):**
  - A round sends the whole package but writes little: about 4–5 US cents on Haiku 4.5 at today's request sizes. The real 15 Oct Pre-market writer call was about 4.4 cents (32,166 input, 2,318 output tokens).
  - The worst case at the defaults is 3 writer calls per run: about 2 extra calls, roughly +8–11 cents. That stays near the US$0.20 ceiling.
  - Each round adds roughly 15–25 s, and no round starts after 150 s.
- **Implementation:**
  - `lib/writer-section-retry.js` (new).
  - `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js` (`invokeClaudeAnalysis` wrapper, `processWriterTransport`, `systemSuffix`/`transformTransport` options on `attemptClaudeAnalysis`, an internal map from each successful result to its raw reply).
  - `lib/contract-retry.js` (`allowRetry` and `guided` options; the second attempt receives the first failure; the classifier ignores it and still sends the identical request).
  - `lib/section-rules.js` (`retryEligibleEmptySections`, `WRITER_SECTION_RETRY_SECTIONS`; rule 9's test lifted into `controlledUnavailable` with no behaviour change).
  - `lib/settings-registry.js`.
- **Evidence/tests:**
  - Full suite 1424/1424 with the switches on (default), and 1424/1424 with `WRITER_SECTION_RETRY_ENABLED=off WRITER_RETRY_GUIDANCE_ENABLED=off`. That is 1402 before, 16 new tests in `tests/writer-section-retry.test.js` and 6 generated settings checks. Syntax check 61 files (`api/*.js`, `lib/*.js`, `lib/sources/*.js`; 60 + the new module), no FAIL. `git diff --check` clean. No paid calls; the writer is a stand-in.
  - New tests, each run in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY:
    - an empty Section 7 is filled on round 1, every other section byte-identical, same package, instruction appended, log line checked, no prose in the log;
    - each eligible section is retried;
    - several empty sections share one call;
    - Sections 4 and 8 are never offered;
    - a no-focus Section 3 is never retried;
    - a retry that stays empty keeps the original, and round 2 is stricter;
    - round 2 drops a section over the sentence limit;
    - an upstream error, a thrown fetch or an unusable reply keeps the original;
    - a retried section that leaks stays blank;
    - the acceptance check rejects any change to a surviving section;
    - the call cap at 3, 2 and 1, including after the whole-report retry;
    - the time limit;
    - retry count 0 and the switch off;
    - the guided whole-report retry and its log, plus the identical retry with the switch off;
    - both switches off give the same requests and result;
    - the failure-category mapping.
  - **18 existing tests pinned to `writerSectionRetryEnabled` off** (`tests/fixtures/writer-section-retry-switch.js`; unchanged otherwise). Each asserts one writer call or an exact list of cleanup events on a reply that leaves a retryable section empty on purpose. None of them checked the identical retry.
    - `tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`:
      - "PRE, REGULAR and POST can synthesize a grounded section when current news is unavailable"
      - "Step 8U.2: in every state group each rule break empties one section, not the report"
      - "Step 8U.5: in every state a made-up ref in Section 5 empties only Section 5"
      - "Step 8U.6: in every state an all-ungrounded Section 6 is still emptied and a grounded one is unchanged"
      - "Step 8U.8: in every state a section with only uncatalyzed causal text is emptied, except completed Section 1"
    - `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js`:
      - "Step 8U.10: a failed classifier on CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY returns a DEGRADED report, not a 502"
    - `tests/section-rules-matrix.test.js`:
      - "Step 9D.1: section rule table runs unchanged in PRE, REGULAR, POST, CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY"
      - "Step 9D.1: R18/R19/R20/R21 Section 3 rules behave the same across every state today"
    - `tests/section-rules-matrix-extra.test.js`:
      - "Step 9D.5a: R8/R27 content citing no evidence is emptied by the last-resort step, in every state"
      - "Step 9D.5a: R11 Section 2 must cite a driver ref, in every state"
      - "Step 9D.5a: R12 Section 2 needs a current-session driver on active days only"
      - "Step 9D.5a: R17 Section 3 must cite a focus ref and name its subject, in every state"
      - "Step 9D.5a: R28 the nothing-survived fallback, recorded in every state"
    - `tests/section-rules-order.test.js`:
      - "Step 9D.5b: every cleanup rule firing at once keeps today's event order, in every state"
      - "Step 9D.5b: rule 12 empties Section 2 before the causal rules can act, on active days only"
      - "Step 9D.5b: Section 3's rule 19 trim comes before rule 21's final-blank events, in every state"
      - "Step 9D.5b: Section 4 is emptied before Section 6, in every state"
      - "Step 9D.5b: last-resort events follow section order and keep the active-only detail"
- **Known limits:**
  - **A report can stay DEGRADED after a successful retry.** The writer's own evidence gaps are kept as they were, so a gap the writer wrote about a section that a round later filled stays in the report.
  - **Further Readings, the evidence reference order and the status may change** when a filled section cites new articles. Only Sections 1–7 must stay byte-identical.
  - **A round's cleanup events are not logged**, only its one round line.
  - **Some reasons are generic.** When the last-resort step blanked a section for a reason the raw-section detectors don't cover (for example the word cap), the reason is `OTHER_CHECK`.
  - **Round 2's reference list is an instruction only.** The existing checks still enforce Sections 2 and 3; the sentence limit is enforced in code.
  - **A completed-day empty Section 1 still fails the whole report** and goes to the (now guided) whole-report retry, not to 9L.
  - **A leak in the report-level evidence gaps list still fails the whole report.** It now gets a guided retry and is logged by field.
  - **No real reply has been seen.** Live writer behaviour under the retry instructions needs a Preview run, and no raw reply from the 17 Oct Trading run was saved.
- **Could not share:** nothing per state. Sections 4 and 8 are excluded by Kevin's rule, not by state.
- **Must not change:** CNBC acquisition code; `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`; every `?rev=` token; Neon; the frozen 8-section structure; Section 7 never filled with unsupported content (a retry may return it empty); Section 4 never retried; every existing section rule and final check (a retried section passes the same checks); the classifier retry (still identical; 9F.4c is Build B); a failed or invalid regeneration preserves the previous valid report; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`.
- **Supersedes:** Step 8R.B (the try-hard Section 3 sentence, never built), replaced by this section retry. It also supersedes part of Step 8L's must-not-change list, for the writer only: the whole-report retry may now name the broken rule instead of being byte-identical (switch `writerRetryGuidanceEnabled`; off restores 8L exactly). It extends the CLAUDE.md "one Anthropic request per structured generation" rule: with the switches on, a run may make up to `writerCallsPerRunMax` writer calls (default 3). Step 8L's retry-only-on-`CONTRACT_FAILURE`, skip-after-`max_tokens` and 150 s guard are unchanged.

## Step 9F.4c + 8W — Guided labelling retry, date accuracy (labels, one instruction, a sentence check), and the "Thu 15 Oct" log-measure fix (repo: mb-proxy)
- **Date:** 18 Oct 2026. Build B of two (Build A was Step 9L, f8d56c9). Kevin's decisions of 18 Oct: build the guided labelling retry now (count 1, range 0–2); the date check removes only the offending sentence and never blanks a section on its own; one shared build for all six market states; a blank section is better than a made-up one (a trim that empties a section is then a Step 9L retry candidate).
- **Why:** On 17 Oct the labelling-step retry was the identical request. Section 7 listed Warsh's remarks as upcoming though he spoke on Thursday. On 11 Oct the writer wrote "Monday" where the Fri 9 Oct article said "next Tuesday" (Sections 3 and 7). On Closed, Weekend and Holiday days the writer had only a raw UTC time, so it could not count day words. The log value "Section 1 leads with current news" read false on the real 15 Oct opening ("At 11:34 am ET on Thu 15 Oct …") because it did not know the day-before-month format.
- **What changed:**
  - **9F.4c, guided labelling retry** (`lib/claude-evidence-role-classification.js`, through the shared hook in `lib/contract-retry.js`). When the labelling step fails its own checks, the retry request adds one sentence naming the broken rule category and the references involved (for example "an item with horizon SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT was given PRINCIPAL_CATALYST … (references e3)"). Categories come only from our own validator's verdict on each entry; no article text or reply text is put in the request or the logs. The request is otherwise byte-identical. One log line per guided retry: `classifierRetryGuidance` with the attempt number and `[{category, references}]`. The shared hook now takes `maxRetries` (default 1 = Step 8L); the writer passes nothing and is unchanged. Step 8L's rules stand: only `CONTRACT_FAILURE`, never after a `max_tokens` cut-off, never after 150 s.
  - **8W (B), data:** with the switch on, every article the writer receives, in every market state, carries the same readable ET publish label (`publishedEt`, from the existing `exchangeTimeLabel` helper), and the request carries one readable report-time label (`reportTimeEt`). One shared place: `lib/claude-model-input-projection.js`. An after-close article keeps the label it already had.
  - **8W (C), wording:** one shared instruction for all sections (`dateAccuracyInstruction`, `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`): count a day word from the article's publish day; write the date with the day name; an event dated before the report time is not upcoming and leaves Section 7. It sits before the active-session and since-close instructions, so the since-close worked example still ends the system text.
  - **8W (D), check:** registry rule 31 `DATE_ACCURACY` (`lib/section-rules.js`: `dateAccuracyContext`, `dateAccuracyViolation`, `dateAccuracyCut`), run once by the cleanup stage after the other rules, for Sections 1–7, in all six US states. (i) A sentence naming a weekday (a full name, or a short name followed by a day number such as "Thu 15") must be supported: the weekday is named in a cited article of that section, or is the weekday of a date such an article gives, or is a cited article's ET publish day, or the report's ET day. (ii) In Section 7 only, a sentence with an explicit date ("Oct 2", "8 October", "2026-10-02", "10/2/2026") before the report's ET date is removed. A year-less date takes the year closest to the anchor date; month names count only when capitalised. Only offending sentences go (trim ladder step 2) and a trim adds no evidence gap. If every sentence goes, the section is emptied with the shared "Not enough data …" wording, and Step 9L may then retry it. One log line per section and rule: `DATE_ACCURACY_WEEKDAY` or `DATE_ACCURACY_PAST_DATE` with `removedSentenceCount`, counts only. The Step 9L retry wording gained a `DATE_ACCURACY` category for a section this check emptied.
  - **Two allowances beyond the plan (for Kevin to confirm):** the weekday of the completed session (`primaryCompletedSessionDate`) and the session days of telemetry the section cites are also allowed, so a correct "At the close on Friday, the S&P 500 …" sentence on a weekend is not removed when the cited articles were published on Saturday.
  - **E, log measure** (`lib/claude-analysis-contract.js`, `activeSessionOpeningDatePatterns`): also recognises the day-before-month form ("15 Oct", "Thu 15 Oct") of the current session date. It feeds only the `sectionOneCurrentFirst` log value; no report, check or retry reads it. Not behind a switch. The two existing tests that assert `sectionOneCurrentFirst: true` (`tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, lines 538 and 589) pass unchanged; no existing test pinned the old false value. The new test "Step 8W (E): the log measure is true on the real 15 Oct opening …" pins the fix.
- **New settings (all Admin):** `classifierRetryGuidanceEnabled` (on; env `CLASSIFIER_RETRY_GUIDANCE_ENABLED`); `classifierRetryCount` (default 1, range 0–2; env `CLASSIFIER_RETRY_COUNT`); `dateAccuracyEnabled` (on; env `DATE_ACCURACY_ENABLED`), covering B, C and D. No other new number.
- **Switches off = f8d56c9:** guidance off with count 1 gives the identical labelling retry; date accuracy off adds no label, no instruction and no check. The full suite passes with both switches off and with them on.
- **Old tests pinned with a switch off (by name):**
  - `tests/claude-evidence-role-classification.test.js` (guidance off): "Step 8L: classifier retries once silently after a contract failure and then succeeds".
  - `tests/claude-analysis-invocation.test.js` (date accuracy off): "Step 8P.2(b): PRE, REGULAR and POST name the right last close and mark each after-close article"; "Step 8P.2(b): switch off gives the previous writer request (on minus only the additions)".
  - `tests/section-rules-matrix.test.js` (date accuracy off; header wrapper only): "Step 9D.1: writer request size on the rich completed fixture is unchanged".
  - `tests/section-four-causes.test.js` (date accuracy off): "Step 21D.1: in every state an unsupported reason is cut, a supported one kept, NORMAL on one call"; "Step 21D.1: in every state the switch off leaves Section 4 exactly as written". Their fixture writes "CPRT rose 5.63% Thursday" with nothing cited that supports Thursday, which the check correctly removes.
  - Changed, not pinned (structure): `tests/section-rules-registry.test.js` "the registry has one entry per inventory rule, 1 to 31" (was 1 to 30), and its label table gains `R31`; new six-state row "Step 8W: R31 …" in `tests/section-rules-matrix-extra.test.js`.
- **Known limits:**
  - **A past event written with no weekday and no date cannot be caught by code.** The Warsh case ("traders will turn their attention to his remarks", written after he spoke, with no day or date) passes the check; only the instruction and the readable labels reduce it. A test shows it as not caught.
  - The weekday check can still remove a correct sentence when the day comes from somewhere it does not read (for example a weekday stated only in an article the section does not cite).
  - Relative words ("tomorrow", "yesterday", "next week") are not checked.
  - Labels and the check use US Eastern time; only the US writer path uses them.
  - Most of 9F.4c's value is temporary: Step 9F.4 later removes the "after-close item can't be the main cause" rule, which is the rule the labelling step mostly fails on.
  - No real reply has been seen; live behaviour needs a Preview run.
- **Could not share:** nothing per state. Rule (ii) is Section 7 only by design.
- **Must not change:** CNBC acquisition code; `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`; every `?rev=` token; Neon; the frozen 8-section structure; Section 7 never filled with unsupported content; the date check never fails a whole report and never blanks a section that still has a valid sentence; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`.
- **Extends:** Step 8L (the labelling retry may now name the broken rule and may run up to `classifierRetryCount` times; guidance off with count 1 restores 8L exactly) and Step 9L (a section the date check empties is a section-retry candidate).

## Step 9F.6b — CNBC finds candidates through free RSS lists (finder only, not connected) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Branch:** step-9f6b-cnbc-rss (from `9c5fddd`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Vercel Preview probe runs still to be recorded below.
- **Why:** CNBC candidates are found today by a paid AI search: about 6 US cents a run, and both searches failed with "too many uses" on the 17 Oct Weekend run. Two more reasons: one finding method for every source (Kevin, 10 Oct 2026), and no rate-limit failures. The saving only arrives at Step 9F.6f. This step adds the finder only.
- **History check (18 Oct 2026, free requests):**
  - The old RSS finder (`ac664d2`, patched in `7fd754c`, removed in `859ab65` on 13 Sep 2026) read only the Market Insider list. On 18 Oct, 29 of that list's 30 items were "biggest movers" pages.
  - Its address-only movers filter misses CNBC's other movers addresses (`stocks-moving-premarket-…`, `midday-stock-movers-…`).
  - The time-format problem was on the article page, not the list: pages write `+0000` without the colon, and `7fd754c` fixed that in the CNBC page reader. List items always carry seconds; only the list's own header time leaves them out.
  - Each item's list time is the page's last-updated time, not its first-published time. Every page checked matched its `dateModified`.
- **Kevin's decisions (18 Oct 2026):**
  1. **Lists:** read 7 lists: Top News 100003114, Economy 20910258, Finance 10000664, Investing 15839069, Earnings 15839135, Tech 19854910, Business 10001147. Market Insider 20409666 is not used (about 90% movers pages), and no optional lists are added.
  2. **Excluded address families:** `/select/`, `/video/` and video items (`cnbcvideo`), `/investingclub/`, `/pro/`, `/make-it/`.
  3. **List time (option B):** the list time is the **list updated time**. It is used only as a hint: candidates are ordered newest list time first, and an item whose list time is before the reading window start is dropped before any download. A page's publish time is never later than its last update, so nothing inside the window is lost. **The list time never admits anything.** Admission stays entirely on each page's own publish time, decided by the shared reader (Step 9F.6d).
  4. **Probe:** a read-only test address `GET ?cnbcRssProbe=1` behind a diagnostic-only switch `cnbcRssProbeEnabled`, default off. It **always answers "not found" in Production** (`VERCEL_ENV=production`). That is a Fixed rule in code, not a setting.
  5. **Neutral log stage names** move from 9F.6b to Step 9F.6d, when CNBC first runs through the shared reader (see Supersedes).
- **Decision:**
  1. **The finder (new `lib/sources/cnbc-rss.js`).** It has the same shape as `lib/sources/yahoo-news.js`: source id `us.cnbc` and `findCandidates({market, window, triggerAt}) -> {ok, candidates, discoveryStats}`. There is no `readPage` until 9F.6d.
     - The 7 lists are fetched in parallel from `search.cnbc.com`, with the `MarketBrief/1.0 evidence-acquisition` agent.
     - Each list is bounded (timeout, 512 KB) and fails soft on its own. A timeout, network error, HTTP error (403/429 included), oversize response or bad XML fails that list only. `ok` is true while at least one list was read.
     - Items are parsed with `fast-xml-parser`, with entities and CDATA decoded. Only https cnbc.com links are kept, with query and fragment removed.
     - Per item, in order: unusable (no address or headline), excluded family, movers, duplicate (by address or CNBC id, across lists), list time before the window start. Then newest list time first (items with no readable list time last, in list order), then the cap.
     - Candidate (frozen): `{headline, url, publisher: 'CNBC', listedAt, listId, cnbcId, contentType}`. There is no age label and no edition.
     - `discoveryStats` holds each list's outcome, HTTP status and item count, plus totals: unusable, excluded, movers (address only, title only, both), duplicates, unreadable list times, dropped before the window, sponsored, kept before the cap, and candidates.
  2. **Biggest-movers rule: address OR title** (Fixed row `cnbcRssMoversPatterns`, case-insensitive).
     - Address patterns: `stocks-making-the-biggest-moves-(midday|premarket|after-hours)-`, `stocks-moving-(premarket|midday|after-hours)`, `(premarket|midday|after-hours)-stock-movers`.
     - Title patterns: starts with "Stocks making the biggest moves", or "Stocks moving (big) premarket/midday/after hours".
     - For review only, a loose net ("movers/moves/moving" in the title or address) lists possible misses in the probe. It never drops anything.
  3. **The probe (new `lib/cnbc-rss-probe.js`; one GET branch in `api/quote.js`, placed before the other routes and loaded only when hit; `Cache-Control: no-store`).**
     - It reads only the fixed lists from the settings table and never an address taken from the request.
     - Per list it reports: HTTP status, time taken, bytes, item count, newest and oldest list time, unreadable list times, movers by address only, title only and both, and up to 10 possible misses. It also reports the overall counts, the reading window and the kept candidates.
     - With `&pages=N` (0–3, clamped; default 0) it downloads the first N kept candidates' pages one at a time. Limits come from the existing `cnbcPageTimeoutMs` 4000 and `cnbcMaxResponseBytes` 1280 KB reference values. For each page it reports the page's own publish and modified times next to the list updated time.
     - No paid call and no Neon. An unexpected failure gives a 502, not a crash.
  4. **Nothing calls the finder.** The orchestration, the shared reader, the CNBC acquisition code and the paid search are unchanged. Reports and cost are identical to `9c5fddd` with either switch in either position.
- **Numbers added (`lib/settings-registry.js`):**
  - `cnbcRssFinderEnabled`: on, Admin, env `CNBC_RSS_FINDER_ENABLED`. Off: the finder makes no request and returns `{ok: false, candidates: [], discoveryStats: {outcome: 'DISABLED', lists: []}}`. Nothing calls it yet, so off only changes what the probe shows.
  - `cnbcRssTimeoutMs`: 4000, range 1000–10000, Admin, env `CNBC_RSS_TIMEOUT_MS`.
  - `cnbcRssMaxCandidates`: 40, range 10–100, Admin, env `CNBC_RSS_MAX_CANDIDATES`.
  - `cnbcRssProbeEnabled`: off, Admin, env `CNBC_RSS_PROBE_ENABLED` (diagnostic only).
  - `cnbcRssProbePages`: 0, range 0–3, Fixed cap 3.
  - Fixed: `cnbcRssLists` (the 7 ids), `cnbcRssMaxResponseKb` 512, `cnbcRssMoversPatterns`.
  - No other number. The excluded address families are a fixed list in the finder, per decision 2.
- **Tests:** full suite 1473/1473 (1442 before; 31 new). The suite passes at the defaults and with both switches flipped (finder off, probe on).
  - `tests/cnbc-rss.test.js` (18):
    - the 7 fixed lists and the agent
    - both list time formats, offsets and bad times
    - an unreadable list time is kept last and counted
    - entities and CDATA
    - bad XML, an oversize list (read and declared) and a timeout each fail one list only
    - 403 and 429 on one list each while others work; every list failing
    - duplicates across lists by address and by CNBC id
    - the cap keeps the newest (10 and the default 40)
    - the window pre-drop (the start itself kept; no window, no drop)
    - the excluded families, videos and non-CNBC links
    - movers fixtures from 18 Oct: the three time-of-day forms, the two variants the old rule missed, the `-on-more-.html` ending, a title-only and an address-only case
    - the lookalike `…biggest-moves-weekly-roundup` passes, and real market articles pass
    - the drop counts per rule
    - switch off means zero fetch calls
    - the source-text and loaded-module check: the new files load no Anthropic, Postgres or CNBC acquisition module.
  - `tests/cnbc-rss-probe.test.js` (9):
    - "not found" in Production even when switched on
    - "not found" when switched off
    - the per-list report, movers counts and possible misses
    - the page option with own publish times, capped at 3
    - the page-count clamp
    - no address taken from the request
    - finder off gives DISABLED with no fetch
    - an unexpected failure gives a 502
    - the `api/quote.js` branch: `no-store`, and 404 in Production and when off.
  - `tests/settings-registry.test.js`: 4 automatic default/clamp/on-off checks for the new Admin rows. No existing test needed changes. No paid calls.
- **Free comparison (scratch only, not committed):** `Replay Packages\harness\compare.js` against `9c5fddd`, exported read-only with `git archive`: 14 packages × 6 scenarios = 84 runs.
  - `9c5fddd` against itself gives 65 identical and 19 different. The 19 are the harness's own run-to-run differences: every "real headlines" scenario, and all six POST 2026-09-29 runs.
  - The working tree against `9c5fddd` gives the same 65/19 with the finder on and with it off. Both output files are byte-identical to the `9c5fddd`-against-itself output, so this step changes nothing the harness measures.
  - Outputs: `compare-9F.6b-old-vs-old-output.txt`, `compare-9F.6b-on-output.txt`, `compare-9F.6b-off-output.txt`. The live probe script is `cnbc-rss-local-probe.js`.
- **Local free run (this PC, 18 Oct 2026 Sunday ~07:00 UTC):**
  - All 7 lists answered 200 in 0.4–1.2 s at about 20 KB and 30 items each: 210 items in total.
  - 13 excluded, 9 movers (all in Finance, all caught by both rules), 25 duplicates and 115 before the window start. 48 were kept and capped to 40.
  - One possible miss, a real article ("…slow moving crisis"), which the strict rule correctly keeps.
  - The 3 pages downloaded showed their own publish times (`+0000` form) earlier than or equal to their list updated times.
- **Vercel test (Preview) must record, per call:** the market state and time; per list the HTTP status, time taken and item count (any 403/429 means CNBC limits Vercel addresses); the movers counts and the possible misses reviewed; the candidate count; and for the page call each page's status and own publish time against the list time. Planned: one call before the US open, one while trading, one with `&pages=3`. Results: *(to be added)*.
- **Known limits and risks:**
  - These are unofficial CNBC lists with no service promise. They can change or disappear; per-list fail-soft and the outcome counts make that visible.
  - CNBC's network provider filters some request agents: the `www.cnbc.com/id/…/rss.html` address refused curl's default agent from this PC. It may also treat Vercel/AWS addresses differently. Only the Preview probe can show this. CNBC article pages are already downloaded from Vercel in Production.
  - The list time is a last-updated time. It is safe only because it never admits anything.
  - Movers addresses drift. The title rule backs up the address rule, and the probe's possible-miss list shows drift.
  - The probe sits on a public Preview address with open CORS. Mitigations: off by default, never in Production, fixed CNBC lists only, at most 3 page downloads, no Neon, no paid call.
  - `api/quote.js` is a shared file (one small branch).
  - Today's live blog appears in the lists; the shared reader's duplicate check handles it at 9F.6d.
  - Investing Club and Pro are excluded because they are subscriber-only.
- **Could not share in this step:**
  - The bounded body reader: the Federal Reserve module's copy is not exported, and exporting it would mean editing that file. The finder has its own copy of the same pattern, which the probe reuses.
  - The CNBC page reader hand-off (`readPage(url, {headline})` to the existing CNBC page reader, which expects a full candidate with a horizon) waits for 9F.6d.
  - `tests/cnbc-news-research-runtime.test.js` forbids "RSS" in the old CNBC runtime. It is untouched here, and Step 9F.6c must supersede it.
  - Neutral log stage names move to 9F.6d.
- **Must not change:**
  - The list time never admits anything.
  - Market Insider is never read.
  - The probe never reads an address from the request, never runs in Production, makes no paid call and touches no Neon.
  - The finder stays unconnected until 9F.6c/9F.6d.
  - CNBC acquisition code (`lib/cnbc-*.js`), `lib/reading-window.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`, `lib/source-reader.js`, `lib/source-audit.js`, every `?rev=` token and Neon are untouched.
  - The protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f` stand.
- **Supersedes:**
  - **The CNBC sitemap plan (Step 21A, a Master-document rule):** CNBC candidates come from the RSS lists instead of CNBC's sitemaps.
  - **The "no RSS fallback" rule (Steps 8D.7B and 8D.7G, Master-document rules):** RSS is now the planned way CNBC finds candidates, under the conditions released on 11 Oct 2026 (filter movers pages; use each page's own publish time; test from Vercel; a new file, with the existing CNBC acquisition code untouched in this step).
  - **The Step 9F.6a.1–9F.6a.3 and 9F.6a.4–9F.6a.6 entries' clauses on log stage names:** "the Yahoo stage names until 9F.6b" and "neutral names come with CNBC in 9F.6b". They now read "until 9F.6d" and "in 9F.6d" (Kevin, 18 Oct 2026). The Yahoo stage names are unchanged in this step.

## Step 9F.6c — CNBC page reader for the shared source reader: shared retries, first 16 KB, publish time only, wire stories (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Branch:** step-9f6c-cnbc-reader (from `0d477b6`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Not connected: nothing calls the new reader until Step 9F.6d, so every report and its cost are unchanged.
- **Trigger:** Kevin's 10 Oct 2026 decision (restated 18 Oct): one method for every source. Yahoo's evidence rules are the baseline, and the reading window applies to every source, including CNBC, replacing CNBC's own age rules. The protection on existing CNBC acquisition code was lifted for the page reader only, and for this step only.
- **What changed:**
  1. `lib/cnbc-article-content-acquisition.js` gains a fourth method, `readPage({url, headline})`, next to the three older ones (full candidate with a horizon; recap; paid-search page). Their behaviour is unchanged. Two internal helpers take a new optional "story types" input whose default is the old single type, so the older methods pass exactly what they passed before. The new method:
     - reuses this file's extraction, time parsing and bounded body reading;
     - reuses the shared `firstBytes` and `articleKb` (`lib/reading-window.js`, imported, not edited);
     - downloads once with the shared failure types: TIMEOUT, RETRIEVAL_FAILURE, RESPONSE_READ_FAILURE, HTTP_FAILURE with the status, RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE, INVALID_CONTENT_TYPE, NO_USABLE_ARTICLE with the shared extraction-failure names, INVALID_INPUT, RESULT_TOO_LARGE, and DISABLED. The shared reader's retry helper decides what is retried. A redirect stays final;
     - keeps the first 16 KB of text on a whole character instead of rejecting a long article;
     - returns the page's own publish time, or a blank when it is missing or unreadable, so the shared reader rejects the page and counts it;
     - returns the modified time blank when it is unreadable or earlier than the publish time, and never rejects on it;
     - writes the address as the 9F.6b finder writes it (https cnbc.com, no query, no fragment);
     - reads CNBC wire stories;
     - never throws.
  2. `lib/settings-registry.js`: a new switch row, `cnbcPageReaderAlignedEnabled`; the `pageTimeoutMs` description now also covers this read.
  3. Tests:
     - New `tests/cnbc-page-reader.test.js` covers CNBC's own parsing, wire stories, live blogs, the switch, the address rule, every failure type, the 1,536 KB limit, the shared size setting and the shared timeout.
     - New `tests/source-reader-contract.test.js` holds the universal rules, run identically against Yahoo's reader and the new CNBC reader.
     - No existing test changed.
- **CNBC age rules the reading window supersedes, for the new reader only** (the older three methods keep them until Step 9F.6f retires them):
  - **R1:** the publish and modified times must both fall inside a caller-supplied horizon.
  - **R2:** a modified time earlier than the publish time rejects the page.
  - **R3:** the recap's publish date, or its headline date, must equal the target session day.
  - **R4:** an unreadable modified time rejects the page.
  - In their place, the page's own publish time alone, judged by the shared reader against the reading window.
- **Kevin's decisions (18 Oct 2026), with reasons:**
  1. **Wire stories:** accept them when the text is on the page; with no readable text, the normal no-usable-article failure. No text is invented, and no other website is fetched. Reason: one rule for every story on a source.
  2. **Page size limit:** 1,536 KB (Yahoo's) for the new reader; the older three keep 1,280 KB. Reason: live pages already reach about 1,140 KB.
  3. **Live blogs:** keep today's extraction: the first usable update's text and that update's own publish time.
  4. **Switch:** on by default, Admin, environment variable `CNBC_PAGE_READER_ALIGNED_ENABLED`, read on each call. Off: the new reader downloads nothing and answers "disabled"; old CNBC rules are not replayed inside it. With either setting, reports are identical to `0d477b6`, because nothing calls the reader yet.
  5. **Universal source rules:** a shared conformance test and the section below. The shared reader is not changed.
- **Wire-story result (free check, 18 Oct 2026):**
  - 224 current list pages were checked, and 9 were wire stories. In all 9, the text sits on the same CNBC address, in the same page-state modules as CNBC's own stories; only the story type differs ("wirestory").
  - Before: 0 of 9 readable. After: 9 of 9 readable by the new reader.
  - A wider free run of the new reader on 40 current list candidates: 40 of 40 read; 0 publish times later than the list time; 0 blank publish times; none over 16 KB (largest 11.2 KB); 18.4 s in total, one after another.
- **Rules every source must follow** (checked for each reader in `tests/source-reader-contract.test.js`). A new source adds only its finder and its own page parsing; everything else is shared and is not copied.
  1. Never throw: every outcome is a result.
  2. Return the shared success shape (source, clean address, page headline, publisher, publish time, modified time, text) or the shared failure shape (type, plus the HTTP status or the extraction reason).
  3. Report the failure types the shared retry recognises: timeout, failed request, failed body read, HTTP failure with its status.
  4. Let the shared retry helper retry only temporary failures (timeout, failed request, failed body read, 429, 5xx), up to the shared attempt count, with the shared pauses, and never past the reading budget. A 404, a redirect, an extraction failure or an oversize page is tried once.
  5. Keep the first 16 KB (the shared `articleKb`) on a whole character; never reject a long article for its length. The classifier reads the first 8 KB, downstream, for every source.
  6. A missing or unreadable publish time is a blank; the shared reader rejects the page and counts it.
  7. The page's own publish time alone admits or rejects it, against the shared reading window. A list time or an age label never admits anything.
  8. The modified time is carried, never judged.
  9. Write the address clean, with no query and no fragment, so the shared duplicate check works across sources.
  10. Make no paid call: a page read is a plain web request to the source's own pages.
- **Universal rules the older CNBC paths still do not follow** (recap and paid-search page; both completed states only):
  - **Article text over 8 KB is rejected, not cut:** rule 5. Retired by 9F.6f for the paid-search path. The recap stays on its own path until a later step moves it.
  - **No retries:** rule 4. Retired by 9F.6d (completed states) and 9F.6f.
  - **Age rules R1–R4 instead of the reading window:** rule 7. R1 and R2 retire with the paid search in 9F.6f. R3 is the recap's own session check, retired only if a later step moves the recap onto the shared reader.
  - **Failures raised as errors, not results:** rules 1–3. Retired by 9F.6f.
  - **Wire stories not read:** retired by 9F.6f.
  - **Address keeps its query string:** rule 9. Retired by 9F.6f.
- **Found while testing (not changed; Yahoo's reader was off-limits):** Yahoo's page reader throws when a response header cannot be read. The shared reader catches it as a fetch failure ("EXCEPTION") and does not retry it. The conformance test lists this as a known Yahoo gap (a to-do, not a skip). A later step should fix it in Yahoo's reader.
- **Limits:**
  - No new tunable number.
  - Shared values reused: article text 16 KB (Admin, 2–32); download attempts 3 (Admin, 1–5); retry pauses 0.25 s and 0.75 s (Fixed); classifier read 8 KB (Fixed); page timeout 4,000 ms (Admin, 2,000–8,000); result cap of text plus 4 KB (Fixed).
  - The 1,536 KB page limit and the 512-byte headline limit are Fixed constants in the reader.
  - Worst case per page with retries: about 13 s (3 × 4 s plus 1 s of pauses). Per source: the reading budget plus at most one timeout.
  - CNBC's reading budget and attempt cap rows come with 9F.6d.
- **Risks:**
  - The story-type input on the shared extraction could leak into the older methods. Mitigation: the default is the old single type, and all old CNBC tests pass unchanged.
  - For a live blog, the time is the first update's, not the whole page's.
  - A malformed streamed body chunk is reported as a failed body read, and so is retried.
  - CNBC page layouts can change. The reader then fails with the normal no-usable-article failure, never with invented text.
- **Could not share in this step:**
  - CNBC's own page parsing (standard block, live blog, page state) and its address rule, which are source-specific by design.
  - CNBC's limits row and log stage names in the shared reader (9F.6d).
- **Must not change:**
  - The three older CNBC methods and their 1,280 KB bound until 9F.6f.
  - The 9F.6b finder (`lib/sources/cnbc-rss.js`, `lib/cnbc-rss-probe.js`), the paid CNBC search code, `lib/reading-window.js`, `lib/source-reader.js`, `lib/source-audit.js`, `timed`/`monotonicNow`/`sleep`, Yahoo's page reader, every `?rev=` token and Neon (all untouched).
  - The rules-every-source list above, unless a superseding entry changes it.
  - The protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f` stand.
- **Supersedes:**
  - The Step 9F.6b entry's note that "Step 9F.6c must supersede" the test forbidding "RSS" in the old CNBC runtime (`tests/cnbc-news-research-runtime.test.js`). This step does not touch that runtime, so the test stays as it is; it is superseded when 9F.6f retires that runtime.
  - The Step 9F.6b entry's "Could not share" note on the CNBC page-reader hand-off: the hand-off shape now exists, and only the connection waits for 9F.6d.

## Step 9F.6d Build A — CNBC shared-reader groundwork (not connected, zero report change) (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Branch:** step-9f6d-cnbc-completed (from `17e4ef1`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not yet committed. Not connected: nothing reads CNBC through the shared reader until Build B, so every report, log line and cost is unchanged.
- **Why:** in completed states CNBC news still comes from the paid AI search (about 6 US cents a run; both searches failed with "too many uses" on the 17 Oct Weekend run). One method for every source (Kevin, 10 Oct 2026). Step 9F.6d has two builds: A adds the shared plumbing and proves it changes nothing; B connects CNBC in Closed, Weekend and Holiday states.
- **Kevin's decisions (18 Oct 2026), with reasons:**
  1. **Slots:** CNBC gets 3 general slots; Yahoo gets 7 plus CNBC's unused slots (total 10). Reason: one shared method, the same total as today.
  2. **CNBC's quota is protected:** CNBC's admitted articles (at most 3, ranked by the same shared score and rules as Yahoo) are never pushed out by Yahoo. Only the slots CNBC leaves unused pass to Yahoo. Reason: CNBC must keep its own quota. Governs Build B; Build A's slot sum is built for it.
  3. **Classifier failure:** CNBC news is kept on a total classifier failure, like Yahoo (Build B).
  4. **Fallback:** the paid CNBC search runs only when all 7 CNBC lists failed (Build B).
  5. **Two builds:** this is Build A.
  6. **Budget and names:** CNBC reading budget 20 s (Admin). Neutral CNBC log names: summary `sourceNewsReading` (with the source id `us.cnbc` in the line), audit `sourceNewsCandidateAudit`; both CNBC timings go into the existing `cnbcNewsResearchMs` total. Yahoo's log names do not change.
- **What changed:**
  1. `lib/sources/cnbc-rss.js`: the CNBC source gains `readPage(url, {headline})`, which hands the shared reader's call to the Step 9F.6c page reader's one bundled input (`readPage({url, headline})`). The CNBC article service is passed in as `pageReader`, not required, so the finder file still loads no CNBC acquisition module. With no page reader it returns the shared `DISABLED` failure; a page reader that throws gives the shared `NO_USABLE_ARTICLE` failure. Never throws. The finder is unchanged.
  2. `lib/source-reader.js`:
     - a CNBC limits row: budget `cnbcReadingBudgetSeconds`, attempts `cnbcMaxArticleAttempts` (slots on or off), admitted cap with slots off `cnbcSlots`, first pass `cnbcInitialCandidateAttempts`, and `portfolioSlots: false` (the 2 portfolio slots stay Yahoo's);
     - `sourceLimits` takes an optional slot context (sources already read, sources still to read), passed to the slot sum; left out, the result is today's;
     - the reading summary carries the source id right after the stage name, only for a source whose log names ask for it (CNBC);
     - **two fixes found by the new end-to-end CNBC test** (both would have broken Build B; neither touches Yahoo's behaviour):
       - **(a) the publisher field.** The reader passed every page's publisher name into the evidence item, but the evidence rules allow an editorial publisher for Yahoo news only, so every CNBC article would have been rejected. The reader now asks the evidence rule (`permitsEditorialPublisher`, moved unchanged into an export of `lib/evidence-items.js`). Yahoo news still passes its publisher. CNBC's publisher comes from its source registry entry ("CNBC").
       - **(b) the outcome field.** A source's listing count named like one of the reader's own summary fields (CNBC's finder reports `outcome`) used to overwrite the reading outcome. It is now written in the same place as `listing` + its name (`listingOutcome`). Yahoo's listing counts use none of those names, so its line is unchanged.
  3. `lib/source-audit.js`: a CNBC log-names row (above) with `summarySourceId: true`. The Yahoo row is untouched.
  4. `lib/article-selection.js`: `generalSlotCapacity` accepts `readSources` ({source: admitted}) and `pendingSources`. A source already read counts by what it actually admitted; a source still to read counts as full; any other source brings nothing (today's rule). Both default to empty, which gives exactly the Step 9F.2e.5 result. CNBC read first, with Yahoo still to read, gets 3 general and no spare; Yahoo after CNBC admits N gets 7 + (3 − N), within `spareAdmits`.
  5. `lib/evidence-items.js`: the editorial-publisher test is now the exported function `permitsEditorialPublisher` (same test, same result).
  6. `lib/settings-registry.js`: the new and changed rows below.
- **Numbers:**

  | Name | Default | Safe range | Admin/Fixed | Env |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `cnbcReadingBudgetSeconds` (new) | 20 | 10–60 | Admin | `CNBC_READING_BUDGET_SECONDS` |
  | `cnbcMaxArticleAttempts` (existing; now wired) | 6 | 3–12 | Admin | `CNBC_MAX_ARTICLE_ATTEMPTS` |
  | `cnbcSlots` (existing; also the admitted cap with slots off) | 3 | 0–6 | Admin | `CNBC_SLOTS` |
  | `cnbcInitialCandidateAttempts` (new) | 3 | — | Fixed | — |

  - **Reused unchanged:** download attempts 3, retry pauses, page time limit 4 s, 16 KB article text, list time limit 4 s, 40 candidates, spare 3.
  - **No switch in Build A;** Build B adds it.
- **Tests:** full suite 1524 tests: 1523 pass, 0 fail, 1 to-do (1504 before; 20 new). The to-do is the known Yahoo header gap from Step 9F.6c. No existing test was edited; the contract test's reader list gained a third entry.

    - the CNBC limits row and neutral log names, with the Yahoo rows unchanged;

It checks the 7 lists, the finder drops, `sourceId` and `listingOutcome` in the summary, a 429 then success retried, a 404 tried once, the recap live blog rejected as a duplicate, the cap of 3, the publisher from the registry, and every timing under `cnbcNewsResearchMs`;
    - the attempt cap of 6;
