## Step 9F.6d Build B — CNBC connected through the shared reader in Closed, Weekend and Holiday reports (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-18 · **Branch:** step-9f6d-cnbc-connect (from `2532952`) · **Status:** Sitting 1 of 2 IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not committed. Sitting 2 (2,000 generated cases, planted mistakes, six-state replay with the switch on) is still to be added here before any commit, then one live Weekend or Closed Preview run.
- **Why:** completed-state CNBC news still came from the paid AI search (about 6 US cents a run, and "too many uses" failures on the 17 Oct Weekend run). Build A added the shared plumbing and proved it changed nothing; Build B connects it.
- **Kevin's decisions (18 Oct 2026), with reasons:**
  1. **Slots:** CNBC fills its 3 general slots; Yahoo gets 7 plus CNBC's unused slots (total 10). Reason: one shared method, the same total.
  2. **CNBC's quota is protected:** CNBC's admitted articles (at most 3, ranked by the shared score and rules) are never pushed out by Yahoo. The existing "CNBC and both recaps are never dropped; lowest-scored Yahoo goes first" budget rule stays. Reason: CNBC's quota is its own.
  3. **Classifier failure keeps CNBC,** like Yahoo, including paid-fallback CNBC. Reason: one rule for every source.
  4. **Paid search only when every list failed** (the finder's outcome `ALL_LISTS_FAILED`, or `FAILED` when the list reader threw); never when any list loaded. One log line `{stage: 'cnbcPaidSearchFallback', reason}`. Reason: lists loaded but nothing qualified is a real "no CNBC news", not a failure.
  5. **The fallback follows the same slot rule** (its first 3 results in its own order). Whenever CNBC fails or yields fewer than 3, the unused slots go to Yahoo (CNBC 2 gives Yahoo 8; CNBC 0 gives Yahoo 10). Reason: one rule everywhere.
  6. **Still over 150 KB after every droppable Yahoo article went:** send it anyway; `writerTextBudgetDrop` gains `overBudget: true` and is written even when nothing was dropped. The writer's own request-size check is the backstop. Reason: only extreme Admin values can cause it (at the defaults the never-dropped worst case is 3 × 16 KB CNBC + 16 KB Yahoo recap + 8 KB CNBC recap = 72 KB).
  7. **No CNBC source connected = the old path** (as with the switch off). The runtime always connects it. Reason: the ~36 existing CNBC tests stay unchanged and are the switch-off proof.
  8. **CNBC's live blog** (`/YYYY/MM/DD/stock-market-today-live-updates.html`) is skipped in the lists before download, as an excluded family. The recap path stays its only owner. Reason: saves an attempt and keeps the recap's session checks.
  9. **One new Admin switch** `cnbcSharedReaderCompletedEnabled` (default on, `CNBC_SHARED_READER_COMPLETED_ENABLED`), read once per run in completed states only. Off = `2532952` exactly. Reason: a safe way back.
- **What changed:**
  1. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`:
     - optional `cnbcNewsSource` input (checked when given);
     - completed states with the switch on and a source connected:
       - Most Active is fetched once, before CNBC;
       - CNBC is read with the shared reader (Build A's CNBC limits, Yahoo still to read; the same horizon rule as Yahoo, after the close = subsequent development);
       - the fallback check (decision 4, then decision 5's cap);
       - the admitted items become today's CNBC record shape (`c1..cN`, the matching canonical horizon object, a validated evidence item) in the existing `cnbcConstructedEvidence`, so references (base, then CNBC, then Yahoo), the classifier fit, retention, Further Readings and gap lines run exactly as before;
       - Yahoo is read with `slotContext.readSources {us.cnbc: N}`;
       - the CNBC candidate pool joins the pool hook;
       - classifier failure (or no classification) keeps CNBC;
       - `overBudget`;
       - `method: 'SHARED_READER'` (or `'PAID_SEARCH_FALLBACK'`) on the `cnbcNewsResearchIntegration` line.
     - **Unchanged:**
       - the switch-off path (today's paid-search code, moved unchanged into one inner function, logs as before);
       - the active states (no CNBC reading);
       - the definitions of `yahooItems`, the recap and Fed item lists and the completed-state horizon rule were moved above the CNBC reading, unchanged and side-effect free.
  2. `lib/analysis-package-runtime.js`: builds `createCnbcRssSource({fetchImpl, env, pageReader: createCnbcArticleContentAcquisitionService({fetchImpl, onDiagnostics, env}), excludeLiveBlog: true})` and passes it as `cnbcNewsSource`.
  3. `lib/sources/cnbc-rss.js`:
     - the live-blog skip (decision 8) as an opt-in `excludeLiveBlog` (counted in `excludedFamilyCount`, plus `liveBlogSkippedCount` only with the option);
     - left out (the probe, the Build A tests), the finder is exactly as before;
     - comments.
     - It is an option, not a change to `isExcludedFamily`'s default, because Build A's end-to-end test (`tests/source-reader.test.js`, "the shared reader reads CNBC end to end on made-up lists and pages") expects the live blog to be downloaded and rejected as a duplicate, and no existing test may change.
  4. `lib/settings-registry.js`: the switch row; descriptions of `cnbcSlots`, `cnbcRssFinderEnabled`, `cnbcPageReaderAlignedEnabled` and the text-budget rows say CNBC is now connected.
  5. `lib/source-audit.js`: comment only.
- **Numbers:** one new number, the switch (default on, Admin). Everything else is reused:

  - list timeout 4 s, page timeout 4 s, 16 KB;
  - 40 candidates, spare 3, 150 KB.

## Step 9F.4d — A failed labelling retry no longer empties the report: only the after-close "main cause" labels are removed (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-20 · **Branch:** step-9-label-failure-partial (from `e4baea5`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not committed. One live Weekend or Closed Preview run is still needed.
- **Why:** on the Weekend run of 18 Oct 16:10Z the labelling step (classifier) marked Yahoo's market-close recap (published 48 minutes after the close, so `SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT`) as a principal catalyst. The guided retry did the same. Step 8U.10 then threw away all 44 labels, so the package had no material events, no principal catalysts, no focus list and the "Could not sort the news …" gap line. Sections 2 and 3 came out empty and Section 4 lost its stock reasons. The earlier run of the same evening (15:40Z) had the same fault on three items, the retry fixed it, and the report was good. Results flipped between good and empty by chance.
- **Kevin's decisions (20 Oct 2026), with reasons:**
  1. When the last attempt fails **only** on "subsequent development cannot be a principal catalyst", just the named items lose `PRINCIPAL_CATALYST` and get `['MATERIAL_EVENT']` (materiality and subjects unchanged); every other label is kept. Reason: one bad label must not empty the report. A demoted item is what the successful retry produced on 18 Oct 15:40Z: it stays in the evidence and the focus list and can never support a causal claim.
  2. The subject-repair call is skipped on this path. Reason: zero added paid calls.
  3. One Admin switch `classifierPartialKeepEnabled` (env `CLASSIFIER_PARTIAL_KEEP_ENABLED`), default on, read once per run in the orchestration. Off = `e4baea5` for every report and log line.
  4. No gap line is added after a partial keep. The writer input equals a successful retry.
  5. The after-close rule itself is **not** changed (a recap of a completed session still can never be a principal catalyst). To be decided in Step 9F.4.
  6. No wider fallback now; a "material" label is never invented by code.
- **What changed:**
  1. `lib/claude-evidence-role-classification.js`: in the failed attempt's catch, when `classifierFailureCategories` returns exactly one category, `SUBSEQUENT_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST`, with at least one reference, every named reference is `SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT` and holds `PRINCIPAL_CATALYST`, and the reply was not cut off at the token limit, the same reply with those items demoted is checked once with `completeClaudeEvidenceRoleClassificationOutput` (the success path's check, same `allowConservativeCompletion`). If that check passes, `{output, classificationCoverage, demotedReferences}` is kept in a WeakMap beside the failure object. New export `classifierPartialKeepFor(result)`. The failure object, its message, the retry and every log line are unchanged; the module never reads the switch.
  2. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: reads the switch once per run. On a failed labelling result with the switch on and a kept record, it logs the unchanged `evidenceRoleClassificationFailure` line, then the new line, and continues down the normal success path with the demoted labels (CNBC retention, final references, roles, focus), skipping the subject repair. No `evidenceRoleClassificationFallback` line and no gap line. Everything else degrades exactly as before.
  3. `lib/settings-registry.js`: the switch row, next to the 9F.4c pair.
- **New log line:** `evidenceRoleClassificationPartialKeep` `{stage, outcome: 'DEMOTED_PRINCIPAL_CATALYST', demotedReferences, demotedReferenceCount, keptReferenceCount, subjectRepairSkipped: true}` (reference codes and counts only). No existing stage name, timing name or field order changed.
- **Today's whole fallback is kept for:** unknown, extra, duplicate or out-of-order references; missing references in a completed state; a malformed or cut-off reply; a bad materiality, roles or subjects value; any mix of the after-close fault with another fault; upstream, input and request-too-large errors; a thrown error; and any stand-in or success result that fails the orchestration's own check.
- **Relation to other switches:** independent of `classifierRetryGuidanceEnabled` (retry wording) and `classifierRetryCount` (how many attempts run); the partial keep looks only at the last failed attempt (with count 0, the first).
- **Numbers:** only the switch. No cap on how many items may be demoted.
- **Not shared:** nothing per state. The same code runs in all six US states; in practice it fires only in CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY, the only states with `SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT` items. The SG/HK legacy path has no labelling step.
- **Tests:** new `tests/classifier-partial-keep.test.js` (made-up articles and replies only) and one new row in `tests/settings-registry.test.js`. No existing test was edited. The tests that pin today's whole fallback pass unchanged: the nine Step 8U.10 tests in `tests/us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js`, "Step 8L: classifier stops after one retry when the second answer also fails", "Step 9F.4c: classifierRetryCount 0, 1 and 2 give 1, 2 and 3 calls …" and the `classifier = 'FAIL'` profile in `tests/cnbc-completed-shared-reader.test.js`. They use stand-in results (no kept record) or assert the unchanged failure result.
- **Proof (20 Oct 2026, free, scratch harness outside the repo; repository, branch and HEAD confirmed `mb-proxy` / `step-9-label-failure-partial` / `e4baea5` before starting):**
  1. **Full suite:** at defaults 1,553 tests, 1,552 pass, 0 fail, 1 to-do (the known Yahoo header gap from Step 9F.6c); with `CLASSIFIER_PARTIAL_KEEP_ENABLED=off` the same 1,553 / 1,552 / 0 / 1. Before this step: 1,546. Syntax check 63/63 files (`api`, `lib`, `lib/sources`). `git diff --check` clean.
  2. **Switch off = `e4baea5`:** old tree = `git archive e4baea5 lib api`. On the 14 packages of the 9F.6dB reference, switch off (and switch on, with the usual succeeding labeller stand-in) gives a file byte-identical to `compare-9F6dB-fix-off-output.txt` (65 identical, the same 19 known "candidate pool vs audit" false failures). With a labelling stand-in that breaks the after-close rule on every attempt (sent through each tree's real labelling module), switch off matches `e4baea5` on all 16 saved packages in both break modes (every after-close material item; the Yahoo recap only): 75 identical, 21 differences, all the known "candidate pool vs audit" kind; the package, writer request, labels and every log line are identical (whole fallback).
  3. **2,000 generated cases** (`fuzz-9F4d-partial-keep.js`) through the real labelling module with a fake fetch, against an independent reference written from the decision text only (replies built from a clean base plus a known list of injected faults; 1–50 references, mixed horizons, retry counts 0/1/2, guidance on/off, the after-close fault on random subsets, mixed with other faults, missing/unknown/duplicate/out-of-order references, malformed, wrong shape, cut off, clean): **0 mismatches** (504 partial keeps, 462 whole fallbacks, 1,034 successes); no after-close item ever kept as a principal catalyst; 0 extra fetches. A second seed: 0 mismatches. (The first run found 2 mismatches, both a bookkeeping bug in the generator, fixed in the generator only.)
  4. **Planted mistakes (8):** caught: (1) demote every principal catalyst; (2) roles `[]` instead of `MATERIAL_EVENT`; (5) skip the re-check; (6) switch ignored when off; (7) the fallback line logged as well; (8) the subject repair run on this path. **Not caught as single changes: (3) accept a mixed-fault reply and (4) accept missing references in a completed state.** Neither can change any result, because the one re-check after demotion rejects both cases by itself. Shown by planting each together with the re-check loosened: both then caught (diagnostic only, not counted).
  5. **Free replay, switch on and off, six states:** PRE (7 packages, including 15 Oct and 20 Oct), REGULAR/Trading (15 and 17 Oct), POST (29 Sep), CLOSED (29 Sep), WEEKEND (17 Oct ×2, 18 Oct, 27 Sep, 3 Oct). **HOLIDAY package: MISSING (covered by fixtures only). Older-style Trading package: MISSING.** With the rule-breaking labeller and the switch on, every PRE, REGULAR and POST run is identical to `e4baea5`; every CLOSED and WEEKEND run changes from the whole fallback (0 material events, 0 focus, the gap line, Sections 2 and 3 blanked by rule 9) to a partial keep (labels kept, no gap line, neither section blanked), with 2 labeller fetches and 0 subject-repair calls. Without a break the switch changes nothing.
  6. **Run 2 (18 Oct 16:10Z) reproduction:** its package was **not saved**; the 18 Oct 15:40Z package (same evidence, same Yahoo recap) was replayed with a stand-in labeller that marks the recap a principal catalyst on both attempts. The stand-in reply is **made up** to match what the logs reported (the raw replies are never logged). `e4baea5`: 0 material events, 0 focus, the gap line, Sections 2 and 3 blanked. This step: the recap becomes a material event, 2 material events and 1 focus entry kept, no gap line, neither section blanked. (The harness rebuilds Yahoo news with its own stand-ins, so the counts are smaller than the real package's and the recap's reference differs.)
  7. **Repo hygiene:** `git status --short` shows only the 6 expected files; the new tests use made-up text only.
  - Scripts and outputs copied to `Replay Packages\harness` (new names): `compare-9F4d.js` (+ 6 outputs), `fuzz-9F4d-partial-keep.js` (+ output), `planted-9F4d.js` (+ 2 outputs).
- **Second-session proof (21 Oct 2026, independent of the 20 Oct proof above):**
  1. **Retry-count-0 gap closed:** `tests/classifier-partial-keep.test.js` gained two orchestration-level cases (switch on and off) that pin `CLASSIFIER_RETRY_COUNT=0` instead of the pinned `1`. Switch on: 1 labeller fetch, the partial keep still applies (the two after-close items demoted, the completed-session principal catalyst kept, no gap line, no subject-repair call). Switch off: 1 labeller fetch, the whole `e4baea5` fallback. Earned its place: planting the fault "retryOnContractFailure returns a fresh object instead of the original on `maxRetries === 0`" (which drops the partial-keep record, since it is held in a WeakMap keyed by object identity) made this new case fail, and also failed the existing "retry count 0 keeps after the first attempt" unit test; both pass again on the unmodified code. Done in a scratch copy outside the repo; no product file touched.
  2. **Fresh reference, independent of the 20 Oct fuzzer:** 2,100 generated cases, 0 mismatches.
  3. **8 new planted mistakes:** 7 caught; the 8th (an accepted mixed-fault reply, same shape as the 20 Oct run's uncaught case 3) is not caught as a single change because the one re-check after demotion rejects it by itself — the retry-count-0 gap from item 1 above is now closed by the new orchestration test.
  4. **Post-market question settled:** the partial keep can fire only on CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY. POST, PRE and REGULAR/Trading completed-session windows never carry `SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT` (after-close) evidence, so the after-close rule the partial keep reacts to can never be broken there.
  5. **First Weekend report found by its generated time:** file named `package-CLOSED-2026-10-18 2346.json`.
  6. **Six-state replay result:** item 3's new harness, `Replay Packages\harness\closed-replay-9F4d-item3.js`, run against `package-CLOSED-2026-09-29.json` (internal `marketState` confirmed `CLOSED`) with a made-up labeller stand-in that calls the Yahoo recap a principal catalyst on every attempt. Switch on: the recap is demoted (`e21`), 3 material events, 3 focus entries kept, no gap line, 2 labeller fetches, 0 subject-repair calls. Switch off: the whole `e4baea5` fallback (0 material events, 0 principal catalysts, 0 focus, the gap line, `evidenceRoleClassificationFallback`/`CONSERVATIVE_UNCLASSIFIED`), matching `e4baea5`.
  7. **HOLIDAY and an older-style Trading package: still MISSING.**
- **Investigate-only findings, NOT fixed here:**
  - The 18 Oct 15:40Z report said Capri "fell" $0.10 (0.63%) when the saved data shows it rose (15.85 to 15.95). The numbers come from our data and the verb from the writer; no code checks the direction of a move. A deterministic check is feasible later.
  - The 20 Oct Pre-market report said Nvidia and AMD "both slipped about 1%" premarket. No existing check catches it: numbers are never checked against sources (D-002), and Section 3's name check needs only one subject of a cited focus entry.
  - No live pre-market price: every saved Pre-market package (including 15 Oct) and the saved Post package has no live price for any symbol (`INVALID_PRICE`: the Yahoo chart reply has no usable pre-market price). Long-standing, not new.
- **Must not change:** a `SUBSEQUENT_DEVELOPMENT` item is never a principal catalyst; any failure other than the after-close fault alone degrades as before; no added paid call; switch off = `e4baea5`; the after-close recap rule (Step 9F.4 decides); Section 3/4/6 grounding; Section 7 empty when unsupported; the frozen 8-section structure; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`.
- **Supersedes / extends:** narrows Step 8U.10's "the rejected output is discarded whole" to every case except the after-close fault alone (switch on). Extends Step 9F.4c (the partial keep follows its last retry).

## Step 9V — Closed, Weekend and Holiday reports say what has happened since the close (repo: mb-proxy)

- **Date:** 2026-10-22 · **Branch:** step-9v-since-close (from `52552bc`) · **Status:** IMPLEMENTED, tests passing; not committed. The independent proof and one paid replay (Kevin's separate go-ahead) are still to come, then live Closed and Weekend Preview runs.
- **Why:** on the 21 Oct 2026 11:18 SGT Closed run (Tue 20 Oct, 11:18 pm ET), SpaceX (revenue more than doubled, shares up about 4% late) and Netflix (weaker fourth-quarter forecast, shares down about 6%) appeared only in Section 7, with unclear sources. The completed-day prompt sent every after-close item "especially" to Section 7, and the named-source rule existed only in the active-day since-close lead (Step 8P.2(b)). That run's package was not saved, so whether both items were in its after-close list is very likely but unproven.
- **Kevin's decisions (22 Oct 2026), with reasons:**
  1. Each after-close item has one home section: Section 3 for company or sector news (a `broadMarketFocus` entry), otherwise Section 1. When every item lives in Section 3, Section 1 gives one pointer sentence naming the companies, with no figures and no times. Reason: SpaceX and Netflix belong near the top, without repeats.
  2. `SINCE_CLOSE_REPEAT` removes a repeated sentence outside an item's home section (its publish time, or one of its subjects plus a figure from its home section). It never blanks a section. Reason: no fact repeated across Sections 1, 3 and 7.
  3. Section 2 stays closing-only: a Section 2 sentence carrying a since-close time is removed and logged; its references are left alone.
  4. With no after-close news and no "since the close" in Section 1, the program puts the fixed sentence "No new market news has been published since the close on [label]." at the start of Section 1. No other wording.
  5. No cap on items per paragraph; no new number.
  6. After-close news is never presented as the cause of any move: the writer states the fact and the price move as two separate attributed facts. Step 9F.4 decides causes later.
  7. The Yahoo and CNBC recaps are left out of the since-close list (they describe the close itself).
  8. Further Readings unchanged.
  9. One Admin switch `completedSinceCloseEnabled` (env `COMPLETED_SINCE_CLOSE_ENABLED`), default on, next to `sinceCloseLeadEnabled`. Off = `52552bc` in all six states, byte for byte.
  10. This entry, in the same pass.
  11. No paid call in the build.
  - Out of scope: Step 9W (earnings and key news coverage), removing Section 2, a repeat check on live days, Further Readings changes.
- **Definitions:** "the close" is the exchange calendar's regular close of `primaryCompletedSessionDate` (America/New_York): Friday's close on a weekend, the trading day before a holiday. It is the same instant the after-close horizon already uses (tested). "The time of the run" is `reportTimeEt` (from `generatedAt`). Times are ET; the user's display time zone stays display-only.
- **What changed:**
  1. `lib/settings-registry.js`: the switch row.
  2. `lib/us-analysis-package-orchestration.js`: completed days with the switch on add `evidenceContext.sinceCloseDevelopments` = the after-close CNBC news + Yahoo news refs (`subsequentCnbcReferences`, `subsequentYahooNewsReferences`), never the recaps. Nothing new is admitted. Active days or switch off: no field.
  3. `lib/claude-analysis-contract.js` (not in the planned file list; needed because the package passes the contract's exact-key check): the evidence context may carry this one optional last list, only as a subset of `subsequentDevelopments`. Without it, exactly as before.
  4. `lib/analysis-package-service.js` (not in the planned list): when future-dated items are dropped, the optional list is re-mapped like the other role lists.
  5. `lib/us-active-session-evidence.js`: `completedSessionCloseAt` and `completedSinceLastCloseContext` (one builder for CLOSED, WEEKEND and HOLIDAY, same shape as the active `sinceLastCloseContext` plus `mode: 'COMPLETED'`, `refsSource`, `homes`). A package saved before this step has no list: the refs fall back to `subsequentDevelopments` news not in `sessionAssociations`, and `refsSource: 'fallback'` says so.
  6. `lib/claude-model-input-projection.js`: on completed days with the switch on, each listed article gets the same `sinceLastClose: true` and `publishedEt` as on active days, plus `sinceCloseHomeSection` (1 or 3); the request gets a top-level `sinceLastClose` entry with `mode: 'COMPLETED'`, `lastCloseEt`, `reportTimeEt`, refs and home sections.
  7. `lib/claude-analysis-invocation.js`: the sentences shared with the active lead (publish time, state only what is cited, naming the source, not the cause of the earlier move, nothing since the close, the no-news sentence) are named constants with identical text, so the active request is byte-identical. `sinceCloseLeadInstruction` branches on `mode: 'COMPLETED'` to `completedSinceCloseInstruction`. New `completedSinceCloseCleanup` runs right after the date check (rule 31); its events are diagnostics only and never degrade the report or start a retry.
  8. `lib/section-rules.js`: `sinceCloseRepeatCut`, `sinceCloseTimePattern`, `sinceCloseFigures`, `sinceCloseNoNewsSentence`, `mentionsSinceTheClose`. `SINCE_CLOSE_REPEAT` is a cleanup-only sentence trim **outside the numbered registry** (like Step 21D.1's Section 4 cut): adding a rule 32 would have required editing the registry tests that pin rules 1 to 31.
- **New log lines:** `completedSinceCloseContext` `{marketState, refsSource, lastCloseEt, itemCount, sectionOneRefs, sectionThreeRefs}` once per completed writer request; `completedSinceCloseCleanup` `{sectionIndex, violationCategory: SINCE_CLOSE_REPEAT | SINCE_CLOSE_SECTION_TWO | SINCE_CLOSE_NO_NEWS_LINE, action: TRIMMED | KEPT_TO_AVOID_BLANK | ADDED | SKIPPED_WORD_CAP, marketState, removedSentenceCount}`. No existing stage name or field changed.
- **Numbers:** only the switch.
- **Not shared, and why:** the section order and the Section 2 rule (the active lead opens Sections 1 and 2 with since-close news; the completed paragraphs come after the closing analysis and keep Section 2 closing-only: opposite goals, so one branch in the same function); the repeat check and the no-news insertion run on completed days only (Kevin's scope; Step 21G can reuse them for active days); live-price wording (completed days have no live overlay).
- **Tests:** new `tests/completed-since-close.test.js` (24 tests, made-up articles and replies only) and new `tests/fixtures/completed-since-close-switch.js`. With Kevin's approval (22 Oct), 13 existing tests that pin the exact `52552bc` completed-day Section 1 text or writer request run with the switch off, through the same per-name wrapper pattern as the Step 8W and 21D.2 switches (only the wrapper lines at the top of each file changed; no test body): 8 in `claude-analysis-invocation.test.js`, 2 in `section-rules-matrix.test.js`, 1 in `us-analysis-package-orchestration.test.js`, 2 in `us-market-brief-quality-fixtures.test.js`.
- **Build checks (22 Oct 2026, free, scratch harness outside the repo; repository, branch and HEAD confirmed `mb-proxy` / `step-9v-since-close` / `52552bc` before starting; baseline = a file copy of `52552bc`'s `lib`, `api`, `tests`):**
  1. **Full suite:** before this step 1,555 tests, 1,554 pass, 0 fail, 1 to-do. After, at defaults (switch on): 1,580 tests, 1,579 pass, 0 fail, 1 to-do (the known Yahoo header gap from Step 9F.6c); with `COMPLETED_SINCE_CLOSE_ENABLED=false` the same 1,580 / 1,579 / 0 / 1. The 25 new tests: 24 in the new file and the generated on/off row for the new switch. Syntax 63/63 files (`api`, `lib`, `lib/sources`). `git diff --check`: run by Kevin.
  2. **Switch off = `52552bc`:** on all 24 saved package files (CLOSED 29 Sep; WEEKEND-state packages 11, 17 (×2 copies), 17 21:44, 18 23:46 and 18 15:40Z Oct, 27 Sep, 3 Oct; PRE ×9 files (7 packages, 2 copies); REGULAR 15 and 17 Oct (×2 copies each); POST 29 Sep (×2)), the writer request and the cleaned report are byte-identical to the baseline. Packages rebuilt through the real orchestration (stand-in sources, `closed-replay` harness) for CLOSED 29 Sep, 17 Oct and WEEKEND 3 Oct are byte-identical with the switch off.
  3. **Switch on, active states:** all 15 PRE, REGULAR and POST files give a byte-identical writer request and report.
  4. **Switch on, completed states:** the rebuilt 29 Sep package lists `e33` and leaves out the session-associated Yahoo recap `e21`; the rebuilt 17 Oct and 3 Oct packages list nothing (their after-close items are recaps). On the saved packages (all from before this step, so `refsSource: fallback`): 0 to 10 items per package, homes split between Sections 1 and 3, writer request +600 to +3,709 bytes (no article text; the 150 KB article budget is untouched; largest request 143,206 bytes against the 500 KB limit). The two packages with no after-close news get the fixed no-news sentence.
  5. **Planted writer replies** (in the new test file): a fact repeated across Sections 1, 3 and 7 and a since-close time in Section 2 are removed and logged, the report status is unchanged; switch off leaves the same reply as written; the no-news sentence is added only when Section 1 does not already mention the close; a cut that would empty Section 7 is not made and is logged; a cause sentence meets rule 16 the same way with the switch on or off.
  - Scripts copied to `Replay Packages\harness` (new names): `9V-package-check.js` (derived from `closed-replay-9F4d-item3.js`, which is unchanged), `9V-writer-check.js`, `9V-run-all.sh`, `9V-writer-check-summary.txt`.
- **Known limits (stated, not fixed here):**
  - **Company-level after-close causes are not caught by code.** Rule 16 checks market-wide cause claims, so "Netflix shares fell 6% because of …" survives with the switch on or off; decision 6 is a writer instruction only for single companies. A deterministic check would be a separate decision.
  - **After-close Yahoo news that describes the close itself** (for example "Stocks slip as dollar heads for weekly loss", 4:50 pm ET on 16 Oct) counts as since-close news: decision 7 leaves out only the two recap items.
  - **Early-close days:** the exchange calendar marks them unsupported (27 Nov and 24 Dec 2026), so no since-close part is built after them (no paragraph, no fixed sentence).
  - **Fallback on old packages:** a CNBC recap published more than 2 hours after the close has no session association, so on a package saved before this step it could be listed. New packages carry the list and never include recaps.
  - **Not checked:** a reworded repeat with no shared time or figure.
  - **Missing evidence:** no saved package of the 21 Oct Closed run, no HOLIDAY package (fixtures only), no package built after this step with real after-close news.
- **Must not change:** the closing analysis stays first and as it is; Section 2 stays closing-only; an after-close item is never a principal catalyst and never the cause of the earlier completed-session move; the recaps stay out of the since-close list; the cut never blanks a section; the exact no-news sentence; one writer call, no added paid call; PRE, REGULAR and POST byte-identical; switch off = `52552bc`; Further Readings unchanged; Section 3/4/6 grounding; Section 7 empty when unsupported; the frozen 8-section structure; protected checkpoints `39da041`, `14534fc`, `c96a132`, `138af60`, `7cad52f`.
- **Supersedes / extends:** extends Step 8P.2(b) (same flag, labels and shared wording) to Closed, Weekend and Holiday reports. Narrows the completed-day instruction that sent after-close items "especially" to Section 7 (its text is unchanged; the since-close paragraphs instruction follows it). Leaves Step 9F.4's decisions open.
